ACA Processing Center

The ACA Processing Center provides convenient access to the forms required to perform the following functions:

  • ACA Feature Setup & Configuration
  • ACA Data Builds, Rebuilds, and Resets
  • ACA Reporting

To open an ACA Processing Center page:

  • On the Work Centers panel, click ACA Processing and then select the page. You can also open the page by searching for its name.

To get detailed instructions for using the open form:

  • Click [Image: screenshot] (Help) in the header area.

To return to the processing page:

  • Click JUMP TO in the header area of the form.

Common Questions about Forms 1094‑C and 1095‑C

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/1094-1095-Common-Qs.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/1094-1095-Common-Qs.htm

Common Questions about Forms 1094‑C and 1095‑C

These are some frequently asked questions about the IRS forms for Affordable Care Act (ACA) reporting.

What goes in Line 14 (Offer Code) and Line 15 (Employee Share)?

The register build only looks at company-level eligibility and contribution rules when it determines what populates each employee’s line 14 and line 15.

The system then scans through the contribution rules for the employee-only plan types for these benefit plans. Generally, the benefit plan with the lowest employee cost is used as the employee share, and this determines the value for the line 14.

Preference is given to a 1A benefit plan, as well as 1I relief.

Note: Note: If terminated employees receive offer codes, your organization probably did not set your eligibility rules to exclude terminated employees. If you receive 1H when you should not, your organization probably did not set a company-level rule entry for any medical plan.

Known Issue

The system assumes one type of employee-only benefit plan per offering. If you have more than one, register build results may vary. This includes usage of networks where the employee is eligible for more than one network.

Workaround

  1. Set up a “dummy” benefit plan by copying a benefit plan with multiple employee-only plan types (or multiple networks).

  2. Assign a single employee-only plan type (the plan type with the lowest cost available to the employee) to this benefit plan.

  3. Assign this benefit plan to the company. Make sure to enter a start date that matches when the benefit should be considered for register build purposes.

  4. Assign the appropriate eligibility and contribution rules to this benefit plan.

If the above steps do not work, terminate the benefit plan that is causing the issue at the company level, making sure to backdate the termination date to a day before January 1 of the current reporting year.

An employee register was note built. What happened?

There are circumstances where the system might not build a register for an employee when your organization believes that employee should have one.

Form 1095-Cs are only necessary for those employees entitled to an offer of coverage during any part of the reporting year. This includes the following:

  • Employees who have the full-time Employee Type, who were hired on or before September 1, and who are therefore out of their limited non-assessment period before the end of the year.

  • Employees listed in the company’s Offered Employees Maintenance form. Your organization must use this register to alert the system of any variable-hour employee (employees who have the part-time Employee Type), who measured full-time in a previous measurement period. You must enter these employees in the company’s Offered Employees Maintenance and include the start and end dates of their subsequent stability period. The system treats these employees as full-time and entitled to an offer of coverage for the duration of this stability period.

Note: Note: Check your system-level setup to see if your employee type codes are appropriately flagged as full- or part-time.

Why are there blank entries in Line 16?

Once an employee is entitled to an offer of coverage (see the previous question for more information), there are three possible scenarios for Line 16 (Safe Harbor Code):

  • The value 2C, if the employee was enrolled in any medical coverage for the entire month

  • Depending on which safe harbor you select, the values 2F, 2G, or 2H, if the employee share meets that safe harbor standard

  • A blank value, if the system determines that the safe harbor was not satisfied by comparison to the employee share

For the W-2 wages safe harbor, federal regulations provide a very specific method of proration for any employee not offered coverage for the entirety of the year.

For the rate-of-pay safe harbor, federal regulations provide numerous options based on changes in rate-of-pay, whether an employee is salaried or paid hourly, and whether the plan offerings are calendar year.

Known Issues

  • When using the rate-of-pay safe harbor with a non-calendar year plan entry, entering rate-of-pay changes may result in the incorrect rate being used to calculate the safe harbor. The PrismHR Customer Support Team recommends that you review the results for any employee with a rate-of-pay change during the appropriate plan years when using this safe harbor.

  • In some instances, you may get a blank safe harbor entry in the employee’s month of termination. The PrismHR Customer Support Team recommends that you review the register build results for any employee terminated during that year.

Why is a 2D Code in Line 16?

The value 2D may appear on the 1095-C for any full-time employee hired between October 2 of the previous year and September 1 of the current reporting year. The standard limited non-assessment period for full-time employees is three months, plus any first partial month after hire. 2D is the code associated with the limited non-assessment period. The system prioritizes 2D over anything other than 2C. Therefore, a 2D code would be appropriate, and take precedence over anything other than 2C, for the following:

  • Three consecutive months, starting with the month of hire, for any employee hired on the first of the month; and

  • Four consecutive months, starting with the month of hire, for any employee not hired on the first of the month.

Importing Form 1095-C Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/1095-C_Data_Import.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/1095-C_Data_Import.htm

Importing Form 1095-C Data

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

Use this form to adjust employee 1095 registers using data from an imported file. The system supports the following file formats:

  • .txt

  • .csv

If you encounter issues during the import process, click Error Report at the bottom of the form.

To import 1095-C data:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Processes and Import/Export, select 1095C Data Import.

  4. Click Upload File and select the appropriate import file from your system file explorer.

  5. Review the information in the Preview panel to ensure its accuracy. This panel displays one employee per page.

  6. Click Import Data.

Exporting 1095 Registers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/1095_Register_Export.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/1095_Register_Export.htm

Exporting 1095 Registers

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

You can use this export utility to review 1095-C register data, efficiently identify errors, and correct them.

Running the Export

This feature exports Form 1095-C register data as a text (TXT) file that you can then view, edit, and import back into the system. The export file includes the records for employee offer and coverage data (type 2), covered individuals (type 3), and the employee’s employer and home worksite location. The Employer and Home worksite are provided for reference only. You will want to delete these fields before moving forward with the edit and import.

Note: Note: Please note that the report includes only employees and months with Form 1095-C register data. For example, if you run the report at the end of October 2026, it will not include any information for November or December of that year.

To export 1095-C registers:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Processes and Import/Export select 1095 Register Export.

  4. Select the appropriate Report Year.

  5. Enter a Client ID if necessary. If you do not specify a client, the export runs for all clients.

  6. You can apply Export Filters to limit the data returned by the export file. By default, the report includes all register data and all codes.

Specify the months and codes you want to include in the report:

  • In the Months selector, specify any months you want to specifically include. The export will exclude data from other months.

  • Use the Offer Codes and Safe Harbor Codes selectors to specify filters for these codes. The report will include only the registers that have those codes.

  1. In the Include Employees with Share Not Less Than field, enter the maximum number of shares needed to exclude an employee from the report. Leave this field blank to include all share amounts.

  2. In the Include Employees with Share Not Greater Than field, enter the minimum number of shares needed to exclude an employee from the report. Leave this field blank to include all share amounts.

  3. Click Build.

  4. After the file builds, click Download 1095 Registers.

Editing the File

You can open the file with a text editor or a spreadsheet program such as Microsoft™ Excel®. Make sure to save it as a text file so you can import any changes back into the system.

There are some important things to note about making edits:

Decimal Places in Employee Share

Note: Note: If a column in a text file is not formatted correctly, decimals can be lost during the import process.

The import process requires 2 decimal places for any Employee Share value. If the decimal places are missing, the import function inserts them.

For example, if the export runs and an employee share is 100.00, but the spreadsheet program changes this to read 100 and saves the value without the decimal places, the system will import it as 1.00. Therefore, you must ensure that values in the text file include the two decimal places.

To prevent decimal place errors for any Type 2 entry (employees):

  • Before you save and import, make sure the seventh column (G, in Excel) only contains numbers with two decimal places.

  • For any Type 3 entry (covered individual), do not format the data as a number with two decimal places: this data represents a birth date.

  • You can sort the spreadsheet so Type 2 entries are at the top and Type 3 entries are at the bottom, and then select all the cells in that column that apply to Type 2 entries.

Considerations for Covered Individuals

When importing the file, covered individuals are identified by Social Security number first, and then by date of birth if no Social Security number is present.

  • If you find that a covered individual’s name is incorrect, you can edit it in this file.

  • If the Social Security number or date of birth is incorrect or missing, do not try to edit these values in the export file. Instead of updating this record with an import (which would create a new covered individual record), you must use the Edit Employee 1095 form to change this information directly (See Editing Form 1095-C Information).

About 1095 and 1094 Registers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/About_1095_1094_Registers.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/About_1095_1094_Registers.htm

About 1095 and 1094 Registers

The system stores ACA-related data in the employee 1095-C registers and the client 1094-C registers. Forms 1094-C and 1095-C rely on the data accumulated in these registers over the course of the reporting year. If you want to generate the IRS forms, you must first build these registers. The system does not create them automatically.

About 1094-C Registers

Note: Note: See here for a copy of Form 1094-C.

The system will pre-populate most data while building these client registers. We highly recommend checking to ensure the accuracy of this data. Remember that this register includes some auto-populated data and some fields that require manual entry.

About 1095-C Registers

Note: Note: See here for a copy of Form 1095-C.

Employee 1095-C registers contain data for lines 14 through 16 of Form 1095-C, for each employee. They also include Part 3/covered individual information, when applicable.

You can build employee 1095-C registers in one of three ways, or using a combination of all three:

  • Register builds

  • Data import

  • Manual data entry

The Data Build Process

The register build feature clears all prior register data. The import and manual entry processes overwrite the specific data, respective to the row being adjusted (or write it, if no entries exist).

  • The data build process is controlled by the ACADATABUILD custom feature code.

  • The next time a user logs into the system after this code is enabled, data begins building for the year-to-date for any large employers.

  • When a user logs in after January 1, the data build completes for the prior year (the reporting year).

The system builds data in the background over the course of the year for clients indicated as ACA large employers, . The system routinely checks if the data is up to date for all prior months in the calendar year. If not, it compiles the data for any missing months.

Manually Re-triggering Data Builds

Note: Note: If you need to re-trigger for 1000 or more employees, please contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team for assistance.

Users can manually re-trigger the data build at any time.

Once re-triggered, the system no longer builds the ACA data for the months that were built with the background process prior. For example, if you manually re-trigger the process in December after using the background process to build the data for January through November, the system will not automatically build the data in the background for January–November, (unless you contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team for assistance). The background process will continue to automatically build the ACA data for future months.

When the data for the final calendar month finishes building, the system uses the aggregated information to make any final adjustments to the data. This is because certain codes, like 1G, cannot be assigned unless data is known for the full year (in the case of code 1G, the system needs to “know” whether the employee was ever not full-time at any point during the year, but was enrolled in self-insured coverage).

Notes on Building 1095-C Registers

Note: Note: The register build process relies almost entirely on ACA data. This means the system must build the data before the register build can work properly. If you have a partial year of data, the system only builds a partial register.

If you previously built your registers based on an incomplete year of data, you can rebuild the registers again once the complete ACA data is recorded. You do not have to perform a rebuild in this case.

If you alter your ACA or benefit plan setup and want to trigger different register build results, you must first rebuild the data.

Form Generation

The system only creates Forms 1095-C for:

  • Employees outside of their 2D limited non-assessment periods, who had an employee type of “full-time” during any part of the year.

  • Employees on the ACA Offered Employees Register (See Setting Up ACA Offered Employees) who were eligible for coverage at some point during the year, based on the standard client-level benefit rules as well as ACA benefit eligibility rules.

  • Employees enrolled in self-insured coverage at any point in the year.

The system does not create Forms 1095-C for:

  • Employees flagged as a sub-S-corp principal

  • Business owners

  • 1099 contractors

The system does not automatically determine if an employee flagged as part-time should be measured as full-time. You must use the ACA eligibility reports and add any part-time employees who should be counted as full-time to Offered Employees Maintenance.

Setting Up ACA Offered Employees

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/ACA-Offered-Employees.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/ACA-Offered-Employees.htm

Setting Up ACA Offered Employees

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Use the ACA Offered Employees form, also known as the ACA Offered Employees Register, to specify part-time employees who need to be reported as full-time employees for ACA purposes.

Overview

Once you have determined the eligibility of employees under a certain client based on the client-level benefits eligibility rules, you can specify stability periods for those employees.

The system uses stability periods to determine whether to generate Form 1095-C for that employee. In the given calendar year, if an employee has at least one month of stability, they will receive a Form 1095-C. The system will populate the appropriate 1095-C offer code for the month (or months) within the stability period.

In short, based on the information entered on this form, any full-time employees who have a stability period of at least one month in the current calendar year will receive a Form 1095-C.

Procedure

To configure the offered employees register for ACA reporting purposes:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Setup and Client Setup, select ACA Offered Employees. The form displays.

  4. Set the following parameters.

Field Description
Filters – These optional filters may be used together or separately.
Stability Period Includes Enter a date to filter the grid based on whether this date falls between employee start and end dates (inclusive). If the filter date falls before or after an employee’s stability period, that employee does not display. Delete the value in this field to display all employees regardless of stability period.
Offer of Coverage Select Yes or No to filter the grid based on the value in the ACA Offer of Coverage column: - Yes displays only rows where the ACA Offer of Coverage = Y. - No displays only rows where the ACA Offer of Coverage = N or empty.
  • Yes displays only rows where the ACA Offer of Coverage = Y.
  • No displays only rows where the ACA Offer of Coverage = N or empty. | Grid Columns | | | Employee ID | Enter or select the employee ID. | | Employee Name | Employee’s full name (populates automatically). | | Stability Start Date | Stability period start date. | | Stability End Date | Stability period end date. This populates automatically when you select a start date. This value is based on the predefined stability periods for the relevant employee type: - First, the system checks for client-level Period Setup information, located in the Client ACA Processing Parameters. (See Configuring Client ACA Parameters). - If the client has no Period Setup information, the system uses the setup information from system-level ACA parameters. (See Configuring Global ACA Parameters). |
  • First, the system checks for client-level Period Setup information, located in the Client ACA Processing Parameters. (See Configuring Client ACA Parameters).
  • If the client has no Period Setup information, the system uses the setup information from system-level ACA parameters. (See Configuring Global ACA Parameters). | Current Employee Status | Status code associated with the employee. For example, “A” for Active or “T” for Terminated. This value populates automatically. | | Type | Employee type. You can select On-Going (default), New Hire, or Null. | | ACA Offer of Coverage | Whether the employee has had an offer of coverage (Y or N). This read-only field populates automatically. Use the Offer of Coverage field to filter the grid based on this column. |
  1. Click Save.

Importing ACA Offered Employees Data

You can access the ACA Offered Employees import template in the Data Import Tool from this form to import ACA offered employees data by clicking Import

[Image: Import icon]

. The ACA Offered Employees import template option and its tabs/fields automatically display.

Note: Note: You can also navigate directly to the Data Import Tool by opening the Back Office menu, then selecting Data Import Tool from the Operations|Import Processing menu. In the Data Import Tool form, select the ACA Offered Employees import template option.

Note: Note the following when importing ACA Offered Employees data:

  • Users whose user role is set to DENY for the ACA Offered Employees form cannot import data using the ACA Offered Employees import template and subsequently, the Data Import Tool icon will not display on this form.

  • Users whose user role is set to FULL or INQUIRY for the ACA Offered Employees form, but whose user role is also set to DENY for the Data Import Tool cannot view the Import icon on the ACA Offered Employees form.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the ACA Offered Employees form can view the ACA Offered Employees import template for Miscellaneous Invoice, but cannot import data.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the Data Import Tool can view the form and import template options, but cannot import data.

  • Users who do not have access to a particular company through client access groups are unable to import data into that company.

  • A warning displays if you import an employee who is not a part-time employee, but you can still import that employee.

  • You can import duplicate part-time employees if they do not have the same dates. For example, if employee ZXY uses the date 1/1/2023, you cannot import that employee using the date 1/1/2023, but you can import employee ZXY using the date 5/1/2023.

Creating the ACA Third Party Download/1095 Export

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/ACA-Third-Party-Download.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/ACA-Third-Party-Download.htm

Creating the ACA Third Party Download/1095 Export

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Use the Third Party 1095 Export form to download 1094-C and 1095-C data, which you can then export for processing by third-parties.

This export is supported by the following third-party processors:

  • CIC

  • BDS

  • Print Management Partners

State Records

The ACA Third-Party Download indicates whether to include New Jersey and Washington, D.C. residents on the ACA Transmission Files of those states. It generates a STATEREC record for each employee who resided in NJ or DC at some point during the year. It appends these records to the end of the file. For NJ residents, additional steps are required (see the note below).

This download also creates STATEREC records for employees in Rhode Island and California.

  • New Jersey - If the Third-Party Download indicates that employees resided in NJ in the prior year, you can use the new NJ State Submission setting on the ACA Transmission File form to report only those residents, as specified by state requirements. There is no DC equivalent to this setting, as DC reporting requirements are different and do not require a filtered submission like New Jersey does. Please see See Creating the ACA Transmission File

  • Rhode Island - The system generates a STATEREC record if the employee address was located in Rhode Island at any point in the given year (same logic as New Jersey).

  • California - The system generates a STATEREC record if the employee had one or more covered individuals on their Form 1095-C and the employee address was located in California at any point in the given year.

Procedure

Note: Note: If needed, you can include the three Jurat signature fields in the download file. These fields are optional, so you only need to supply this information if the third-party will be producing E-files.

Note: Note: An error message displays for each client on the download if the MEC indicator is not enabled for any month. This provides visibility to potential errors that could result in penalties upon submission to the Internal Revenue Service.

To generate the ACA Third-Party Download:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Processes and Import/Export, select Third Party 1095 Export.

  4. Select the Tax Year.

  5. If you want to include specific clients, select the appropriate Client ID. The Client Name displays. If you leave this grid blank, the download will include all clients.

  6. Select whether you want to Include ALE Members on the file. If selected, the system will create ALE Group records on the download file.

  7. If your organization offers early electronic Form 1095-C access to your employees, you can use Exclude Electronic 1095C to mark the records of employees who do not want a printed copy of the form. This indicates to the third party that they should not print the forms, but allows them to include the employee information in a data transmission to the IRS. The default behavior is to include all employees.

  8. In the Use SSN OR EEID as Employee ID field, indicate whether to use the employee ID (default) or Social Security Number as their ID on the export file.

  9. Enter an optional Employee ID Prefix, such as a database account prefix.

  10. If appropriate, enter the Jurat Signature Pin, Signature Title, and Signature Date. See the note above for details.

  11. When you are ready to create the file, click Build. When the build completes, the system sends you an email notification.

ACA 1095‑C Data Import Specifications

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/ACA_1095-C_Data_Import_Specifications.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/ACA_1095-C_Data_Import_Specifications.htm

ACA 1095‑C Data Import Specifications

Employee Form 1095-C data must meet the following specifications before it is imported into the system.

File Format

The file should be in ASCII text file (.txt) with tab delimiters. For example, you can use an Excel spreadsheet to enter all of the data as specified below, then save it as a tab-delimited .txt file.

Record Types

The 1095 Data import file contains two record types:

  • Employee Offer and Coverage (2) – Transaction record to submit employee offer and coverage data included in Part II of the form.

  • Covered Individuals (3) – Transaction record to submit data on covered individuals included in Part III of the form.

Field Type in the Import File

  • Alphanumeric – any printable character

  • Amount – any digit from 0 through 9 and an embedded decimal, for example, 123.98

  • Numeric – any digit from 0 through 9

  • Date – date field in either MM-DD-YYYY or MM/DD/YYYY format

Covered Name Formatting

Covered (dependent) names cannot include any special characters, such as commas, periods, apostrophes, or quotations marks. It should be in a format such as:

FirstName LastName

FirstName MiddleInitial LastName

FirstName MiddleName LastName Suffix

For example:

Jason Argonis

Jason A Argonis

Jason Aristos Argonis Jr

Notice that there are no periods after abbreviations, nor commas before the suffix.

File Order

The records in the file can be submitted in any order.

File Name

We recommend that you use a unique name for each file, for example, CL100_1095C.txt.

Record Layouts

Employee Offer and Coverage Data (2)

Field Position Type Comments
Record Type 1 Numeric The value must be 2.
Company 2 Alphanumeric Must be a defined Company (Client) ID in the system.
Social Security Number 3 Alphanumeric Must be the social security number of a defined employee in the system, in the format 999-99-9999 or 999999999.
Tax Year 4 Numeric YYYY
Tax Month 5 Numeric MM
Offer Code 6 Alphanumeric Must be a valid offer code: 1A, 1B, 1C, 1D, 1E, 1F, 1G, 1J, 1K, 1L, 1M, 1N, 1O, 1P, 1Q, 1R, or 1S.
Employee Share 7 Amount The employee’s share of lowest cost monthly premium.
Safe Harbor Code 8 Alphanumeric Must be a valid Section 4980H Safe Harbor Code, for example, 2B.
Type Class 9 Alpha Optional; valid values are F for full time, P for part time, or I for inactive
Employee ID 10 Alphanumeric Optional; the employee’s system ID.
Employee Name 11 Alpha The employee’s name. Informational only. > Note: Note: If entering values in positions 12 and 13, be sure to also enter a value or an additional tab in this position.
Work ZIP 12 Numeric Optional; Employee’s work ZIP code. Must be five digits or blank. Used to determine HRA offer codes (1L through 1S).
Resident ZIP 13 Numeric Optional; Employee’s residential ZIP code. Must be five digits or blank. Used to determine HRA offer codes (1L through 1S).

Covered Individuals (3)

Field Position Type Comments
Record Type 1 Numeric The value must be 3.
Company 2 Alphanumeric This must be a defined Company (Client) ID in the system.
Soc-Sec-Num 3 Alphanumeric This must be the social security number of a defined employee in the system, in the format 999-99-9999 or 999999999.
Year 4 Numeric YYYY
Covered Name 5 Alphanumeric Enter the name of the covered dependent, without any special characters. It cannot include commas, periods, apostrophes, quotation marks, and so on. See below for examples. See above for more details.
Covered SSN 6 Alphanumeric Enter the social security number of the covered dependent, in the format 999-99-9999 or 999999999.
Covered DOB 7 Date Enter the date of birth of the covered dependent, in the format MM-DD-YYYY or MM/DD/YYYY.
Covered Year 8 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered for all twelve months of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered JAN 9 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in January of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered FEB 10 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in February of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered MAR 10 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in March of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered APR 12 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in April of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered MAY 13 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in May of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered JUN 14 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in June of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered JUL 15 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in July of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered AUG 16 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in August of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered SEP 17 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in September of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered OCT 18 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in October of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered NOV 19 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in November of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.
Covered DEC 20 Numeric Enter 1 if the dependent was covered in December of the reported year, otherwise enter 0.

Building ACA 1095 Registers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Build%20ACA1095%20Registers.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Build ACA1095 Registers.htm

Building ACA 1095 Registers

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Use this form to build Form 1095-C data registers for ACA reporting. To avoid building incomplete data, PrismHR generally recommends running the register build after the end of the reporting year.

Whenever your organization runs the build process, the system will overwrite any existing ACA 1095 register records. It will also update the counts in the client 1094-C register for the selected reporting year, because those counts are derived from the employee 1095-C registers.

Note: Note: Before building registers, we also recommend running the ACA Data Status Report to verify that the ACA data is complete. See Running the ACA Data Status Report.

To build ACA 1095-C registers:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Processes, select Build ACA 1095 Registers.

  4. Select the reporting Year for which you need to build registers.

  5. Enter or select a Client ID if you want to build registers for only that client, or leave the field blank to process all applicable clients.

  6. Click Create 1095 Data. The system will send you an email notification when the process completes.

[Image: PrismHR system email notification, stating that an initialized build of the ACA 1095 registers has completed.]

Deleting ACA Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Delete%20ACA%20Data.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Delete ACA Data.htm

Deleting ACA Data

WARNING: You cannot retrieve ACA data after running this process on it.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

Use the Delete ACA Data form to permanently delete ACA data for one or more clients, for a particular reporting year.

To permanently delete the system’s existing ACA data:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Processes, select Delete ACA Data.

  4. Select the appropriate Year.

  5. If you want to delete data for a specific client, select its Client ID. Otherwise, the system will delete ACA data for all clients.

  6. Click Delete.

Notes on Form 1094‑C and Form 1095‑C

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Notes_On_1094c_1095c.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Notes_On_1094c_1095c.htm

Notes on Form 1094‑C and Form 1095‑C

Note: Note the following about the way the system handles the information and builds the registers for these forms.

Employee Data in the Form 1094-C Register Build

The Full-Time Employee Count includes any employee who was active on the first of a month that the system determined to be ACA full time for the month. It determines this at the time of the register build or the import.

Employees in the Form 1095-C Register Build

Employees’ employment status determines whether they are included in the Form 1095-C register build.

Full-Time Employees

If an employee is flagged as full-time, the system considers them eligible on the first of the month that is three months after the hire date. If, under company eligibility rules, the employee was not offered benefits on or before that date, Line 16 is blank for those months.

Variable-Hour Employees

If an employee qualifies for coverage under the client’s benefits eligibility rules, the system populates the correct code on the Form 1095-C. This is based on the entries in Offered Employees Maintenance form. See Setting Up ACA Offered Employees.

COBRA Enrollees

The register build handles employees who were offered or enrolled in COBRA during the current year. Employees who were covered by COBRA but were not employed at any point during the year are correctly excluded from the register.

W-2 Wages for Affordability Testing Calculations

The system factors the number of months that an employee was offered coverage into the Safe Harbor proration.

When the system uses the employees’ W-2 wages for affordability testing, it adjusts the amounts used in the calculation by performing these steps:

  1. Get the year-to-date W-2 wages.

  2. Get the number of months in the year when coverage was offered for any day in the month.

  3. Get the number of months in the year when the employee was active on any day in the month.

  4. Divide the number of months coverage was offered by the number of months employed.

  5. Multiply the result by the W-2 wages.

  6. Multiply that result by 9.5 percent (0.095).

  7. If the lowest-cost contribution is less than or equal to the final result, then the plan is affordable.

========================================================================

More Notes About Offer Code Calculation

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Offer_SafeHarbor_Code_Calc.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Offer_SafeHarbor_Code_Calc.htm

More Notes About Offer Code Calculation

The system calculates the offer codes based on information entered in the various forms that pertain to the ACA.

A few notes about this calculation logic:

  • The system replaces 1E with 1A when the employee share is less than the federal poverty line.

  • If an employee has the 1G offer code in a month, the employee’s share and safe harbor codes (Lines 15 and 16) are empty.

  • Code 1J indicates that the employee was offered a medical plan that covers the employee and conditionally covers a spouse.

  • Code 1K indicates that the employee was offered a medical plan that covers the employee and other dependents and conditionally covers a spouse.

  • Codes 1I and 2I were valid for reporting year 2015. They are not valid codes for any year after that.

  • Codes 1L through 1S were introduced in 2020. They correspond to individual coverage health reimbursement accounts (HRAs) offered to employees, non-employees, spouses, and dependents. Some codes also determine whether the employee’s worksite ZIP code or their residential ZIP code displays on line 17, which was also introduced on the 2020 version of the form.

Rebuilding ACA Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Rebuild%20ACA%20Data.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/Rebuild ACA Data.htm

Rebuilding ACA Data

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

Use the Rebuild ACA Data form to rebuild employee ACA data for one or more clients within a single reporting year. You can also use it to build data for a client that was not set up for ACA reporting in time to have its data built automatically over the course of the year.

Note: Note: This process overwrites any existing employee ACA data in the holding file. It can take a long time to run, so you should run this process during off-peak hours, if possible.

The system may look like it is frozen while it rebuilds the ACA data. Please be patient: a message will display when the process completes.

To rebuild ACA Form 1095-C data:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Processes, select Rebuild ACA Data.

  4. Select the Report Year.

  5. Enter one or more client IDs in the Client table. To rebuild data for all clients, leave this table blank. Building for all clients will take a long time.

  6. Click Rebuild ACA Data. The system will send you an email when the rebuild completes.

  7. Once the process completes, build the 1095-C registers for the client: see Building ACA 1095 Registers.

[Image: Notification email from the PrismHR system stating that the ACA Rebuild Data is complete.]

ACA Penalty Avoidance Recommendations

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/TS-ACA_Penalty_Avoidance.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Data-Build/TS-ACA_Penalty_Avoidance.htm

ACA Penalty Avoidance Recommendations

Before submitting Forms 1094‑C and 1095‑C to the IRS and your employees, PrismHR strongly recommends you review the contents of these forms for common errors resulting in penalties.

The IRS leverages three types of penalties for (real, or perceived) employer mandate violations. These penalties are:

  • Failure to file/furnish: up to $270 (or more in the case of intentional disregard) per Form 1095‑C with incorrect data or if the form is submitted late to the IRS/employees

  • 4980H(a): $2,320 per every full-time employee for any month the 95% minimum essential coverage (MEC) standard is not met, if a single employee receives a premium tax credit

  • 4980H(b): $3,480 per individual employee who was not offered MEC

The most common errors that result in penalty assessment notices are usually self‑evident reporting errors on Forms 1094‑C and 1095‑C. Two specific examples include:

  • Form 1094‑C submissions with the MEC Indicator for one or more months set to No

  • Form 1095‑C submissions with blank Box 16Safe Harbor code entries

WARNING: In order to provide the most accurate reporting results possible, PrismHR uses configuration and data‑driven logic when it sets the MEC Indicator and Safe Harbor codes. This leaves open the possibility that the system might set the MEC Indicator to No and leave Box 16 for Safe Harbor codes blank. Therefore, we highly recommend reviewing MEC indicators and safe harbor codes before submission.

Recommended Review Processes

PrismHR recommends using the following processes to avoid potential issues when submitting Forms 1094‑C and 1095‑C to the IRS.

MEC Indicator

Use the Part 3 of the Edit 1094 Form screen (See Editing Form 1094-C Information) to adjust the MEC Indicator for all ALE clients.

The values for any month and client can be edited. If a value for any month is incorrectly set to “No”, set it to “Yes.”

Safe Harbor Codes

Use the Form 1095-C Register Export utility (See Exporting 1095 Registers) to review system-generated safe harbor codes on this form.

The default download configuration produces all Form 1095‑C data for all clients and employees in our import format. You can scan the output to identify any missing safe harbor codes.

[Image: Section of the 1095-C register export file. In the safe harbor codes column, blank spaces are highlighted indicating missing codes.]

To add or edit any codes in the export file:

  1. Edit the export file.

  2. Save the file on your local machine.

  3. Import the updated file back into the system (See Importing Form 1095-C Data).

You can also adjust Form 1095‑C data per employee directly (See Editing Form 1095-C Information).

ACA 1094 Count Report

In PrismHR, use the ACA 1094 Count Report (found in theReports menu under HR > Report) to review MEC Indicator values per employee for any month of the year.

[Image: Example spreadsheet output for 1094 Count Report. Employee IDs, names, and employers are listed along with the total, full-time, and MEC counts for a month.]

MEC must be offered to at least 95% of ALE Member’s full-time employees and their dependents. The report tests if employees are included in the count by using the total full‑time count for any month as the denominator, and the total MEC count for any month as the numerator.

On this report, review any employee with 1 for full‑time count (FT) and 0 for MEC, as this indicates they should have been offered MEC, but were not. This is any employee out of a 2D limited non‑assessment period that either:

  • was a full‑time employee for the month, or

  • was identified as being ACA Full Time for that month on the ACA Offered Employees register.

Common Sources of Coding Issues

Coding issues are a common source of configuration/data errors within PrismHR. The most common of these errors are related to incomplete medical plan setup, including:

  • System‑level ACA configuration in Group Benefit Plans (in the Back Office menu under System|Change)

  • Company‑level eligibility rules in Benefit Plans (found in the Client menu under Client|Change)

Note: ACA data and Form 1095‑C registers must be rebuilt in order for configuration changes to take effect.

To rebuild ACA data and Form 1095‑C registers, see:

  • Rebuilding ACA Data

  • Building ACA 1095 Registers

Creating the 1094-C Report Download

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/1094C_Report_Download.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/1094C_Report_Download.htm

Creating the 1094-C Report Download

The Form 1094-C Report creates a PDF version of Form 1094-C and populates it with information from the current client, for the given year.

To run this report:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Reporting, select 1094C Report Download.

  4. Enter the Year for the 1094-C report.

  5. By selecting the Generate All Clients option, you will only receive a PDF for all the clients you have access to. Now, you do not have to print client by client the ACA Form 1094-Cs.

  6. Once you click Run, a Job Control window displays the progress and the number of selected clients.

In case of any errors, redo the process. The system downloads a zip file to your computer, containing a Form 1094-C PDF for each client to which you have access.

Creating the 1095-C Report Download

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/1095C_Report_Download.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/1095C_Report_Download.htm

Creating the 1095-C Report Download

Use the 1095-C Report Download form to generate a PDF of Form 1095-C for employees within a specific client or for employees for all clients that you can access.

Note: Note: Client Access Group security is enabled on this form.

To access this form, on the Work Centers under Compliance, click ACA Processing. Under ACA Reporting, click 1095-C Report Download.

To download the ACA 1095-C Report:

  1. Complete the following fields:

Field Name — Description

Tax Year — Select the appropriate tax year.

From Employee ID — This field is optional. If you want to run this report for a set of employees, enter the first Employee ID here.

To Employee ID — This field is optional. If you want to run this report for a set of employees, enter the last Employee ID here.

Mask SSN — This field is optional. Select this option if you want to mask the Social Security number of the employees in the output.

  1. Click Run to generate the report for the selected tax year.

  • You can generate Form 1095-C PDFs for a maximum of 50,000 employees for a client.

  • When the job completes, you receive a confirmation email. You need to use the Transfer Downloads File to PC form to download the zip file.

  • The zip file will contain the PDF files in the format COMPANYID.YEAR.EMPLOYEEID.f1095c.pdf.

  • The zip file will be downloaded to your system from the main form. Depending on the number of employee records, this can take a while. However, you can continue with your work while this process runs in the background. For example, it can take about two hours to process records for 40,000 employees.

Running the ACA Data Status Report

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

Note: Note: Run this report on or after the new year to verify that data has been built for all employees.

Before you build the 1095-C registers, you can use this report to verify that employee ACA data is complete. First, the system selects all clients set up as ACA large employers (ALEs) for that year. Then, it checks employee ACA data to ensure that data exists for all twelve months of the reporting year.

If data for the specified year is not complete, the report will display all clients as incomplete.

To run this report:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Reporting, select ACA Data Status Report.

  4. Select the appropriate reporting Year.

  5. Click Build ACA Data Report.

Running the ACA 1094 Count Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_1094_Count_Report.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_1094_Count_Report.htm

Running the ACA 1094 Count Report

Note: Note: This form supports entity-level security for Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors, as defined on the Users > Actions > Data Security form.

Use the ACA 1094 Count Report to view a complete list of the employees that make up the Total and Full‑Time Counts per month. In the system, this data is located on Part 3 of Form 1094‑C (See Editing Form 1094-C Information). This report indicates whether or not employees were included in the count and how they influence the Minimum Essential Coverage Offer for a year.

PrismHR populates the Minimum Essential Coverage Indicator, Full‑Time Employee Count, and Total Employee Count fields of the Form 1094‑C when building Form 1095‑C registers, or when you click Update Counts on the Edit Form 1094‑C form. This data displays on the Form 1094‑C third‑party download, eFile, and PDF.

[Image: Detail view of Part III of Form 1094-C, showing the Minimum Essential coverage indicator box for a few months, along with other ALE Member details.]

Procedure

To run this report:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Reporting, select ACA 1094 Count Report.

Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors can access this report through Reports > HR | Report > ACA 1094 Count Report.

  1. Enter the report Year.

  2. Enable Include Employer ID? to list employer IDs on the report.

  3. Click Run. The ACA 1094 Count Report window displays.

[Image: ACA 1094 Count Report progress dialog, showing steps in the report build process.]

  1. Select Close to download the report. The report output file opens in Excel.

[Image: Example spreadsheet output for 1094 Count Report. Employee IDs, names, and employers are listed along with the total, full-time, and MEC counts for a month.]

Report Columns

Refer to the table below for a description of the report columns.

Field Name — Description

Employee ID — The employee ID number in PrismHR.

Employee Name — The employee name in PrismHR.

Employer ID — The ID of the employer that the employee was hired under. You must enable the Include Employer ID? field to view this column.

(Month) Total — Indicates whether or not the employee was included in the total count, including full‑time and non‑full‑time employees, and employees in a Limited Non‑Assessment Period, for each calendar month. 1 = Yes, 0 = No

(Month) Full‑Time — Indicates whether or not the employee was included in the total count for full‑time employees, including employees in a Limited Non‑Assessment Period, for each calendar month. 1 = Yes, 0 = No

(Month) MEC — Indicates whether or not the employee was included in the minimum essential coverage (MEC) offer, for each calendar month. 1 = Yes, 0 = No

Running the ACA 9.5% Rule Test Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_9point5_Percent_Report.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_9point5_Percent_Report.htm

Running the ACA 9.5% Rule Test Report

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

This report displays the Total Annualized Pay and Form W-2 Box 1 calculations for currently elected employee health plans. The results of this report can help employers decide which type of wages they will use to determine plan affordability.

The ACA advises employees to not pay more than 9.5% of their household income to an employer in exchange for a single portion of the lowest cost health care plan available to that employee. If employers need to use alternative methods for calculating this value, ACA regulations specify three safe harbor options they can use instead of the annual household income:

  • Form W-2, Box 1 wages

  • Total Annualized Pay

  • Federal Poverty Level (FPL)

Note: Note: During the annualizing process, the system bases employee rate of pay on the last day of the report period.

Procedure

To run this report:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Reporting, select ACA 9.5% Rule Test Report.

Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors can access this report through Reports > HR | Report > ACA 9.5% Rule Test Report.

  1. Enter the Client ID or IDs for each client you want to include in the report. To include all clients in the report, leave these fields blank.

  2. Enter a report Report Start Date and Report End Date – no more than twelve months.

  3. If you need to include the information for only large employers on the report, select Yes in the Large Employer? field and then enter the Large Employer Year.

  4. To exclude employees who do not have coverage, select the Exclude Employees Without Coverage? option.

  5. Click Generate Report.

Report

The report output includes the following columns:

  • Company ID and Name

  • Employee ID and Name

  • Status and Status Class

  • Type and Type Class

  • Hire Date

  • Health Coverage (Y/N)

  • Waived Coverage (Y/N)

  • Monthly Single Coverage

  • Employee Contribution

  • 9.5% of Fed Taxable Income

  • 9.5% of Employee Annualized Pay

  • Exceeds 9.5% of Fed Taxable

  • Exceeds 9.5% of Annualized Pay

Running the ACA Eligibility Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_Eligibility_Rpt.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_Eligibility_Rpt.htm

Running the ACA Eligibility Report

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Note: Note: This form supports entity-level security for Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors, as defined on the Users > Actions > Data Security form.

The Eligibility Reports indicate employee eligibility for health care coverage under the ACA.

Eligibility is based on employee hours worked over a certain measurement period. You can establish this period at the global level and override it at the client level, if necessary. For more information about these setup parameters, see the topics on global and client-level period setup:

  • Configuring Global ACA Parameters

  • Configuring Client ACA Parameters

You can run the Eligibility Report for either current employees or new hires. The period information comes from the Period Setup section of the Client ACA Parameters. If that information is not defined, the Period Setup values come from the system-level ACA parameters.

The following example of the global Periods panel includes sections for new employees and ongoing employees. These correspond to the two types of ACA eligibility report you can run.

[Image: Eligibility period controls on the Client ACA Parameters form, for initial/new employees and standard/ongoing employees. You can set the measurement, administration, and stability periods by the number of months or days.]

Before You Begin

You can specify certain leave status codes to include on the report, even if the report would not normally include those employees: use the Leave Status Codes Overrides for ACA Coverage Report form to do this. See Including Leave Status Codes on the Eligibility Report for details.

About the Start and End Date Fields

When setting up this report, you must enter a Start Date and End Date. The system uses this date range to select any employees who had a measurement period during this time. These dates can be up to one (non-calendar) year apart.

The report logic is different based on whether you run the report for new hires or for ongoing employees.

New Hire Report

The new hire report selects employees using the report start and end dates, in conjunction with the New Hire Measurement Period in the global or client ACA settings.

For example, if you run the report for new hires with a start date of 9/15/2026 and an end date of 10/15/2026, and the New Hire Measurement Period is 9 months, then for all clients on the report the system will include employees who were hired between 12/16 of the previous year and 1/16/2026 (notice that this range is 9 months before the entered dates).

Note: Note the following:

  • This measurement start date is specific to this report. Each employee has a different measurement start based on their effective hire date.

  • To select vouchers, the system uses the date that comes 31 days after the End Date.

  • There is nothing in the client or global Period settings that affects the validity of the start and end dates you enter for this report.

Ongoing Employee Report

For the ongoing report, the system uses the year passed in the report start and end date fields. The month and day must be within the range from the Start Month/Day specified for the ongoing period.

For example, if you enter 1/1/2026 and the start for the period is January 1, then the measurement start date for the report will be 1/1/2026. If the Ongoing Measurement Period is 6 Months, then the measurement end date will be 6/1/2026.

  • Like the new hire report, this report selects vouchers based on the date that comes 31 days after the specified End Date.

  • The Start Date must be before, or the same as, the Measurement End Date.

  • The End Date must be the same as, or after, the Measurement End Date.

  • If these dates are not correct, the report will not be generated for the client.

Procedure

To run this report:

  1. Go to the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. From the ACA Reporting column, select ACA Eligibility Report.

Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors can access this report through Reports > HR | Report > ACA Eligibility Report.

  1. Do one of the following:

  • To enter individual clients, enter each client that you want to include in the report in the Client ID field.

  • To include all clients in the report, leave the Client ID field blank. (Later, when you click Generate Report, a confirmation message displays asking you to confirm whether you want to run the report for all clients. Select Yes to continue.)

  1. Enter the Report Start Date and Report End Date. The date range cannot be greater than a year.

The employees included in the report are those who have a measurement period that ends between these dates. See the previous section for details about this logic.

  1. Select Large Employer to include only large employers in the report. Otherwise, the report includes all clients.

Note: Note: If you select the Large Employer field, you must also enter the Large Employer Year. If a client was not a large employer in that year, the report does not include it.

  1. In the Include in Report panel, select one of the following options:

  • All Employee Types and Classes – this is the default option.

  • Employee Type – in the look-up field that displays, select any employee types you want to include on the report.

  • Employee Type Classification – in the field that displays, select whether you want to include Full-Time, Part-Time, or all employee type classes.

  1. In the Report Type field, select whether you want to run the New Hire or Ongoing employee report. The default is Ongoing.

See the previous and next sections for more details about the differences between these two reports.

  1. Click Generate Report to run the report.

  2. After the report generates, click Download Summary Report to obtain the report download.

Interpreting the Eligibility Report Output

Use this section to better understand the output of the eligibility report.

“Number of Weeks” and Week End Dates

  • Weeks run from Sunday through Saturday, except the first and last weeks in the measurement period.

  • The first week starts on the measurement period start date, and ends on the following Saturday.

  • The last week starts on Sunday and ends on the last day in the measurement period.

  • The number of weeks in the report is based on the measurement periods, as well as the Start Date and End Date from the report setup parameters.

  • The Number of Weeks count is the number of days in the measurement period divided by seven. It is not the total number of weeks for all employees on the report.

Employee-level “Number of Weeks” and Hours

  • Report hours accumulate for each employee, in each week in the measurement period.

  • When assigning hours to a particular week, the system uses the charge date on the employee time sheet to select that week.

  • If the hours do not have an associated charge date, then the system uses the Pay Period End date for the voucher.

  • If there are no hours for a certain week within the employee measurement period, the report still uses that week when it calculates the average.

Calculating Hours

  • If the pay code Calculation Method is Standard and the Pay Reason is Hourly, then the report counts the number of Hours Paid. Otherwise, it counts the Hours Worked.

  • If a pay code is set to be excluded from ACA hours calculation, the report does not include those hours in the report.

  • You can also import external hours into the system. The report will include these hours.

Review the following table for descriptions of other values in the report:

Column Description
Employee Total Hours The total of all hours for every week in the employee measurement period.
Average Hours Total employee hours divided by the number of weeks in the employee measurement period.
Valid Leave Days If an employee was on valid leave during the reporting period, the report totals the number of leave days. The number of days in the report is weighted against the number of days of valid leave. This factor is used to adjust the full-time employment (FTE) requirements for the employee.
FTE Flag If the employee worked an average of 30 hours per week, this flag is set to Y, otherwise it is set to N. If valid leave days affected this calculation, an asterisk also displays (Y or N), and the report includes a footnote with an explanation.
Health Coverage The report checks for all medical benefits plans. If the employee is actively enrolled in a medical benefit plan with a coverage start date before the end of the measurement period, this is set to Y, otherwise it is N. If an employee is actively enrolled in any medical benefit plan, then Waived is set to N.
Waived If an employee: - is not actively enrolled in any other medical benefit plan, and - has waived coverage in any other medical benefit plan with a coverage start date before the end of the measurement period this column displays a Y.
  • is not actively enrolled in any other medical benefit plan, and
  • has waived coverage in any other medical benefit plan with a coverage start date before the end of the measurement period | Break in Service | A break in service is a period when the employee is not active. The Break in Service Term is usually 13 weeks unless otherwise specified in the client-level ACA Period setup. Valid leave days are counted as active and are not treated as a break in service. The report uses the last hire date for all employees when determining a break in service: - If the last hire and original hire dates are the same, then there was no break in service. - If the dates are not the same, but the difference is fewer than 4 weeks, the system counts those weeks as zero. - If the system determines that the total employment time – before being terminated or going on invalid leave – is greater than the difference between 4 weeks and the number of Break in Service Term weeks, then the system counts those weeks as zero. - If total employment time – before being terminated or going on invalid leave – is less than the difference between 4 weeks and the Break in Service Term weeks, then the employee starts new on the last hire date. - If the difference between the last hire date and original hire date is greater than the Break in Service Term, the employee starts new on the last hire date. - If the employee goes back and forth between employed and not employed, and never exceeds the maximum break in service time, the system uses their original hire date. If a break in service with the allowed weeks occurs during an employee measurement period, a double asterisk displays in front of the employee status class (for example, **A) and the report includes an explanatory footnote. |
  • If the last hire and original hire dates are the same, then there was no break in service.
  • If the dates are not the same, but the difference is fewer than 4 weeks, the system counts those weeks as zero.
  • If the system determines that the total employment time – before being terminated or going on invalid leave – is greater than the difference between 4 weeks and the number of Break in Service Term weeks, then the system counts those weeks as zero.
  • If total employment time – before being terminated or going on invalid leave – is less than the difference between 4 weeks and the Break in Service Term weeks, then the employee starts new on the last hire date.
  • If the difference between the last hire date and original hire date is greater than the Break in Service Term, the employee starts new on the last hire date.
  • If the employee goes back and forth between employed and not employed, and never exceeds the maximum break in service time, the system uses their original hire date. ## More Information About the Dates on the Eligibility Report

On both reports

  • The Effective Hire Date is the last employee hire date, based on the hire date in the employee record and the break in service logic.

  • If Period setup requires that the administration period start on the first of the month, the measurement end date is extended to the last day of the month when it is calculated.

  • Both reports select employee vouchers by employee ID, then by client, and finally by the period end date on the voucher.

  • To select vouchers, both reports use the date 31 days after your entered End Date. The system processes the hours in the voucher based on the charge date or — if a charge date is not used — based on the voucher period end date. These settings ensure that the system processes all charge dates.

  • Both reports use the charge data associated with the hours from the voucher (or the voucher period if there is no charge date associated with the hours) to determine the week within the measurement period. The reports then accumulate the hours in that week.

  • If the voucher hours date is outside the employee measurement period, the reports do not include those hours.

On the new hire report

On the New Hire report, all start and end dates vary based the employee effective hire date.

Date — Description

Measurement Start Date — The employee effective hire date. The starting date to include in the report is the Start Date that you entered minus the number of days or months in the New Hire Measurement Period fields, defined in the client or global Period Setup.

Measurement End Date — The last day of the employee measurement period based on the effective hire date and the New Hire Measurement Period fields, defined in the client or global Period Setup.

Admin Start Date — The day after the Measurement End Date. If this day must be the first of the month, the Measurement End Date is extended to the last day of the previous month.

Admin End Date — Based on the Admin Start Date and the New Hire Administration Period fields in Period Setup.

Stability Start Date — The day after the Admin End Date.

Stability End Date — Based on the Stability Start Date and the New Hire Stability Period fields in Period Setup.

On the ongoing employee report

On the Ongoing report, all start and end dates will be the same for all employees. These dates are based on the criteria in Period Setup and the year for the Start Date you entered.

Date — Description

Measurement Start Date — Based on the Start Month/Day in Period Setup and the Start Date you entered when you ran the report.

Measurement End Date — The last day of the employee’s measurement period, based on the Measurement Start Date and the Ongoing Measurement Period fields in Period Setup.

Admin Start Date — The day after the Measurement End Date. If this day must be the first of the month, the Measurement End Date is extended to the last day of the previous month.

Admin End Date — Based on the Admin Start Date and the Ongoing Administration Period fields in Period Setup.

Stability Start Date — The day after the Admin End Date.

Stability End Date — Based on the Stability Start Date and the Ongoing Stability Period fields in Period Setup.

Running the Monthly ACA Eligibility Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_Eligibility_Rpt_Monthly.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_Eligibility_Rpt_Monthly.htm

Running the Monthly ACA Eligibility Report

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Note: Note: This form supports entity-level security for Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors, as defined on the Users > Actions > Data Security form.

Use this report to display month-by-month ACA eligibility information (hours) for specific clients and employees. An employee is considered full-time if they worked an average of 130 hours or more per month.

Column Name Description

Hire Date System-supplied employee hire date
Monthly Columns These columns display employee hours paid or worked for a given month in the report date range. The system uses the Hours Paid value for pay codes with their Calculation Method set to Standard and their Pay Reason set to Hourly. For any other pay codes, the system uses the Hours Worked value.
Health Coverage Y if the employee is currently enrolled in a medical benefit plan that has a Coverage Start Date prior to the end of the measurement period. Otherwise, N.
Waived N if the Health Coverage column is Y. Y if the employee: - is not enrolled in a medical benefit plan, and - has waived coverage under any other medical benefit plan with a Coverage Start Date prior to the end of the measurement period.
  • is not enrolled in a medical benefit plan, and
  • has waived coverage under any other medical benefit plan with a Coverage Start Date prior to the end of the measurement period. | FTE | Whether or not the employee counts as a full-time equivalent for the purposes of ACA reporting. Y if their average hours per month is greater than or equal to 130. Otherwise, N. | ========================================================================

Running the Large Employer Test Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_Lg_Employer_Test_Report.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/ACA_Lg_Employer_Test_Report.htm

Running the Large Employer Test Report

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Note: Note: This form supports entity-level security for Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors, as defined on the Users > Actions > Data Security form.

The Large Employer Test Report identifies clients that employ 50 or more employees. It displays the status of each employee, as of the End Date of the report.

Background

The system pulls the hours for this report from two sources:

  • Processed payroll data

  • Imported employee hours

Note: Note: Before running the Large Employer Test Report, PrismHR recommends that you analyze average employee hours worked and whether each employee’s type is appropriate. For example, if an employee averages 80 hours per month, they should not be flagged as full-time. If they are, it can have a negative impact on the output of the report.

Important Notes and Considerations

Employment Classifications

The system considers the following information when creating the Large Employer Test Report:

  • If an employee is classified as full-time active on the first and last day of a month, the report displays 120 hours for that month.

  • If an employee is not full-time, the report displays their actual hours , but the amount displayed will not exceed 120 hours per month.

The report uses hours paid for any Standard pay codes with Pay Reason set to Hours. For pay codes that do not meet these criteria, the report uses hours worked, if any exist.

  • If the employee works at least 120 hours each month for the months specified, the report counts the employee as 1.

Seasonal Employees

  • Employees with Seasonal status will not be included if they worked 120 or fewer consecutive days.

  • The report does include employees who were not considered seasonal-only during the reporting period. In other words, if they were Active and Full- or Part-Time, Non-Seasonal at any point in the report period, the report will include them. The system will also activate the Seasonal flag on their employee record so that, if there is any question about their status, the Seasonal flag will make them stand out in the report.

Consider this example: Joe starts the year as a seasonal employee. Sixty days later, Joe becomes a full-time employee. After this change, a service provider runs the Large Employer Test Report for the year and includes Joe’s client or employer. In this scenario, Joe’s entire employment period will be included in the report, and his Seasonal column entry will display as Y on the report (and on his employee record in the system).

Procedure

To run the Large Employer Test Report:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Reporting, select ACA Large Employer Test Report.

Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors can access this report through Reports > HR | Report > ACA Large Employer Test Report.

  1. Select either Employer or Client and then, in the grid that displays, enter the IDs of any employers or clients you want to include on the report. Leave the grid blank to include all employers or clients.

  2. Specify whether you want the report to select data from a specific Date Range (Start Date and End Date) or for the Full Year.

The Start and End Dates must be the first and last days of the month, respectively.

There is no minimum number of months that you can include in the range, but the maximum is 12 months.

  1. Select Generate Report.

Running the Employee Classification Analysis Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/EE_Classification_Analysis_Rpt.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/EE_Classification_Analysis_Rpt.htm

Running the Employee Classification Analysis Report

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Note: Note: This form supports entity-level security for Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors, as defined on the Users > Actions > Data Security form.

This report displays any employee hours worked associated with the provided employment type class codes or employment type codes. It uses current employee statuses to gather this information.

You can use this report to review selected employee types so that employers can correct their data before running the Large Employer Test Report. The Large Employer Test Report helps determine which clients are affected by the Affordable Care Act, so accurate employee type data is essential.

To run this report:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Reporting, select ACA Employee Classification Analysis Report.

Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors can access this report through Reports > HR | Report > ACA Employee Classification Analysis Report.

  1. Enter the Ending Year and Ending Month for the report data.

  2. Enter a Client ID for each client you want to include in the report

Use the following fields to limit the classes and codes included in the report. Otherwise, the system will include all classes and codes:

  1. In the Employment Type Classification field, specify which employment type (or types) to include: Full-Time, Part-Time, or both.

  2. Enter each Employment Type Code to include in the report.

  3. Specify any Status Classification Codes and Status Codes you want to include in the report.

  4. In the Exclude from Report panel, specify any Status Classification Codes and Status Codes you do not want to include in the report.

  5. Click Generate Report.

Report

For each employee on the report, the system includes:

  • Employment type and status (for example, Full-Time and Active)

  • Type class and status class associated with the type and status codes

  • Whether the employee is seasonal

Finally, the report displays their last active date and the date of their last termination (if applicable), followed by the number of hours worked for each month in the date range.

Review this data and ensure that employee hour counts are appropriate for their current status and type.

Running the Electronic 1095-C Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Electronic_1095C_Report.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Electronic_1095C_Report.htm

Running the Electronic 1095-C Report

The Electronic 1095-C Report lists employees for a specified year, along with their Form 1095-C register status and the identity of the individual designated to receive an electronic Form 1095-C.

Procedure

To run this report:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Reporting, select Electronic 1095-C Report.

  4. Enter the reporting Year.

  5. If you want to include employees who have a Form 1095-C for the selected year, select Yes for Employees with 1095C.

  6. If you want to include employees who elected early access to Form 1095-C, select Yes for Employees with Electronic 1095C Form. The report will include employees if Electronic 1095C Form is selected in the Tax section of their Employee Details record.

  7. Click Run to generate the report.

Report

The report includes the following columns:

  • Employee Name: Employee name displays as last name first.

  • Employee ID

  • 1095C Register: Y (yes) if the employee is listed on the 1095-C Register, or N (no) if they are not listed on the 1095-C Register.

  • Electronic 1095C: Y (yes) if the employee receives a electronic 1095-C or N (no) if they receive a paper 1095-C.
