Omitting Employees from the Large Employer Test Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Employees-to-Omit-for-Large-Employer-Report.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Employees-to-Omit-for-Large-Employer-Report.htm

Omitting Employees from the Large Employer Test Report

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

This form defines the employees – company owners and/or their family members – that you want to exclude from the Large Employer Test Report.

Note: Note: You can find a client’s Business Entity type in the Detail tab of the Client Details form. You can configure an employee’s Business Owner and Family Member settings in the Work tab of the Employee Details form.

To configure employee exclusion from the Large Employer Test Report:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Setup and Reporting Setup, select Employees to Omit on Large Employer Report.

  4. Select a Business Entity type.

  5. Select whether you want to omit the Owner and/or Family Members from the report.

  6. Repeat as needed for other rows and business entities.

  7. Click Save when you finish.

========================================================================

Establishing ACA Large Employer Status

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Establish_ACA_Large_ER_Status.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Establish_ACA_Large_ER_Status.htm

Establishing ACA Large Employer Status

After running the ACA Large Employer report (See About the Large Employer Test Report), you can use PrismHR to indicate which clients count as Large Employers according to ACA Guidelines. You only need to perform this procedure if a client actually qualifies as a Large Employer.

To set a client as a large employer:

  1. Open the Client menu. Select Client Details under Client|Change and then select the Benefits tab.

  2. Specify the calendar Year in which this client is considered a Large Employer according to ACA guidelines.

  3. Select Yes in the ACA Large Employer field.

  4. Click Save.

========================================================================

Excluding Pay Codes from ACA Reports

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Excl_Pay_Codes_from_ACA_Reports.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Excl_Pay_Codes_from_ACA_Reports.htm

Excluding Pay Codes from ACA Reports

You can exclude specific pay codes from the output of several reports, including the Large Employer Test Report, Eligibility Report, and Eligibility Report (Legacy).

To exclude pay codes from these reports:

  1. Open the Back Office menu.

  2. Under System|Change, select Pay Codes.

  3. Enter the ID of the Pay Code you need to change.

  4. Select the Exclude From ACA Hours Calculation option.

  5. Click Save.

========================================================================

Granting ACA Report Access to Client Users

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Granting_ACA_Rpt_Access_to_Clients.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Granting_ACA_Rpt_Access_to_Clients.htm

Granting ACA Report Access to Client Users

Though most ACA processing forms are only accessible to Service Provider users, you can give your client-level users – such as Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors – access to certain ACA reports.

To grant client users access to these reports, you must add that form to the User Role of the relevant user.

Note: Note: To grant ACA report access to a client-level user, you must select the path that starts from the Reports menu instead of the Work Centers menu. You cannot grant Work Center access to Worksite Managers or Trusted Advisors, but you can reach the same set of ACA reports from either menu.

Example

For example, to grant a Worksite Manager access to the ACA 9.5% Rule Test Report:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. Select User Roles under System|Change.

  3. Enter or select the Role ID associated with the relevant client-level user.

A list of accessible forms displays.

  1. Click Add at the bottom of the User Roles form. A new form overlays on the previous one and displays a list of forms you can add to the role.

  2. Locate the name of the form you want to add. For example: HR > Report > ACA 9.5% Rule Test Report.

  3. Select that form in the list and then click Accept to return to the User Roles form.

  4. Click Save on the User Roles form. Any Worksite Manager or Worksite Trusted advisor with this user role will now have access to the 9.5% Rule Test Report.

========================================================================

About the Large Employer Test Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Large_Employer_Test_Report.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Large_Employer_Test_Report.htm

About the Large Employer Test Report

The Large Employer Test Report identifies clients that employ 50 or more employees. It displays the status of each employee, as of the End Date of the report.

The system derives the hours for this report from two sources:

  • Processed payroll information from the system.

  • External ACA-related employee hours that have been updated using the External ACA Hours utility.

Note: Note: Before running the Large Employer Test Report, PrismHR recommends that you analyze the average hours worked and if the current employee type is appropriate. For example, if an employee averages 80 hours per month then they should not be flagged as full-time. If they are, it can have a negative impact on the Large Employer Test Report.

Review the following topics for more guidance:

  • Large Employer Test Report Considerations

  • Omitting Employees from the Large Employer Test Report

  • Establishing ACA Large Employer Status

========================================================================

Including Leave Status Codes on the Eligibility Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Leave-Status-Code-Report-Overrides.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/Leave-Status-Code-Report-Overrides.htm

Including Leave Status Codes on the Eligibility Report

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

The system uses the information entered on this form to determine whether an employee has worked enough hours during the reporting period to be identified as full-time. If you identify an employee as on leave, the report calculation will include the days they were on leave.

Background

To be considered a full-time employee, an employee must work an average of 130 hours for each month included in the date range for which the report is run. If the report is run from January 1 to March 31, that would mean an employee needs to work at least 390 hours during the reporting period:

(9 months x 130 hours)/1 month = 1170 hours

(where one month = 24 hours x 31 days = 744 hours)

If, during that same time period, an employee was on valid leave (as indicated by one of the Leave Status codes on the ACA Reports Parameters), then the number of required hours will be adjusted by a factor based on the total days in the report and the total number of days the employee was on valid leave. For example:

  • Employee is on valid leave for 25 days

  • The number of days in the report is 273 days

  • Factor = (273 - 25) / 273 = .9084

  • The number of hours for this employee to be full time = .9084 x 1170 hours = 1062.828 hours.

Procedure

To set up leave status codes:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Setup and Reporting Setup, select Leave Status Codes Overrides for ACA Coverage Report.

  4. Enter or select each Leave Status Code you want to include in the eligibility report.

  5. Click Save.

========================================================================

Large Employer Test Report Considerations

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/LgER_Report_Considerations.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Reports/LgER_Report_Considerations.htm

Large Employer Test Report Considerations

Employment Classifications

The system considers the following information when creating the Large Employer Test Report:

  • If an employee is classified as full-time active on the first and last day of a month, the report displays 120 hours for that month.

  • If an employee is not full-time, the report displays their actual hours , but the amount displayed will not exceed 120 hours per month.

The report uses hours paid for any Standard pay codes with Pay Reason set to Hours. For pay codes that do not meet these criteria, the report uses hours worked, if any exist.

  • If the employee works at least 120 hours each month for the months specified, the report counts the employee as 1.

Seasonal Employees

  • Employees with Seasonal status will not be included if they worked 120 or fewer consecutive days.

  • Employees will be included on the report if they were not considered seasonal-only during the reporting period. In other words, if they were Active and Full- or Part-Time Non-Seasonal at any point in the report period, they will be included in the report, regardless of the number of days this was true. The Seasonal flag will also be enabled on their employee record so that, if there is any question about their status, the Seasonal flag will make them stand out in the report.

Consider this example: Joe starts the year as a seasonal employee. Sixty days later, Joe becomes a full-time employee. In this scenario, Joe’s entire employment period will be included in the report and the Seasonal flag will be set to Y.

Control Groups

When clients are part of a Control Group, as indicated in Client Details or Company Master configuration, they will be listed under that Control Group Client in the Large Employer Test Report.

Importing External ACA Hours

Use the ACA External Hours Import form to import external ACA hours into PrismHR. This import allows you to add hours to be used in ACA eligibility calculations when bringing on a client mid-year.

For example: if your client started in PrismHR in June last year but you are now processing ACA eligibility for the prior year, you can use this form to import each employee’s hours for January to May last year, month by month.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To access this form, under Work Centers click ACA Processing. Under ACA Processes, under IMPORT/EXPORT click ACA External Hours Import.

  • Skip record – Select this option to skip the new data completely for the existing employees in PrismHR.

Setting Up ACA Benefits Eligibility Rules

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/ACA-Bnft-Elig-Rules.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/ACA-Bnft-Elig-Rules.htm

Setting Up ACA Benefits Eligibility Rules

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Use the Benefit Eligibility Rules form to manage the eligibility rules for client benefits that apply to employees in Offered Employees Maintenance (see Setting Up ACA Offered Employees). These are typically variable-hour employees that are considered full-time for ACA reporting purposes.

You must specify the employment status and type for each of the relevant medical plans. You do not need to accommodate employee statuses and types that satisfy standard client-level benefit rules.

Note: Note: The system determines benefits eligibility based on employee status and type, based on the rules your organization entered into the system, how you assign employees, and other factors. This form defines eligibility for both benefits enrollment and under the ACA. If an employee does not qualify for the benefit plan offered by the client – for example, by not working enough hours – then they are eligible for coverage under the ACA. For more details, see How the System Determines Employee Eligibility.

To set up ACA benefits eligibility rules:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Setup, select Benefit Eligibility Rules (ACA).

  4. Select the Benefit Plan ID from the Select Benefit Plan list to configure.

  5. Enter a Network ID, if applicable, and specify an Effective Date for the benefit plan.

  6. Specify the Employment Statuses that qualify as eligible for this plan.

  7. Select the Employment Types that qualify as eligible for this plan.

  8. Click Save.

Setting Up ACA Type Overrides

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/ACA-Type-Overrides.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/ACA-Type-Overrides.htm

Setting Up ACA Type Overrides

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

Use this form to define full-time employees as part-time for certain periods, if this is required for ACA reporting.

To set up employee type overrides for ACA reporting:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Setup and Client Setup, select ACA Type Overrides.

  4. Select an Employee.

  5. Enter the Start Date and End Date of the period in which the system will treat the employee as part-time, for ACA reporting purposes.

  6. Click Save.

Configuring Client ACA Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Client-ACA-Parameters.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Client-ACA-Parameters.htm

Configuring Client ACA Parameters

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

You can use the client-level ACA parameters to override system-level ACA parameters (see Configuring Global ACA Parameters). You can also specify other client-specific information on this form.

Basic Parameters

Review the information below for details about some of these override settings.

Safe Harbor Compensation Basis Types

The system provides three basis type options to determine employee compensation and, by extension, the affordability of offered ACA coverage:

Basis Type — Description

Federal Poverty Level — Affordability is based on annual household income, factored by household size; you can set annual federal poverty level overrides in the table below.

Rate of Pay — Affordability is based on a multiple of the lowest pay rate for the employee.

Form W-2 Wages — Affordability is based on a multiple of the employee’s Form W-2 wages for the calendar year.

About the Rate of Pay Safe Harbor

The Rate of Pay safe harbor looks at the monthly pay of salaried employees. It also excludes any salaried employee whose rate of pay dropped, during the calendar year, from the safe harbor consideration.

Rate of pay is determined differently for salaried and hourly employees:

  • For salaried employees, the system uses the rate of pay on the first day of the plan year.

  • For hourly employees, the safe harbor uses the lesser of: either the rate of pay at the start of the year or the rate of pay as of the first of each month.

As documented on the form and in the official ACA guidance, each of these basis types is also associated with its own ACA series 2 offer code:

  • 2G – The coverage offered is affordable based on the Federal Poverty Level safe harbor

  • 2H – The coverage is affordable based on the Rate of Pay safe harbor

  • 2F – The coverage is affordable based on employee Form W-2 Wages

Federal Poverty Level Overrides

If you set the client basis type to Federal Poverty Level/2G, you can also specify override values for this metric. The Federal Poverty Amount and Percent of Federal Poverty Amount fields are assigned based on plan years, not calendar years.

For example, if the plan year runs from June 1, 2019 through May 31, 2020, the system users the amounts entered for Plan Year 2019. If the plan year starts on January 1, then the plan year matches the calendar year.

Use the table on this form to specify yearly poverty level overrides, as needed:

  • First, select the appropriate calendar Year.

  • Enter the Federal Poverty Amount for a household of one.

  • Finally, enter the % of Poverty Level used to calculate affordability.

Procedure

To establish general client-level override parameters for the ACA:

  1. Go to Work Centers > ACA Processing > ACA Setup > Client ACA Parameters (Overrides).

  2. Select a Safe Harbor Compensation Basis Type (see above for details).

  3. Select the Benefit Plan Year Start Month and Day if the plans are not on a calendar year cycle and you need to override the system-level date settings.

  4. Specify whether Cafe Plans Are For Medical Only – this only applies to clients who have cafeteria plans. This determines whether the system takes into account the client cafeteria plan contribution when calculating the cost of insurance for applicable employees and benefit plans.

  5. If necessary, specify any annual overrides related to the Federal Poverty Level (see the sections above for details).

  6. Click Save.

Setting Up Look-back Periods

Use the Periods panel to set up measurement, administration, and stability periods for new and ongoing employees at the client level. These settings override the settings at the system level.

About Look-back Periods

The ACA specifies three periods for determining employee eligibility: Measurement, Administration, and Stability.

  • Measurement Period: This look-back period determines whether new variable hour/seasonal employees are full-time (working 130 hours or more within the period). For Ongoing Employees, who have worked one full standard measurement period, this determines the average number of hours they work (130 or more = full-time).

  • Administration Period: This is the period between the Measurement Period and implementation of benefits. 90 days maximum.

  • Stability Period: This is the coverage “lock-in” period for employees identified by the system as full-time (based on the results from the Measurement Period). Must be 6-12 months long.

On this form, you can configure these periods for both new and existing employees.

Procedure

To configure look-back and eligibility periods on the client level:

  1. Specify the number of Months or Days in the Measurement Period. For Ongoing Employees, specify the period Start Month/Day.

  2. Specify the number of Months or Days in the Administration Period, and indicate whether the period Starts First of the Month.

  3. Specify the number of Months or Days in the Stability Period.

  4. Indicate the Break-In Service Term. This field specifies maximum number of weeks (default is 13) where the employee is not employed but remains eligible for healthcare if they are rehired. After this period, the system counts the employee as “starting new” instead of as ongoing. For example, you might enter 26 weeks for an educational institution.

Setting Up ALE Members

Use this panel to enter any control group clients (Applicable Large Employers, or ALEs) besides those specified at the system level, who will use the same FEIN that your organization uses in other parts of PrismHR.

To configure ALE members for this client:

  1. Expand the Applicable Large Employer (ALE) Members panel.

  2. Enter or select an ALE Group ID. All the clients in PrismHR who have the same Control Group populate on the form.

  • This data is dynamically displayed as clients under this group change.

  • You can edit this information as you may need to list members that are not stored in PrismHR.

  • This data populates using the Client Legal Name and FEIN.

  1. Edit the entries as necessary.

  2. Click Save.

Configuring the Lowest Cost Calculation Table

Note: Note: This table does not allow you to enter values in both the Benefit Plan ID and Amount fields. You can only use one or the other.

If your organization cannot pull the lowest-cost plans from your database, enter the data in the Lowest Cost Plan Calculation Table. You do not need to use this form unless accurate reporting absolutely depends on it.

The system uses this information when some employees are not eligible for any medical plans under either standard or ACA benefit rules (for example, if there are no medical plans set up for the client, or if employees are not eligible for any medical plans based on their benefit rules).

The system checks each line item in the grid, for each affected employee, to determine whether the employee meets the criteria specified on that line. If so, the system uses the information on that line and does not check any more lines. With this in mind, ensure that you dedicate the top of the list for the plans with the most criteria.

To set up the Lowest Cost Plan Calculation Table:

  1. Expand the Lowest Cost Calculation Table panel.

  2. Enter the eligibility information as appropriate (all fields are optional):

  • Start Date

  • End Date

  • Location

  • Division

  • Department

  • Benefit Group

  • Plan ID

  • Amount

  • Offer Code

  1. Click Save.

Specifying ACA Large Employer Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Client_ACA_Reporting_Fields.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Client_ACA_Reporting_Fields.htm

Specifying ACA Large Employer Information

To set ACA large employer reporting information in PrismHR:

  1. Make sure you see the correct client record and name near the top of the form. If not, click on the client name and select the correct client.

  2. Open the Client menu.

  3. Under Client|Change, select Client Details.

  4. Select the Benefits tab.

Then, for each year:

  1. Enter the Year.

  2. Indicate whether the client qualifies as an ACA Large Employer according to the IRS definition. If you do not select a value, the system defaults to No.

  3. Indicate whether to Show in ESS (this determines whether or not employees can see their 1095-C information in Employee Portal (EP)). You should do this for a reporting year once you have built the registers and confirmed that the information is complete. If you do not select an option, the system defaults to No.

  4. In the 1095 Employer field, indicate whether the system should use the employer name and address from Form 1094-C or the Employer Master/Employer Details record. These values populate in the Applicable Large Employer Member fields on the Form 1095-C.

  5. Click Save.

Giving Employees Access to Form 1095-C

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/EE_Access_to_1095C.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/EE_Access_to_1095C.htm

Giving Employees Access to Form 1095-C

Your organization can grant electronic access to employee Forms 1095-C by adding menu items to the Employee Portal (EP) menu. You can also give employees early access to this form.

To set up employee access to Form 1095-C (follow the links for detailed instructions):

Step # Description Link
1 Write the early 1095-C election text and set the lockout dates. See Creating 1095-C Election Text
2 Add the menu items for early 1095-C election and access to the electronic Form 1095-C to the EP menu. See Employee Portal Menu Items for Form 1095-C
3 Employees decide to elect early 1095-C access in EP, which updates their records in PrismHR. See Employee Election for Early 1095-C
4 You can run the Electronic 1095-C Report to see which employees have 1095-Cs and which ones elected to have early access. See Running the Electronic 1095-C Report
5 Build the 1095-C registers. Building ACA 1095 Registers
6 Set up the clients to display the 1095-Cs in EP. See Specifying ACA Large Employer Information

Employees who elected to participate in early Form 1095-Cs can see them online as soon as the registers are built and available. Employees who did not elect to participate in early Form 1095-Cs can see them starting on March 2.

Employee Election for Early 1095-C

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Employee_Elects_Early_1095C.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Employee_Elects_Early_1095C.htm

Employee Election for Early 1095-C

Part of a process on giving employees access to Form 1095-C (See Giving Employees Access to Form 1095-C).

Employees of clients managed in PrismHR can elect to get early access to their Forms 1095-C.

In Employee Portal (EP), the employee uses the screen that you set up to elect coverage.

  • When an employee chooses to participate in the early electronic 1095-C program, this is indicated by the Electronic 1095C Form option in the Miscellaneous panel of the Employee Details > Tax tab. They will be able to log on to EP and check for their 1095-C using the 1095-C menu item before March 2.

  • Employees who do not elect to participate in the program will receive it in the mail. They can access it online starting on March 2.

[Image: Miscellaneous panel of Employee Details Tax tab. The checkbox “Electronic 1095C Form” is selected and highlighted.]

========================================================================

Activating the ACA Data Build

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Enabling_ACA_Databuild.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Enabling_ACA_Databuild.htm

Activating the ACA Data Build

After your organization enables the ACADATABUILD custom feature code, the system builds employee 1095-C registers once a month over the course of the year. This improves the performance of the final register build.

Note: Note: After setting the feature code, the first user who logs in will initiate the first build process. You should either log out and lock back in to start the build, or have another user log in.

If you have not set the custom feature code yet, you can set it up and initialize it during an off-peak time, like the evening or a weekend. This is because the initial run of the process takes a long time and impacts performance. After the initial run, the process will run in the background when users log in, updating information.

If at any point you need to adjust your information and rebuild it, you must do that manually.

Procedure

To enable this feature:

  1. Open the Back Office menu and select System Parameters under System|Change.

  2. In the Custom Feature Codes table, create a new row and enter ACADATABUILD.

  3. Click Save.

========================================================================

Employee Portal Menu Items for Form 1095-C

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/EP_Menu_Items_for_Form_1095C.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/EP_Menu_Items_for_Form_1095C.htm

Employee Portal Menu Items for Form 1095-C

Part of the process on giving employees access to Form 1095-C (See Giving Employees Access to Form 1095-C).

You can use the global or client-level “ESS Default Menu Builder” to add the Early 1095-C and 1095-C tiles to the Employee Portal (EP) menus of your clients.

These tiles serve two different roles:

  • The Early 1095-C tile lets employees choose to receive their Form 1095-C electronically.

  • The 1095-C tile lets employees see their Form 1095-C in EP for the years specified in the Client Details > Benefits tab.

To add 1095-C tiles to client Employee Portal (EP) menus:

  1. Open the appropriate form:

  • Global: Open System Parameters from the Back Office menu (under System|Change) and select ESS Default Menu Builder from the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] .

  • Client: Open Client|Change > Client Details and select ESS Menu Builder from the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] .

  1. Expand the menu header where you want to place the item. (If you want to create a new menu header, click New Menu Header and fill out the form).

  2. Drag Early 1095-C to the menu and place it where you want it to display in the menu.

  3. Repeat steps 2 and 3 for 1095-C.

  4. Click Save.

Configuring Global ACA Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Global-ACA-Parameters.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Global-ACA-Parameters.htm

Configuring Global ACA Parameters

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To process ACA data properly, you must establish system-level ACA parameters. If necessary, you can also override these at the client level: see Configuring Client ACA Parameters.

Setting Up Global ACA Parameters

To set up global ACA parameters:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Setup and Global Setup, select Global ACA Parameters.

  4. First, set the global safe harbor compensation basis. You can choose one of three options, described by the fields below.

Select this field… — to…

Federal Poverty Level (2G Series 2 Code) — Base affordability on the federal poverty amount for the appropriate year, specified in the table below.

Rate of Pay (2H Series 2 Code) — Base affordability on each employee’s lowest pay rate during the plan year. You must enter the Benefit Plan Year Start Month and Day.

Form W2 Wages (2F Series 2 Code) — Base affordability on employee Form W-2 wages for the calendar year.

The Rate of Pay Safe Harbor looks at monthly pay for salaried employees. It excludes any salaried employee whose rate of pay dropped from the Safe Harbor consideration during the calendar year.

  • For salaried employees, it uses the rate of pay as of the first of the plan year.

  • For hourly employees, it uses either the rate of pay at the start of the year or the rate of pay as of the first of each month (whichever is less).

  1. Set the Benefit Plan Year Start Month and Day. These values support benefit plans that are not on a calendar year cycle. You can override this at the client level. If you do not enter a value, the system defaults to January 1.

  2. If your clients use cafeteria plans, select the Cafe Contributions Are For Medical Insurance Only field. The system will take into account the client cafeteria contribution when it calculates the cost of insurance for applicable employees and benefit plans.

  3. The next field is called Enter “Yes” for MEC on 1094 Form Starting In. Beginning with the year selected here, the system will automatically set the Minimum Essential Coverage indicator to “Yes” in the Form 1094-C record. This option overrides the Does NOT Meet ACA Minimum Essential Coverage field on the Group Benefit Plans form.

  4. Select whether you want to Mask Employee SSN on 1095 Forms. This replaces the first five digits with ‘X’.

  5. Adjust the data in the Federal Poverty Levels table, as needed. See Setting Federal Poverty Data, below, for details.

  6. The Limited Non-Assessment Period (LNAP) Overrides panel provides three options for adjusting LNAP calculation logic. Select only one option. For details, see Setting LNAP Overrides below.

  7. Configure your look-back period settings in the Periods panel. For details, see Setting Up Look-back Periods

  8. Click Save.

Setting LNAP Overrides

These parameters relate to IRS code 2D: “Employee in a section 4980H(b) Limited Non-Assessment Period”.

By default, PrismHR will assign the 2D series 2 code for the maximum possible LNAP, even if coverage is offered during that period. These settings provide more coding flexibility on Forms 1095‑C for employees who are still within their first 90 days of employment.

Field Name — Action

Limit “2D” Status to 3 Months — Limits the 2D offer code to the first 90 days of employment. After 90 days, you must select a different offer code, even if the employee has a mid-month hire date.

Continue “2D” if Employee’s Status Changes to Full-Time During LNAP — When an employee’s status changes to full-time, PrismHR will continue using “2D” for up to 3 calendar months, or 4 months if the period includes a partial month. You cannot use this setting to include more “2D” months than defined in the initial measurement period.

End “2D” Period on Offer of Coverage — Prevents the system from defaulting to a “2D” code when the employee’s series 1 code reflects an offer of coverage (for example, 1A).

Setting Federal Poverty Data

The Federal Poverty Amount and % of Poverty Level fields are assigned based on plan years, not calendar years.

For example: if the plan year runs from June 1, 2019, through May 31, 2020, the system uses the amounts entered for Plan Year 2019. If the plan year starts on January 1, then the plan year matches the calendar year.

This feature provides flexibility in case the IRS makes changes to the percentage before the start of the next year.

To configure federal poverty data:

Enter the federal poverty amounts for each year. You need to do this for each upcoming year before the end of January of that year. For example, you need to enter the amounts for 2026 before the end of January 2026.

  1. Create a new row in the table.

  2. Select the reporting Year.

  3. Enter the Federal Poverty Amount for a household of one.

  4. Enter the % of Poverty Level the system uses to calculate affordability. Do not include the percent sign: for example, 9.5% = 9.50.

For example: the Federal Poverty Amount for 2015 was $12,000 and the percentage was 9.5%. To determine the affordability of benefits in this year, PrismHR divides 12,000 by 12 to get a monthly amount, and then multiplies the result by 0.095. The final result is $95. If a benefit plan costs an employee more than $95 per month, then it is not considered affordable for ACA reporting.

Setting Up Look-back Periods

Use the Periods panel to set up measurement, administration, and stability periods for new and ongoing employees at the global level. You can override these settings at the client level.

About Look-back Periods

The ACA specifies three periods for determining employee eligibility: Measurement, Administration, and Stability.

  • Measurement Period: This look-back period determines whether new variable hour/seasonal employees are full-time (working 130 hours or more within the period). For Ongoing Employees, who have worked one full standard measurement period, this determines the average number of hours they work (130 or more = full-time).

  • Administration Period: This is the period between the Measurement Period and implementation of benefits. 90 days maximum.

  • Stability Period: This is the coverage “lock-in” period for employees identified by the system as full-time (based on the results from the Measurement Period). Must be 6-12 months long.

In this panel, you can configure these periods for both new and existing employees.

Procedure

To configure look-back and eligibility periods on the system level:

  1. Specify the number of Months or Days in the Measurement Period. For Ongoing Employees, specify the period Start Month/Day.

  2. Specify the number of Months or Days in the Administration Period, and indicate whether the period Starts First of the Month.

  3. Specify the number of Months or Days in the Stability Period.

  4. Indicate the Break-In Service Term. This field specifies maximum number of weeks (default is 13) where the employee is not employed but remains eligible for healthcare if they are rehired. After this period, the system counts the employee as “starting new” instead of as ongoing. For example, you might enter 26 weeks for an educational institution.

Configuring Group Benefit Plan Settings for the ACA

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Group_Benefit_Plans_for_ACA.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/Group_Benefit_Plans_for_ACA.htm

Configuring Group Benefit Plan Settings for the ACA

On the Group Benefit Plans form, your organization can indicate that a plan does not meet ACA guidelines. Since most plans meet those guidelines, you only need to edit the few plans that do not.

To enter the ACA reporting information for group benefit plans in PrismHR:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. Open the Group Benefit Plans form under System|Change.

  3. Enter the Benefit Plan ID. Benefit information populates on the form.

  4. Locate the ACA panel.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Set any of the nine ACA parameters, as needed.

Note: Note: The ACA section is visible only when you select an existing Benefit Plan ID which has an Insurance Class set up as Medical.

Parameter Description
Self Insured Plan Select if this is a self-insured plan.
Exclude from ACA Reporting Select to exclude the plan from ACA reports.
Does NOT Meet ACA Minimum Essential Coverage Select if the plan’s coverage does not meet the ACA minimum requirements
Does NOT meet ACA Minimum Value Requirements Select if the plan does not meet the ACA minimum requirements for value
Plan Covers Employee? Select if the plan covers employees.
Plan Covers Spouse? Select one of the following: - Yes: the medical plan covers spouses - No: the medical plan does not cover spouses - Conditional: the medical plan conditionally covers spouses. Typically, this applies to medical plans that only offer coverage to spouses if they are not offered medical insurance by their own employer.
  • Yes: the medical plan covers spouses
  • No: the medical plan does not cover spouses
  • Conditional: the medical plan conditionally covers spouses. Typically, this applies to medical plans that only offer coverage to spouses if they are not offered medical insurance by their own employer. | Plan Covers Other Dependents? | Select if the plan covers other dependents. | | Single Coverage Gross Wages % | Whether the benefit plan is employee-only. | | Allow Gross Wage % Contribution Method | Select this checkbox to set the Percent of Gross Wages as the employee contribution towards a medical plan (employee premium only). Clicking this checkbox will display the multivalued grid where you can enter an Effective Date along with a Percent of Gross Wages which will be used as a default setting. > Note: Note: To select the Allow Gross Wage % Contribution Method checkbox please ensure that the following checkboxes have been unchecked: - Single Coverage Gross Wage % - Do NOT Include In ACA Reporting Additionally, you can select this checkbox only if the Cost Basis field is set to either Monthly, Quarterly, or Yearly. If it is set to Weekly, then a message stating This feature is not compatible with a Cost Basis of Weekly is displayed. |
  • Single Coverage Gross Wage %
  • Do NOT Include In ACA Reporting

  1. Repeat for any applicable benefit plans.

  2. When you finish, click Save.

Workarounds for Multiple Employee-Only Plans Within a Single Benefit Plan

To ensure the best results and performance, the register build does not consider benefit plans for employee-only plans if the benefit plan has more than one employee-only plan defined. There are some workarounds, listed below. Use the one that works best for your organization.

Option 1: Temporarily Change the Higher Cost Plan Types

  1. Immediately before building the ACA data, adjust the setup of higher cost employee-only Benefit Plan Types to be not employee-only.

  2. Build the ACA data.

  3. Set the plan types back to employee-only.

Option 2: Change the Higher Cost Plan Types in Test Environment

If you have a test environment that matches and is perfectly up to date with your live environment, you can follow this procedure.

  1. In your test environment:

  • Before you build the ACA data, adjust the setup of higher cost employee-only Benefit Plan Types to be not employee-only.

  • Build the ACA data.

  • Build the 1095-C and 1094-C registers.

  • Export the register data using the Export 1095-C Registers feature.

  1. Import the register data into your live environment.

  2. Verify that the information is correct in the live environment.

  3. Set the plan types back to employee-only in your test environment.

Option 3: Set Up a Dummy Plan

Create a “dummy plan” with the same system- and client-level rules as an existing plan, but with a single employee-only plan type that reflects the lower cost employee-only plan type offered.

  1. Create a new Group Benefit Plan, and assign it one employee-only plan type, entering all of the amounts for the lowest cost employee-only plan type that you offer.

  2. Assign it to the client.

  3. Proceed with the ACA data and register builds.

Option 4: Use the Export 1095-C Register Feature

  1. Export the register data using the Export 1095-C Registers feature.

  2. Review the data, searching for shares and offer codes that reflect the higher-priced employee-only plan types. Correct those inappropriate codes.

Note: Note: If the higher-cost plan type was picked up, also note this could result in the employee not satisfying the applicable safe harbor code. In those cases, no safe harbor code is applied. In that case, review the safe harbor codes for months with the higher share, review the safe harbor codes. If blank, make any necessary adjustments.

  1. Import the register data.

How the System Determines Employee Eligibility

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/How_System_Determines_Elig.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-Setup/How_System_Determines_Elig.htm

How the System Determines Employee Eligibility

The system checks for the lowest-cost single plan for which an employee qualifies. The lowest cost is based on the size of the employee contribution, so it is important to set up this information correctly.

  1. First, the system checks the benefits rules. Employees qualify for these based on their status, type, and seniority:

  • Status indicates whether the employee is active, on leave, or terminated.

  • Type indicates whether the employee is full-time, part-time, and so on.

  • Seniority is based on either the hire date or seniority date, depending on your settings.

  1. If the employee does not qualify for any of those and is included in Offered Employees Maintenance, the system then checks the ACA benefit rules.

  2. If employees do not qualify based on either of those sets of rules, the system uses the optional grid. It checks the grid line-by-line until it reaches a plan for which the employee qualifies. Your organization should use the optional grid only if you are not maintaining benefits and benefit rules in the system and you have no other option for getting the information out of the system.

[Image: A flowchart showing the three steps in determining eligibility: 1. Looking at eligibility settings on the benefit rule 2. Company benefits eligibility rules and 3. Finding a plan on the Lowest Cost Plan Calculation Grid.]

Setting Up ACA Transmitter Control Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/ACA-Download-Master-Code.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/ACA-Download-Master-Code.htm

Setting Up ACA Transmitter Control Information

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

Under the Affordable Care Act (ACA) insurance companies, self-insured companies, and large businesses that provide health insurance to their employees must submit information returns to the IRS reporting on the health insurance coverage of each individual. You are required to file electronically if you plan to submit 250 or more returns.

You must have a Transmitter Control Code (TCC) to electronically file AIR Forms 1094/1095-B and 1094/1095-C. You can obtain this code during the registration process. Use the ACA Download Master Code form to add your master code to PrismHR.

To download the master code for the Affordable Care Act:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Setup and Global Setup, select ACA Download Master Code.

  4. Complete these fields as required:

Field Description
Transmitter Control Code The TCC that was assigned during the ACA registration process.
Transmitter Name The name associated with the TCC.
Employer ID Number The employer ID number associated with the TCC.
The following information is required for the CompanyInformationGrip section:
Company Name The name of the company associated with the TCC.
Company Address The street address of the company associated with the TCC.
City, State, Zip Code The city, state, and zip code associated with the TCC.
Contact Name The name of the contact associated with the TCC.
Contact Phone The phone number of the contact associated with the TCC.
  1. Click Save.

Entering State TCCs

You can use the table at the bottom of this form to enter any Transmitter Control Codes assigned by state-level reporting authorities. In a new row, enter a two-character State code (for example, CA) and its corresponding Transmitter Control Code, as needed.

ACA Transmittal Tips & Tricks

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/ACA_Transmittal_Tips.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/ACA_Transmittal_Tips.htm

ACA Transmittal Tips & Tricks

Use this information if you need help during the IRS electronic transmission process.

Important: Register Rebuilds Require PrismHR Support

If you already submitted an original file and you find that you need to rebuild the 1095-C registers, you must contact PrismHR support! Do not do this without assistance from a representative of the PrismHR Customer Support Team. Rebuilding the 1095-C registers on your own could cause you to lose critical transmission ID data and the IRS will reject your new transmission in that case.

Contacting Support

If you need to contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team, please provide this information in your ticket:

  1. Take a screenshot of the errors.

  2. Click Download and save the error file.

  3. Open a support ticket and include the screenshot.

  4. When a support representative contacts you, send a secure e-mail containing the files you submitted to the IRS and the detailed download of errors.

[Image: Example of IRS tranmission status page with errors. The errors state “TIN validation failed”.]

Additional Clients

Based on client setup, you might need to select additional clients so that you can file your forms correctly with the IRS. Our system creates Form 1094-C and Forms 1095-C at the client level. If you select additional clients, they will all be reported under the Form 1094-C filing of the active client. This includes all employee Forms 1095-C.

One possible real-world example is a staffing organization that needs to separate a single company into multiple client accounts.

Keep Transmission IDs and Receipt IDs

Make sure you keep a record of each transmission ID and its corresponding receipt ID, when you receive it from the IRS. The system populates the transmission ID on “Edit 1094 Form” (See Editing Form 1094-C Information), but you must enter the receipt ID when the IRS sends it to you.

Note: Note: The receipt ID is separate from the submission ID. Make sure you use the receipt ID.

Transmission versus Submission

A transmission is an entire file that you transmit to the IRS. The transmission also contains the submission, which is the Form 1094-C record with or without associated Form 1095-C records.

At this time, PrismHR supports only one submission per transmission. That means that there will be only one Form 1094-C on each XML file that you create in the system.

It is possible that the IRS will reject either the transmission or the submission.

  • If they reject the transmission, that means that there is something inherently wrong with the file overall.

  • If they reject the submission, there are some specific issues with the file.

When you submit a replacement, you will indicate whether you are replacing a transmission or submission based on the rejection statement from the IRS.

Error in Transmission File Upload

If you receive an immediate error upon submission (as shown below), you need to submit another transmission using the same type, whether it was an original, correction, or replacement.

[Image: Example error on the IRS transmission file upload page, showing error message about the file name containing an invalid character.]

Transmission/Submission Accepted with Errors

If a transmission or submission is accepted with errors, you should submit a correction. The IRS will identify the specific items that are in error. For example, they might specify an item in the Form 1094-C or certain Forms 1095-C.

Once you make the corrections, submit them: see Submitting a Correction.

If you cannot resolve the problem, submit a ticket to the PrismHR Customer Support Team: to see the information you need to provide so that they can quickly assist you.

Correcting a Correction

Consider the following special case:

  1. The IRS accepted a transmission, with errors.

  2. You submit the correction and the IRS accepts it.

  3. Later, you determine that you actually need to make another correction to that record.

In this case, when you submit the correction, you need to use the receipt ID for the correction (not receipt ID of the original transmission).

The IRS Rejected My Transmission or Submission

If the IRS rejects your original transmission or submission (as in the case below), then you must send another one using the Replacement Submission Type.

  • Do not use replacement for rejected corrections. See Submitting a Replacement.

  • If you are having trouble understanding the issues, see Contacting Support above.

[Image: Another example of IRS tranmsision file errors. These errors state “XML Schema Validation Failed - not well formed or missing required elements”]

What do the error codes mean?

See Appendix A of the AIR Submission Composition and Reference Guide for a list of error codes and descriptions.

Other Common Issues

Here are some common issues that can cause the IRS to reject a transmission, or accept it with errors:

  • Any non-standard punctuation, as well as starting or trailing spaces

  • A covered individual is listed, but not marked as “covered” for any month

  • The value in an All 12 months field is different from a setting in any an individual month or months

  • Invalid Social Security or Employer Identification numbers

  • Invalid Receipt ID

  • Incomplete data, such as the information for only the first six months of a year

  • Sending the same file after it got “through the door” and was rejected (you must send a new one).

  • Creating the wrong type of file (original, replacement, or correction). If you submit an original and it should be a replacement, the IRS rejects it.

  • Manually editing a file: the manifest has a checksum and a file size count. If someone manually edits the form file and does not manually edit these, it will not work.

  • Trying to submit a test file for a client after your TCC code has moved to the IRS Production system.

  • Attaching the wrong files to send to the IRS, such as the Form file from one ZIP and the Manifest file from another.

AIR Transmission: Uploading the ACA Files

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/AIR_Submission.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/AIR_Submission.htm

AIR Transmission: Uploading the ACA Files

Use these instructions as general guidelines for submitting final client information to the IRS.

If you submit this file to the state of New Jersey or to Washington, D.C., you must select the NJ State Submission option before you create the file. When you select this option, the system appends a record to the transmission file for every employee who resided in New Jersey or D.C. at some point during the year.

Note: Note: These instructions are not meant to replace the instructions provided by the IRS or your state reporting body. Refer to Section 4 of the Affordable Care Act (ACA) Information Returns (AIR) Submission Composition and Reference Guide, or any documentation provided by your state government.

To submit your ACA transmission files:

  1. Log in to the AIR UI Channel (Testing or Production) using the credentials for the Contact or Responsible Official on the AIR TCC Application.

  2. Select the organization and click Submit Selected Organization.

  3. If prompted, select AATS and click Submit Selected Version. The Affordable Care Act Information Returns page displays.

  4. Click Upload ACA Forms. The ACA Transmission File Upload page displays. Use it to upload the Manifest and Form files that you generated for the client. Make sure that these files have the same time stamp and have same names (aside from the prefix), or the IRS will not accept the transmission.

  5. Click the Browse button next to the Manifest File field.

  6. Find and select the manifest file for the upload. This is the file that begins with the Manifest_ prefix.

  7. Click the Browse button next to the Form File field.

  8. Select the form file for the upload. It has the same name as the manifest file, only without the prefix.

  9. Verify that the information in the ACA Transmission File Upload page is correct.

[Image: ACA Tranmission File Upload webpage]

  1. Click Transmit.

  • If there is a problem with the files, the system requires you to correct them.

  • If there is not a problem “at the door”, the system takes you to a page with the Receipt ID. You must make record of it: see Entering the IRS Receipt ID.

Checking the AIR Transmission Status

Use these instructions as general guidelines for checking the status of the transmission.

Note: Note: These instructions are not meant to replace the instructions provided by the IRS. Refer to Section 4 of the Affordable Care Act (ACA) Information Returns (AIR) Submission Composition and Reference Guide.

To check the status of your transmission file:

  1. Log in to the AIR UI Channel (Testing or Production) using the credentials for the Contact or Responsible Official on the AIR TCC Application.

  2. Select the organization and click Submit Selected Organization.

  3. On the Select the AATS Version page, select AATS [Year] and click Submit Selected Version. The Affordable Care Act Information Returns page displays.

  4. Click Check Transmission Status. The Transmission Status Record page displays.

[Image: IRS webpage to check the transmission status]

  1. Enter your TCC.

  2. Enter the Receipt ID.

  3. Click Find. The Transmission Status Details page displays.

[Image: IRS webpage containing the details about a specific transmission and its status]

Click Back to return to the Transmission Status Record page. Click Print to print the details.

Submitting a Correction

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Correction_Transmission.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Correction_Transmission.htm

Submitting a Correction

If you want to create an Original or Replacement file, please see See Creating the ACA Transmission File or See Submitting a Replacement

Introduction

If you need to correct a Form 1094-C record or any Form 1095-C records after you already made an accepted submission to the IRS, you need to submit a correction file. This file only includes the employees whose information changed.

For detailed information about submitting corrections to the IRS, please see Section 7 of the Guide for Electronically Filing Affordable Care Act Information Returns for…Transmitters.

State Reporting

For details about PrismHR’s support for state reporting, please see the introductory material under Creating the ACA Transmission File.

Note About File Sizes

According to IRS guidelines, the transmission file must be smaller than 100 MB. However, there is no consistent rule about the number of records that will cause a file to exceed 100 MB. Internal testing shows that it involves many thousands of employees. Please check the size of the output file after you create it. If it exceeds the limit, use the From Employee and To Employee fields as described below to specify a smaller range of employees instead.

Procedure

WARNING: Before running this process, ensure that there are no users accessing any 1094-C or 1095-C records in the system. If anyone is editing those records, the system will hang.

Before starting the correction process, ensure that you already have the Receipt ID for the transmission you need to correct.

Please only use the Jurat Signature PIN, Person Title Text, and Signature DT if you know precisely what should go in those fields. If you enter the wrong information, or the information is in the wrong format, the IRS will reject the submission.

To submit an ACA correction file:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu.

  2. Under Work Centers|Compliance, select ACA Processing.

  3. Under ACA Processes, select Edit 1094 Form.

  4. Under the Federal Transmission Info tab, select Send As Correction.

  5. Click Save.

  6. For each employee that you need to correct:

  • Go to the employee Form 1095-C edit form (Editing Form 1095-C Information).

  • Enter the Employee ID.

  • Select Send As Correction.

  • Click Save.

  1. Log in to PrismHR, click the Reports menu [Image: Reports menu icon] , and navigate to the HR|Report > ACA Transmission File form.8. Enter the Year.

  2. In certain situations, you may need to report multiple clients as one single company. In this case, select the IDs of any additional clients you want to include in the file (See ACA Transmittal Tips & Tricks).

  3. Use the From Employee and To Employee fields only if this is a large client and you need to create multiple smaller files. The IRS requires that the XML file be less than 100 MB in size. for detailed instructions.

  4. In the State Code field, select Federal or California depending on which transmission file you want to create.

  5. Set Submission Type to Correction.

  6. If you are submitting a Federal correction, select whether the Correction Type is for the 1094 or 1095.

  7. Enter the Original Receipt ID from the IRS.

  8. Click Create File.

If there are many hundreds or thousands of records, this can take a few minutes; the system might time out without warning you. Please contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team if you experience an issue.

Checking the Generated File

The system creates a ZIP archive containing two files: the form file and the manifest file. The manifest file includes the unique transmission ID for the client company, and the system populates that ID in the Par 1 panel of the Client 1094 record.

If you unzip the aca.zip file, you will see two XML files with similar names, as in the example below. The manifest file has the prefix Manifest_, and the other file contains the Form 1094-C and Form 1095-C information. Make sure that the files are smaller than 100 MB. If they are not, you must perform the procedure again and follow the instructions for Editing Form 1094-C Information. Also, refer to About 1095 and 1094 Registers for more information.

Note: Note: The two files you ultimately submit must have the same time stamp. Check the Date modified column in your file explorer, as shown below.

[Image: Example of IRS transmission and manifest files in the Windows file explorer.]

see Reviewing the ACA Transmission File XML Output for error-checking suggestions. When you are satisfied with the contents of the files, you can submit them to the IRS (see AIR Transmission: Uploading the ACA Files or the appropriate state reporting entity. Remember: as soon as you receive your receipt ID from your reporting entity, enter it into the system (see Entering the IRS Receipt ID).

========================================================================

Creating the ACA Transmission File

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Create_Transmission.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Create_Transmission.htm

Creating the ACA Transmission File

Note: Note: This form supports entity-level security for Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors, as defined on the Users > Actions > Data Security form.

If you want to create a Correction or Replacement file, please see See Submitting a Correction or See Submitting a Replacement

Introduction

When you are ready to finalize your submission, you can create and submit your official (“Original”) transmission file. To review details about this process, refer to Section 4 of the IRS guide linked here.

California Reporting

As part of the state’s 2021 individual health care mandate, California requires self-insured employers to submit their reporting data to the California Franchise Tax Board. The specifications for this transmission file differ from those of the federal transmission file. If you need to create the California version of the file, select California in the State Code field.

New Jersey Reporting

If the ACA Third Party Download (see Creating the ACA Third Party Download/1095 Export) indicates that employees resided in New Jersey in the prior year, you can enable NJ State Submission to report only those residents, as specified by state requirements.

While the Third-Party Download includes a similar option for Washington, D.C., there is no D.C. equivalent to this setting on this form, because D.C. reporting requirements are different and do not require a filtered submission like New Jersey does.

Note About File Sizes

The transmission file must be smaller than 100 MB. However, there is no consistent rule about the number of records that will cause a file to exceed 100 MB. Internal testing shows that it involves many thousands of employees. Please check the size of the output file after you create it. If it exceeds the limit, use the From Employee and To Employee fields as described below to specify a range of employees instead.

Procedure

WARNING: Before running this process, ensure that there are no users accessing any 1094-C or 1095-C records in the system. If anyone is editing those records, the system will hang.

Note: Note: Please only use the Jurat Signature PIN, Person Title Text, and Signature DT fiels if you know precisely what should go in them. If you enter the wrong information, or the information is in the wrong format, the IRS will reject the submission.

To create the ACA transmission file:

  1. First, go to the Form 1094-C data screen (in PrismHR, go to Work Centers|Compliance > ACA Processing > ACA Processes > Edit 1094 Form). Ensure that the Year is correct and that the Test Scenario Code and Test Start Month fields are blank.

  2. Click Save.

  3. Next, go back to the ACA Transmission File form in PrismHR: go to Work Centers|Compliance > ACA Processing > ACA Processes > ACA Transmission File.

Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors can access this report through Reports > HR | Report >ACA Transmission File.

Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors can access this report through Reports > HR | Report > ACA Transmission File.

  1. Enter the Year: for example, 2026.

  2. In certain situations, you may need to report multiple clients as one single company. In this case, select the IDs of any additional clients you want to include in the file (See ACA Transmittal Tips & Tricks).

  3. Use the From Employee and To Employee fields only if this is a large client and you need to create multiple smaller files: the IRS requires that the XML file be less than 100 MB in size. for detailed instructions.

  4. In the State Code field, select Federal or California.

  5. Set Submission Type to Original.

  6. If appropriate, identify this transmission file as a NJ State Submission. See the note above.

  7. Click Create File.

If there are many hundreds or thousands of records, this can take a few minutes; the system might time out without warning you. Please contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team if you experience an issue.

Checking the Generated File

The system creates a ZIP archive containing two files: the form file and the manifest file. The manifest file includes the unique transmission ID for the client company, and the system populates that ID in the Edit 1094 Form > Part I tab.

If you unzip the aca.zip file, you will see two XML files with similar names, as in the example below. The manifest file has the prefix Manifest_ , and the other file contains the Form 1094-C and Form 1095-C information. Make sure that the files are smaller than 100 MB. If they are not, you must perform the procedure again and follow the instructions for . You can also for more information.

Note: Note: The two files you ultimately submit must have the same time stamp (check the Date modified column in your file explorer, as shown below).

[Image: Example of IRS transmission and manifest files in the Windows file explorer.]

See Reviewing the ACA Transmission File XML Output for error checking suggestions. When you are satisfied with the contents of the files, you can submit them to the IRS (see AIR Transmission: Uploading the ACA Files or the appropriate state reporting entity. Remember: as soon as you receive your receipt ID from your reporting entity, enter it into the system: see Entering the IRS Receipt ID.

Reviewing Transmission File History

On the Form 1094-C edit form, you can view a list of past transmission files for a specific employee or client, for both the Federal and California downloads.

Entering the IRS Receipt ID

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Enter_IRS_Receipt_ID.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Enter_IRS_Receipt_ID.htm

Entering the IRS Receipt ID

You must enter the receipt ID for each transmission once you receive it from the IRS.

Note: Note: The receipt ID is different from the submission ID. Make sure you enter the Receipt ID into the system.

  1. After you successfully submit your transmission, the IRS system displays the Transmission Confirmation page, including the Receipt ID and other information.

[Image: IRS transmission confirmation webpage]

  1. Open the Work Centers menu and select ACA Processing under Work Centers|Compliance.

  2. Select Edit 1094 Form under ACA Processes. Locate the panel labeled Federal Transmittal Info.

  3. Enter the corresponding Receipt ID provided by the IRS, whenever they supply it. You MUST perform this step after you receive the Receipt ID!

  4. Click Save.

Entering the California Receipt ID

If you submitted your transmission files to the State of California, you can use a state-specific table on the Form 1094-C screen to enter the receipt ID they gave you.

[Image: California state transmission details, listed in a panel on the Form 1094-C form in PrismHR]

To enter a receipt ID provided by California:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu and select ACA Processing under Work Centers|Compliance.

  2. Select Edit 1094 Form under ACA Processes. If a California transmission file exists, the form displays a panel labeled California Transmittal Info.

This panel includes a grid that lists basic metadata about the file, including the transmission ID and date and time of transmission.

  1. In the Receipt ID column, enter the receipt ID provided by the State of California.

  2. Click Save.

Viewing Employee-Level Information

You can also view transmission file data for California employees who were included in the build.

To view this data:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu and select ACA Processing under Work Centers|Compliance.

  2. Select Edit Employee 1095 Form under ACA Processes. If a California transmission file exists, the form displays a panel labeled California Transmittal Information.

  3. In the grid, review the relevant transmission file IDs and their corresponding record IDs.

Creating the Test Submission

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/IRS_Test_Submission.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/IRS_Test_Submission.htm

Creating the Test Submission

This process creates an XML file and populates the required fields for a test communication to the reporting body. Once the reporting body (for example, the IRS) informs you that your test submission was accepted, you do not need to use this procedure again.

The IRS provides various test scenarios, and each test requires the system to have invalid Social Security numbers embedded.

The system is configured to allow the SSN required for Tests 3-0 and 3-1. Please use Tests 3-0 and 3-1 for the approval process, as described here.

Note: Note: For further details, please see the Test Package for Electronic Filers of Affordable Care Act (ACA) Information Returns (AIR) here.

Overview

To set up data for the one-time IRS test transmission:

  1. Set up your test client.

  2. Set up your test employee.

  3. Update the client Form 1094 record.

  4. Update the employee Form 1095 record.

These steps are reviewed in detail below.

Submitting a Replacement

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Replacement_Transmission.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Replacement_Transmission.htm

Submitting a Replacement

If you want to create a Correction file, please see See Submitting a Correction

Introduction

You only need to submit a replacement when the IRS rejects a transmission or submission after it “gets through the door” to them. This process replaces the entire file.

For detailed information about replacement files, please see Section 7 of the Guide for Electronically Filing Affordable Care Act Information Returns for…Transmitters.

State Reporting

For details about support for state reporting, please see the introductory material under Creating the ACA Transmission File.

Note About File Sizes

The transmission file must be smaller than 100 MB. However, there is no consistent rule about the number of records that will cause a file to exceed 100 MB. Internal testing shows that it involves many thousands of employees. Please check the size of the output file after you create it. If it exceeds the limit, use the From Employee and To Employee fields as described below to specify a range of employees instead.

Procedure

Before you begin, ensure that you have the original receipt ID for the transmission that you need to replace. Note that the receipt ID is different from the submission ID. Make sure you use the receipt ID.

WARNING: Before running this process, ensure that there are no users accessing any 1094-C or 1095-C records in the system. If anyone is editing those records, the system will hang.

Note: Note: Please only use the Jurat Signature PIN, Person Title Text, and Signature DT if you know precisely what should go in those fields. If you enter the wrong information, or the information is in the wrong format, the IRS will reject the submission.

To submit an ACA replacement file:

  1. Log in to PrismHR, select the Reports menu and navigate to HR|Report > ACA Transmission File.

  2. Enter the appropriate Year.

  3. In certain situations, you may need to report multiple clients as one single company. In this case, select the IDs of any additional clients you want to include in the file (See ACA Transmittal Tips & Tricks).

  4. Use the From Employee and To Employee fields only if this is a large client and you need to create multiple smaller files: the IRS requires that the XML file be less than 100 MB in size. for detailed instructions.

If the file size does not exceed 100 MB, continue.

  1. In the State Code field, select Federal or California depending on which transmission file you want to create.

  2. Set Submission Type to Replacement.

  3. Select the Replacement Type, based on the instructions from the IRS:

  • Replacing Transmission: replacing the entire file to resolve a transmission error.

  • Replacing Submission: replacing the entire file to resolve a submission error.

  1. Enter the Original Receipt ID that you received from the IRS or the corresponding reporting entity.

  2. Click Create File.

If there are many hundreds or thousands of records, this can take a few minutes; the system might time out without warning you. Please contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team if you experience an issue.

Checking the Generated File

The system creates a ZIP archive containing two files: the form file and the manifest file. The manifest file includes the unique transmission ID for the client company, and the system populates that ID in the Par 1 panel of the Client 1094 record.

If you unzip the aca.zip file, you will see two XML files with similar names, as in the example below. The manifest file has the prefix Manifest_, and the other file contains the Form 1094-C and Form 1095-C information. Make sure that the files are smaller than 100 MB. If they are not, you must perform the procedure again and follow the instructions for . You can also for more information.

Note: Note: The two files you ultimately submit must have the same time stamp (check the Date modified column in your file explorer, as shown below).

[Image: Example of IRS transmission and manifest files in the Windows file explorer.]

see Reviewing the ACA Transmission File XML Output for error checking suggestions. When you are satisfied with the contents of the files, you can submit them to the IRS (see AIR Transmission: Uploading the ACA Files or the appropriate state reporting entity. Remember: as soon as you receive your receipt ID from your reporting entity, enter it into the system: see Entering the IRS Receipt ID.

Reviewing the ACA Transmission File XML Output

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Reviewing_XML_Output.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/Reviewing_XML_Output.htm

Reviewing the ACA Transmission File XML Output

Open the XML output file in a text editor, like Notepad, and search for the words “Issue”, “Omit”, and “Null”.

  • If you find “Issue” then there is a data discrepancy on the client side.

  • If you find “Omit” or “Null” then you need to contact PrismHR Customer Support for assistance.

Checking that the File Size is less than 100 MB

The IRS requires that files be smaller than 100 MB. The size of a file is dictated by the number of characters in it, so file size varies based on many different factors.

There is no hard and fast rule about the number of records that will cause a file to exceed 100 MB. Internal testing shows that it involves many thousands of employees. You must check the size of the output file to ensure that it is the correct size.

If the file is larger than 100 MB, use data from an employee report to complete the From Employee and To Employee fields. See

Using the From Employee and To Employee Fields

Use the From Employee and To Employee fields in the ACA Transmission Form only if the output files you created for this client were larger than 100 MB. With these fields, you can specify a range of employee IDs to include in the file. Using some simple math, you can calculate the approximate number of employees you can include on the file based on the size of the original output file, and then use the report output below to adjust your range appropriately.

To use the From Employee and To Employee fields:

Step 1: Run the Employee Report, Sorted by ID

  1. Select the Employee Name report and set the Primary Sort to Employee ID.

  2. Click Run.

Step 2: Use this Information In the Submission Process

  1. In the Employee From and Employee To fields, enter the range of employees that will reduce the size of the output file to meet the IRS requirements.

For example, if you generated a file for all of the client’s employees and the output file was 150 MB, you can estimate that if you include the first half of the employees from the report in first file and the second half of the employees in the second file, then each of the two output files will be less than 100 MB.

  1. Proceed with the remaining steps in the process, then check the size of the output file. If any file exceeds the limit, begin the procedure again and use a more limited range of employees.

Transmitting ACA Data to the IRS

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/UG_Chapter_Transmit_to_IRS.htm Path: CMP/ACA/ACA-XML-Transmission/UG_Chapter_Transmit_to_IRS.htm

Transmitting ACA Data to the IRS

The PrismHR system can electronically transmit client ACA returns to the IRS. This process is called E-filing.

Note: Note: If you want to electronically transmit your Forms 1094-C and 1095-C, you must register in the ACA Information Returns (AIR). You can register with the IRS at this link.

These procedures are required for each 1094-C client account that you need to submit:

Step Description Reference
1 After registering with the IRS, enter your TCC in the system. See Setting Up ACA Transmitter Control Information.
2 Create and send the test files. See Creating the Test Submission.
3 Once the IRS approves the test submission for the client, create and submit the transmission files. See Creating the ACA Transmission File.
4 Submit the files to the IRS using their AIR UI Channel. See AIR Transmission: Uploading the ACA Files
5 After submitting the files, you will receive a receipt ID from the IRS that you need to track. See Entering the IRS Receipt ID.

In some circumstances, you might need to correct or replace information:

  • Corrections: If you need to make adjustments to a client or employee after the IRS accepted the transmission, you can send a correction. See Submitting a Correction.

  • Replacements: If the IRS rejected a transmission or submission, you must send a replacement. See Submitting a Replacement.

If you encounter problems, see ACA Transmittal Tips & Tricks for suggestions.
