About the Affordable Care Act

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/Background/About_ACA.htm Path: CMP/ACA/Background/About_ACA.htm

About the Affordable Care Act

The Affordable Care Act (ACA) is a comprehensive health care reform law enacted in March 2010. The law was enacted to make affordable health insurance available to more people. It provides consumers with subsidies in the form of premium tax credits. These tax credits lower costs for households with incomes between 100% and 400% of the federal poverty level.

The Affordable Care Act also expanded the Medicaid program to cover all adults with incomes below 138% of the federal poverty level. However, not all states have expanded their Medicaid programs.

The Affordable Care Act requires employers to offer health insurance to their full-time employees if they have at least fifty full-time equivalents . According to the act, employers must complete Internal Revenue Service (IRS) Form 1095-C for any employees who qualify as full time and send this form to them. Once per reporting year, employers must also send one Form 1094-C and all employee Forms 1095-C to the IRS.

Employers must submit the information to the IRS by the end of February if filing by paper, or by the end of March if filing electronically. To review the official IRS documentation, see the online Instructions for Forms 1094-C and 1095-C.

========================================================================

About Year-End Processing: IRS Forms

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/Background/About_YE_IRS_Forms.htm Path: CMP/ACA/Background/About_YE_IRS_Forms.htm

About Year-End Processing: IRS Forms

ACA-related year-end processing involves working with IRS Forms 1094-C and 1095-C.

Background

Form 1095-C indicates, for each month of the reporting year:

  • Coverage offered to an employee

  • Lowest-cost premium available

  • Safe harbor coding

  • Plan enrollment for self-insured plans

Form 1094-C contains employer information (“client” information in PrismHR). This includes:

  • Contact information

  • Number of employees

  • Percentage of employees offered appropriate coverage

  • Transitional relief or methods used

  • Other employers part of the same large employer group

Supported Outputs

Note: Note: Because the Form 1095-C PDF uses employer information from Form 1094-C, you must fill in this information in the client 1094‑C record before you can print any 1095-Cs.

The system builds ACA data and ACA reports on a client-by-client basis. If you configure your settings correctly, you can also account for employees who worked for multiple clients within the same group. In this case, you must identify the appropriate employees and then manually adjust their coding – for one or more clients.

At this time we do not support Forms 1095-B and 1094-B.

The system produces the following ACA transmission outputs:

  • Printable Form 1094-C and Form 1095-C PDFs, for each client

  • Billing Document Specialists (BDS) and CIC Plus (CIC) download files to submit to third-party processing companies

  • Employee Portal (EP) PDF downloads – PrismHR only

  • ACA Information Return (AIR) file

The ACA Information Return File

After you finish building your ACA data, you can create the ACA Information Return (AIR) file, which enables you to e-File your ACA information to the IRS.

This file includes:

  • A checksum in the SHA256 format.

  • A Prior Year indicator. This tells the IRS if you are filing for a reporting year prior to the most recent one. It is set to No unless you run it for a year two or more years before the year in which you build the AIR file. For example, if you run the report in 2021 for 2020, it will be No. If you run it for 2019, it will be Yes.

A General Outline of ACA Processing

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/Background/ACA_Proc_General_Outline.htm Path: CMP/ACA/Background/ACA_Proc_General_Outline.htm

A General Outline of ACA Processing

This chapter provides a step-by-step general description of how to set your system up for ACA processing, from configuring system parameters to transmitting your data. If you need concrete procedures for completing these tasks, please skip to the next chapters. However, if you need a more general overview of the major steps in the process, review this chapter.

Step 1: Ensure Proper Benefit Setup

Note: Note: If you need to refresh your knowledge of benefits administration concepts, you can review the Benefits Administration User Guide.

Before setting up ACA features in PrismHR, review and adjust your global and client benefit plan settings. Proper configuration will provide a smoother experience with our ACA reporting features.

For detailed procedures, see Setting Up ACA Benefits Eligibility Rules.

System-Level Group Benefit Plan Setup

Set up the following benefit parameters on the Group Benefit Plans form (from the Back Office menu under System|Change):

Parameter — Action

Insurance Class — Ensure medical plans are set to Medical. Exclude non-medical plans flagged as Medical from ACA reporting (select Do NOT Include in ACA Reporting).

Plan Types — This panel requires one employee-only plan type.

Bill Rates — You must set these up for an employee-only plan type.

Review and set the seven ACA parameters in the Special Options panel:

Parameter Settings
Does NOT Meet ACA Minimum Essential Coverage Select or deselect.
Does NOT Meet ACA Minimum Value Requirements Select or deselect.
Do NOT Include In ACA Reporting Select or deselect.
Self-Insured Plan Select or deselect.
For ACA Reporting, Plan Covers Employee? Select or deselect.
For ACA Reporting, Plan Covers Spouse? Yes, No, or Conditional
For ACA Reporting, Plan Covers Other Dependents? Select or deselect.

Client-Level Benefit Plan Setup

You might need to adjust client-level benefit plans and benefit rules.

When you adjust client benefit plans:

Set the following parameters on the Client > Change > Benefit Plan Setup form:

  • The appropriate Status, Start Date, and Stop Date for each medical plan.

  • The Coverage Ends On field in Group Benefits Setup.

When you adjust client benefit rules:

Set the following parameters on the Client > Change > Benefit Rules form:

  • The Contribution Rules for each plan, for each effective date.

  • The Eligibility Rules for each plan for each effective date: employee status, type, and service rules.

  • For any plan with networks, set an eligibility rule for a plan without entering a network ID.

Other Settings

Remember to adjust these miscellaneous settings as necessary:

  • If a benefit plan contains multiple employee-only plan types, use one of the workarounds to ensure that the correct codes are reported. See Configuring Group Benefit Plan Settings for the ACA.

  • If employees are eligible for multiple networks within a single plan, you might need to configure a “dummy plan” for the client without networks.

  • Otherwise, configure all plans appropriately. These configurations include but are not limited to:

  • Cafeteria plan rules

  • Networks

  • Risk factors

  • Employee overrides

  • State-based offerings

  • Alternate contribution plans

Step 2: Adjust System and Client Parameters

After you review and adjust your benefit plan setup, you can set global and client-specific ACA parameters. Your configuration can vary based on your organizational needs. Contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team for help.

The following procedures relate to this topic:

  • Configuring Global ACA Parameters

  • Configuring Client ACA Parameters

Step 3: Configure Other Client-Level Settings

Set up the following client-level parameters as necessary:

Setting Action Related Procedures
ACA Offered Employees Register (Offered Employee Maintenance) Add any employee who has a part-time employment type but needs to be considered full-time for ACA reporting purposes. Setting Up ACA Offered Employees
ACA Type Overrides If you want to treat any full-time employee as a part-time employee for ACA reporting purposes, add them here. Include the appropriate override start and end dates. Setting Up ACA Type Overrides
Form 1094-C Data The information in Part 1 is especially important because it populates on Form 1095-C for every employee. You do not need to complete parts 2, 3, and 4 with total accuracy until you finalize the employee 1095-C registers for the year. Once finalized, review these parts to ensure that they are accurate.
ACA Large Employer option, in Client Details Enable this for each client, for each year subject to ACA reporting. For details about this process, review the section called About the Large Employer Test Report.

Step 4: Start Building Data in Background

After setting up the appropriate parameters, you can initiate ACA data builds.

This procedure is documented under Activating the ACA Data Build.

At the start of the next month after you enable the ACA data build feature code, the system automatically builds ACA data for any previous months in the reporting year. Users cannot see or access the ACA data, but the register build process uses it to generate the reporting files and forms.

If your organization made any system changes that, you think, unintentionally impacted your ACA data, then you need to rebuild it. The system will not rebuild those months as a background process. For assistance with these issues, contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

Step 5: Process ACA Data

When you are ready to begin year-end reporting, use these and related procedures to review your ACA data, finalize it, and prepare it for transmission to the IRS and employees.

Step # Description Notes Related Procedures
1 Rebuild ACA Data, if required If you made adjustments to setup, benefit plans, employees, or anything else in any way that could impact the 1095-C registers, you need to rebuild the ACA data. Rebuilding ACA Data
2 Build ACA 1095-C Registers The system populates 1095-C registers using ACA data, so you must build that data first. The register build process automatically populates the Employee Form 1095-C Registers. It also clears prior data from the registers. After this initial build, you can rebuild the 1095-C registers as many times as needed. The results will not change, however, unless the underlying ACA data changes due to a rebuild or background aggregation of another month of data. Building ACA 1095 Registers
3 Run the ACA Data Status Report After January 1, check whether all the ACA data is complete for the reporting year. Once you know it is complete, you can run a register build for each client for the year. This provides employee Form 1095-C codes for the entire year. Running the ACA Data Status Report
4 Export the 1095-C Registers This feature outputs a report of all 1095-C registers in an export/import-compliant format. Use this output file to review a summary of all 1095-C register data, quickly make changes to it, and import those changes back into the system. Important! Do not complete ACA register rebuilds when making manual changes to the ACA data (either during imports or updating directly in the 1095C form). Otherwise, the system removes all manual changes. Exporting 1095 Registers

Step 6: Adjust 1095-C Registers

Review the following procedures for details:

  • Editing Form 1095-C Information

  • Importing Form 1095-C Data

Manually Adjusting Employee Form 1095-C Registers

If needed, you can manually adjust or create any register entries. Data from the registers populates on the Form 1095-C PDFs, the third-party download file, and the IRS transmission form.

Importing 1095-C Data

You can also import ACA data for an employee’s Form 1095-C:

  • Go to Work Centers > ACA Processing > ACA Processes > Import/Export > 1095C Data Import.

  • Any month imported for any employee will overwrite that month’s data only.

  • Existing covered individuals will be overwritten only if there is an exact name match. If the name does not match precisely, the system creates a new entry on the register.

  • Your organization can use the import in place of the register build, or in conjunction with it.

Step 7: Handle Mid-Year Client Considerations

Review the following common issues and apply the suggested workarounds if they arise. If you need additional help, do not hesitate to contact support.

For more troubleshooting-related topics, please see Common Questions about Forms 1094‑C and 1095‑C and, more generally, the section on Additional Information about Building ACA Forms.

Issue 1: Employee is not in the system but requires a 1095-C

Issue: You cannot create a 1095-C register record for former employees that are not in the system, either manually, by register build, or by import.

Solution: Create a terminated take-on employee record. Access the Terminated Take-On Employee Entry form (located in the HR menu under HR|Action. Complete this form.

Issue 2: No Offer Codes or Employee Share

Issue: For the months prior to the client start date with your service provider, there are no offer codes or employee share data. This is because there is no benefit plan or eligibility data.

Solution: Before the ACA data build, set up one or more benefit plans for the client with the appropriate bill rates, eligibility rules, and so on, to reflect the offerings in those previous months.

Issue 3: 2C Safe Harbor Coding/Enrollment Data Not Working Correctly

Issue: For months prior to the client start date with your service provider, there will be no employee enrollment data. As a result, the 2C safe harbor code will not populate appropriately for those months. For self-insured plans, covered individuals’ coverage will also not be reflected accurately.

Solution:

  1. Build the ACA 1095-C registers.

  2. After building the registers, use the Export 1095-C Register Data feature to export the months prior to the client start date.

  3. Adjust any safe harbor code to 2C (other than 2A or anywhere 1G applies) for months each employee was enrolled in coverage.

  4. Adjust covered individual entries as well.

  5. Import the file.

Issue 4: 2F Safe Harbor Coding/W-2 Wages Safe Harbor not Generating

Issue: For the entire year (including after the client start date with your service provider), 2F might not generate in situations it otherwise should if the W-2 wage data does not reflect the total W-2 wages for the year. Blank entries would generate in its place.

Solution:

  1. Build the 1095-C registers.

  2. After building the registers, use the Export 1095-C Register Data feature to export all months.

  3. Review for blank safe harbor code entries.

  4. For employees where blank entries exist, determine the full-year W-2 data and whether it would satisfy the 2F safe harbor.

  5. Adjust the file and import it.

Step 8: Processing the AIR Transmission File

When ready, you can use PrismHR to set up and process the transmission file for the Affordable Care Act Information Returns (AIR) Program.

Note: Note: If you submit this file to the state of New Jersey, you can select the NJ State Submission option before you create the file. When you select this option, the system includes only employees who lived in New Jersey at some point during the prior year, in accordance with state reporting requirements.

For detailed procedures, see the chapter: Transmitting ACA Data to the IRS.

Important Notes

Please note that:

  • PrismHR creates these files for the specified client.

  • You must be registered with the IRS to submit files.

  • The Additional Clients selection list is not for submitting multiple clients at once. It is only for adding other client 1095-C records under a single client 1094-C record. For example, you have a single company, ABC, with a single FEIN, split up as company 100, company 101, and company 102.

How the System Builds ACA Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/ACA/Background/How-the-System-Builds-ACA-Data.htm Path: CMP/ACA/Background/How-the-System-Builds-ACA-Data.htm

How the System Builds ACA Data

The system builds ACA data using specific rules outlined here.

Determining Employee Eligibility

The system checks for the lowest-cost single plan for which an employee qualifies.

Note: Note: The cost is based on the size of the employee contribution. Therefore, set this information up carefully.

  1. First, it checks the benefit rules. Employees can qualify for benefit rules based on their status, type, and seniority:

  • Status indicates whether the employee is active, on leave, or terminated.

  • Type indicates whether the employee is full-time, part-time, or some other designation.

  • Seniority is based on either the employee hire date or seniority date, depending on your settings.

  1. If the employee does not qualify under any of those benefit rules, and is included in Offered Employees Maintenance (see Setting Up ACA Offered Employees), the system then checks the ACA Benefits Eligibility Rules (see Setting Up ACA Benefits Eligibility Rules).

  2. If employees do not qualify based on either of those sets of rules, the system checks the optional grid. It reviews the grid line-by-line until it reaches a plan for which the employee qualifies. We recommend using the optional grid only if you do not maintain benefit plans and benefit rules in the system and you have no other option.

Determining Offer Codes and Employee Shares

Form 1095-C provides spaces for a monthly breakdown of employee healthcare coverage offers and contributions. The system uses specialized logic to determine which offer code (line 14) and contribution amount (line 15) to populate in these spaces.

For the purposes of the data build process, an offer of coverage – as defined by the ACA rules – has been made if an employee is eligible for at least one qualifying benefit plan.

Note: Note: Ensure that you have already set up the appropriate system-level billing rates for the relevant benefit plans.

For more information about offer code and safe harbor code calculation logic, see the reference flowchart under Additional Information about Building ACA Forms.

The system reviews data in the following locations:

Category Associated Form/Data Reviewed
Employee details HR > Change > Employee Details Employee benefit group, status, type, hire date, status date, and ZIP code. > Note: Note: ZIP code data is used for any location that has regional plans. Please note any employee ZIP code changes during the year, as this information is used in ACA data builds.
Client-level benefit eligibility rules Client > Change > Benefit Rules and Work Centers > Compliance > ACA Processing > Benefit Eligibility Rules (ACA) Each benefit eligibility rule must define specific employee statuses, types, and wait periods that qualify under the ACA. The system also notices any employee-level overrides for ACA-related features. For more details about client benefit eligibility rules, please see Setting Up ACA Benefits Eligibility Rules.
Client-level contribution rules System > Change > Group Benefit Plans For plans where the employee is eligible, the system reviews the associated employee-only plan type to obtain the lowest employee contribution amount.
Client benefits setup Client > Change > Benefit Plan Setup The system looks for client-level benefit plan data during this process. For best results, ensure that: - The appropriate start and stop dates are set up for each plan. - Employee statuses are associated correctly on each plan. - The Coverage Ends On field is correct – this field impacts safe harbor coding.
  • The appropriate start and stop dates are set up for each plan.
  • Employee statuses are associated correctly on each plan.
  • The Coverage Ends On field is correct – this field impacts safe harbor coding. | System-level group benefit plan setup | System > Change > Group Benefit Plans/Group Benefit Plan Types The process checks the system-level setup of each plan for which the employee is eligible. It determines whether it is a medical plan, is self-insured, meets the ACA minimum value and minimum coverage requirements, and is offered to employees, spouses, and/or dependents (see image below for the associated settings). Plans can also be conditionally offered to spouses (defined by 1J and 1K coding). You can configure this in the Special Options panel of the Group Benefit Plans form. | [Image: Part of the special options panel of Group Benefit Plans form. The following fields are highlighted: Self Insured Plan, Does NOT Meet ACA Minimum Essential Coverage, Does NOT Meet ACA Minimum Value Requirements, Plan Covers Spouse for ACA Reporting, Plan Covers Employee for ACA Reporting, and Plan Covers Other Dependents for ACA Reporting.]

Offer Code Selection Logic

Note: Note: For detailed descriptions of each code, please see the IRS instructions for Form 1095-C. You can also review the status code logic flowchart under Additional Information about Building ACA Forms.

To populate offer codes, the system determines the appropriate “Code Table 1” code for each eligible plan, for each month. It populates the code in this priority order (where 1A is highest priority):

1A > 1E > 1K > 1C > 1D > 1J > 1F > 1H

The other two offer codes follow specific rules:

Offer Code Rule
1H (no coverage offered) Populates: - When an employee is inactive for an entire month. - In the month of termination if coverage would not have continued through the end of that month (as determined by the Coverage Ends On field in client-level benefit setup).
  • When an employee is inactive for an entire month.
  • In the month of termination if coverage would not have continued through the end of that month (as determined by the Coverage Ends On field in client-level benefit setup). | 1G | Populates only when all 12 months of data are complete for any employee who was: - Not full-time during any part of the year. - Enrolled in self-insured coverage at any time during the year. |
  • Not full-time during any part of the year.
  • Enrolled in self-insured coverage at any time during the year. ## Employee Share Calculation Logic

For each month, the system compares the cost of the employee-only plan type of all eligible plans for the employee. It then selects the lowest value and populates it in the ACA register.

An employee share only populates on Form 1095-C when line 14 contains one of the following offer codes: 1B, 1C, 1D, 1E, 1J, or 1K.

Determining Safe Harbor Codes

Safe harbor codes populate on line 16 of Form 1095-C (“Section 4980H Safe Harbor Codes and Other Relief for ALE Members”). The safe harbor code logic is complex and based on many factors. First, the system checks the applicability of IRS non-safe harbor codes in the following order: 2A > 2C > 2D > 2B. Then, it follows the order 2F > 2G > 2H, based on the safe harbor method enabled in the system- or client-level ACA parameters. For details, see Configuring Global ACA Parameters and Configuring Client ACA Parameters.

Important Notes About Safe Harbor Determination

The safe harbor column clears at year-end for any employees who have offer code 1G.

If you have set client-level plans to terminate at the end of the month (rather than when the employee is terminated) normal safe harbor coding applies in the month of termination. If the plan terminates upon employee termination, code 2B populates.

System Limitations

The IRS states that if the Minimum Essential Coverage Indicator is No for any month, then codes 2F, 2G, or 2H should not be used for those months. To avoid any situation where the value is set incorrectly to No, we did not create any logic for this use case.

If you want the Minimum Essential Coverage Indicator to be “No” for any months:

  1. Use the Export 1095 Registers tool. See Exporting 1095 Registers.

  2. Limit the results to only the months in question with safe harbor codes 2F, 2G, or 2H.

  3. When you generate the file, delete those safe harbor codes from the file. Leave the entries blank.

  4. Import the file back into the system.

The Indexed 9.5%

According to the IRS, the 9.5% standard for affordability testing is actually an indexed percentage and follows the plan year. The system allows your organization to enter the appropriate indexed percentages at the system- and client-level. The percentage you enter follows the plan year. For example, the system uses the 2019 amount until the benefit plan year start date, and the 2020 amount for the remainder of the year.

Form W-2 Wages Safe Harbor Logic (Code 2F)

To evaluate this safe harbor code, the system:

  1. Calculates employee Form W-2 wages for the entire calendar year.

  2. Divides the wages by the months the employee was employed during the calendar year.

  3. Multiplies the resulting value by the indexed percentage (see the section above).

  4. Compares this value to the employee share of any month during which the employee was offered coverage.

Federal Poverty Level Safe Harbor Logic (Code 2G)

Like the 9.5% indexed percentage, the system value for the federal poverty level also follows the plan year. This means that it would use the 2019 value until the benefit plan year start date, and would use the 2020 amount for the remainder of the year.

The IRS provides flexibility on the federal poverty value you can use for a given year. Federal poverty levels are released at the end of every January and must be adopted within six months (mid-July). For this reason, your organization can indicate the appropriate federal poverty level for each plan year on a client-by-client basis, using the client-level ACA Parameters. If you do not enter anything at the client level, the system-level values apply.

When applying the safe harbor, the system:

  1. Multiplies the applicable federal poverty level by the indexed percentage indicated.

  2. Divides this value by 12.

  3. Compares it to the employee share for the month.

Rate-of-Pay Safe Harbor Logic (Code 2H)

Per federal regulations, the system rate-of-pay logic also follows the plan years.

  • For employees who are not salaried, the system uses either the hourly rates at the start of the plan year OR the rate during the month tested (whichever is less). That rate is multiplied by 130.

  • For salaried employees, the system uses the monthly salary at the start of the plan year. If the employee salary is reduced during the year, the system does not apply the safe harbor for the entire plan year.

When applying the safe harbor, the applicable rate of pay is multiplied by the indexed percentage indicated and compared to the employee share for the month, as it does for codes 2F and 2G.

  1. Use the Covered Dependents panel to specify the months in which certain individuals counted as covered dependents of this employee.

Manually Creating Client-level COBRA Subsidy Credits

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/2021/Cobra_Credit_Entry_Prism.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/2021/Cobra_Credit_Entry_Prism.htm

Manually Creating Client-level COBRA Subsidy Credits

You can use the COBRA Premium Credit Entry form to create new COBRA benefit cost credits for employees, in compliance with the provisions of the American Rescue Plan Act (ARPA).

When you save a new COBRA credit, the system creates a Tax Adjustment voucher for tax deduction code 00-19, which tracks the accumulation of COBRA credits. If you select Credit Client? the system also creates a client bill pending record for the credit, for refund purposes.

Note: Note: You can configure creditable clients on the Cobra Credit Parameters form, available from the Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

on System Parameters.

To create a new COBRA credit:

  1. To access the form, select the Client menu and go to Client|Change > Client Details.

  2. Open the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select Cobra Credit Entry.

  3. Click New Adjustment.

  4. Select the Employee to associate with this credit.

  5. Enter the Credit Date.

  6. Select the Employer to associate with this credit.

  7. Enter the COBRA Credit Amount as a positive value. This amount will accumulate as a negative value under tax code 00-19. If you enter a negative value in this field, a positive entry will accumulate under 00-19 and on the bill pending entry.

  8. Select Credit Client? if you want to create a Bill Pending entry for this client.

  9. If you selected Credit Client, you can also enter a Credit Billing Date to associate with the newly created client bill pending entry.

  10. Click Save. The system creates the new COBRA credit under the specified employee and employer.

Introduction and Background

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/Background-Content/COVID_941_Intro.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/Background-Content/COVID_941_Intro.htm

Introduction and Background

The changes implemented under the Families First and CARES Acts significantly altered the tax reporting structure of the 2020 Form 941. In the weeks and months following these acts, PrismHR began planning features to track the associated wage and tax credits so they can populate on employers’ quarterly Forms 941.

The draft version of Form 941 also evolved over this same period as its instructions became clearer and more precise. New features in PrismHR, developed according to these guidelines, can help service providers pass this new tax information into MasterTax and ensure that their 941 calculations comply with the new legislation. These features include:

  • Master Tax RTS File v. 39, which introduced file updates directly related to COVID-19 data transfer between PrismHR and MasterTax.

  • A Form 941 Worksheet report, designed to help employers fill out their tax forms in accordance with IRS calculations.

  • A Schedule R report, designed to help employers detail their client by client tax data.

You can find documentation for these features in the following sections.

========================================================================

Background

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/Background-Content/COVID_Background.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/Background-Content/COVID_Background.htm

Background

Coronavirus Disease 2019 (COVID-19) is a 2019-2021 pandemic caused by the virus SARS-CoV-2. In March of 2020, the United States government began passing legislation to help mitigate the economic effects of the viral outbreak. From March onward, PrismHR began introducing features to help service providers comply with these new legislative requirements.

Note: Note: The information in this section is included to help place the new COVID-19 features in their proper context. This section is not an exhaustive summary of these federal acts and their provisions.

Families First Coronavirus Response Act (FFCRA)

The first legislative initiative took effect on April 1, 2020 and applies to employers with fewer than 500 employees, with some exceptions. It establishes special expansions to family medical leave and paid sick leave for employees affected by the COVID-19 outbreak. These payments can all be rebated, up to certain limits, by the federal government. The PrismHR COVID-19 features include logic that helps determine how these rebate credits accrue.

PrismHR Features

Using the new, system-level COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters form, you can designate special pay codes and billing events to track COVID-related employee sick and medical leave. When you run a payroll batch that includes these pay codes, the system calculates the accrued (and remaining) rebate credits for each type of leave. It creates Client Bill Pending items associated with each type of rebate credit and, if any credits remain after the client rebate is applied, the system stores these credits in a carry-forward bill pending entry. The underlying logic can be adjusted at the system level. You can also view and adjust these bill pending entries in the system, if needed.

Please note the following characteristics of the new Families First feature set:

  • Creditable wages paid under the sick leave and FMLA pay codes accrue under the following tax deduction codes: 00-80 (FMLA) and 00-81 (Sick time, self-care and dependent-care). The system uses these codes to track tax credits associated with these wages.

  • Medicare taxes associated with these creditable wages accrue under tax deduction code 00-82. Employers are entitled to a credit on all employer-side medicare taxes associated with these creditable wages.

  • The system uses payroll warnings and hard stops to enforce wage cap limits, credit cap limits, and auto-rebate logic based on your configuration.

CARES Act

The CARES (Coronavirus Aid, Relief, and Economic Security) Act was also passed by the United States government in response to the 2019-2020 coronavirus pandemic. It provides financial support to both individuals and businesses affected by the pandemic. This support includes payments to taxpayers, expanded unemployment benefits, and payroll tax credits for businesses (see the section below).

Businesses can claim tax credit on 50% of employee wages paid between March 13 and December 31, 2020. There is a cap of $5,000 credit per employee (equivalent to $10,000 in wages paid between these dates).

Additional legislation at the beginning of 2021 updated these values to 70% of employee wages and $7000 credit per employee. This currently applies between 1/1/2021 and 7/1/2021.

Both acts also establish rebates associated with healthcare-related payments. The system records rebate credits under tax deduction code 00-83 for this piece of legislation.

Paycheck Protection Program

The CARES Act also implemented the Paycheck Protection Program (PPP), which provides 100% federally guaranteed loans, backed by the Small Business Administration, to small businesses with financial need. It provides an incentive for small businesses to retain their employees during coronavirus-influenced economic turbulence. Businesses can use the PPP to cover payroll and employee benefit costs (up to $100,000 in payroll expenses per employee), as well as mortgage interest payments, rents, and utility costs, to a certain extent.

From this loan program, small businesses can claim 2.5 times their 2019 monthly average payroll costs OR 10 million dollars (whichever is less). Seasonal employers calculate their average monthly costs using one of two lookback periods:

  • February 15, 2019 – June 30, 2019

  • March 1, 2019 – June 30, 2019

PPP Loan Forgiveness

To qualify for full PPP loan forgiveness, businesses must demonstrate that their full-time equivalency count did not decrease when compared to a provided lookback period. They must also indicate their use of loan funds for payroll and other costs.

August 8 Executive Order

On August 8, President Trump authorized “Memorandum on Deferring Payroll Tax Obligations in Light of the Ongoing COVID-19 Disaster,” which instructs the Treasury to provide guidance on employee deferral of Social Security taxes between September 1 and December 31, 2020. PrismHR implemented basic functionality to support this deferral. For more information, please see Deferring Employee Social Security Tax.

You can use Paycheck Protection Program Reports in PrismHR to determine your clients’ loan forgiveness details. One report breaks down your PPP-related payroll costs. Another displays a breakdown of your monthly full-time equivalents.

2021: Extensions to the CARES Act and the FFCRA

In late 2020 and early 2021, Presidents Trump and Biden authorized legislation extending the benefits of the CARES Act and the FFCRA, respectively. The first round of legislation emerged from the Bipartisan-Bicameral Omnibus COVID Relief Deal and extended the expiration of CARES Act credits until March 31, 2021.

The second round, passed in March 2021 as the American Rescue Plan Act of 2021, extended the expiration of FFCRA credits and expanded their original caps. It also implemented some extensions to the CARES Act’s Employee Retention Credit and introduced new benefits for affected workers, such as fully-subsidized COBRA coverage for employees who lost their insurance during the pandemic.

Response and Interpretation

PrismHR’s development approach was based around the following assumed requirements:

  • Reset COVID-19 wage, credit, and hour caps, such that on April 1 2021, any employee who previously used qualified sick leave would once again be able to claim the full amount of hours/wages allotted by the act.

  • Add support for a new employer Social Security tax credit.

  • Orient credit calculation toward charge dates. In other words, the prior legislation’s rules and limits would apply to sick leave taken on March 31, regardless of pay date. For example, if an employee took sick leave that was charged for March 31, but that employee was not paid until April 10th, they should accumulate sick leave credits under the old legislation because the charge date falls before April 1.

Important Setup Procedures

Use Case Suggestion Explanation
Required Fix Medicare Credit End Date You must set the Medicare Credit End Date field to 12/31/21 to ensure that 00-82 credits continue to accrue. Currently, its default value is 03/31/21.
Handling payrolls within the changeover period. Split payrolls based on whether charge dates fall before or after April 1, and strategically adjust the new start/stop dates to ensure proper credit calculation. These new features assume that the legislative changeover will emphasize charge dates, rather than pay dates, for credit calculation purposes. Recall that the new SS Credit Start Date field refers to pay dates, not charge dates. (The Medicare Credit End Date field also applies to pay dates, but is not relevant to this example). If you will be paying employees in a single batch with a pay period that extends between March and April, you may be able to perform the following steps: 1. Separate the payroll into two pay periods: one containing March charge dates and the other containing April charge dates. 2. Adjust the SS Credit Start Date to be sometime later in April. 3. Run the payroll for your March pay period. This will ensure that tax credits calculate correctly on March (pre-ARPA) charge dates. 4. Finally, reset the start and end dates to their appropriate values and run your April batch to begin compliance with the new legislation. This strategy is especially useful if your first April charge dates will fall significantly later than April 1, as it will provide more time for you to make the appropriate calculations and wrap up your pre-ARPA payrolls without worrying about the April 1 deadline.
  1. Separate the payroll into two pay periods: one containing March charge dates and the other containing April charge dates.
  2. Adjust the SS Credit Start Date to be sometime later in April.
  3. Run the payroll for your March pay period. This will ensure that tax credits calculate correctly on March (pre-ARPA) charge dates.
  4. Finally, reset the start and end dates to their appropriate values and run your April batch to begin compliance with the new legislation. Finally, we suggest that you use each pay code’s Conditional Rules to create a new custom payroll warning. Associate these warnings with both your old and new COVID-19 pay codes during the changeover period (see the example below). This can help raise the profile of these pay codes for payroll staff and serve as a reminder that the changeover is in progress.

[Image: Detail of Conditional Rules tab, showing the Payroll Warning field. The field contains the text “Enter your payroll warning here.”]

Before You Begin

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/Background-Content/COVID_BeforeBegin.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/Background-Content/COVID_BeforeBegin.htm

Before You Begin

Before configuring any COVID-19-specific settings, ensure that you have set up all the appropriate pay codes, billing codes, and billing events.

Note: Note: You do not need to set up any new PTO plans for these features. But if you want to do so, you can.

Background

In order to claim and properly account for the tax credits specified under the Families First/CARES Acts and the ARPA, you need to create spaces in the system to track each component of the payments and their credits:

  • Billing Event Codes: for tracking client-level credit rebates on invoices.

  • Billing Events: to trigger the recording of qualified wage and tax credits, for invoicing purposes.

  • Pay Codes: for paying any COVID-related wages that qualify for tax credits.

  • Accounting Settings (not addressed in this document).

After creating pay codes for the different types of COVID-19 qualified wages, you should also mark them as exempt from employer-side Social Security taxes (tax deduction code 00-16).

Setting Up Billing Codes for COVID-19-Related Leave

You must set up billing codes to track coronavirus-related credits. We recommend using the same setup as the standard tax billing code.

To set up a COVID-19 billing code:

  1. Open the Back Office menu.

  2. Select Billing Codes under System|Change.

  3. Enter a unique Bill Code ID, such as COVID19.

  4. If you want, enter a Bill Description.

  5. Save the record.

  6. Repeat for the second bill code.

Setting Up Billing Events for COVID-19-Related Leave

You must also set up billing events and associate them with the new billing codes. These events are assigned on the system-level COVID-19 parameters, described later.

To set up a COVID-19 billing event:

  1. Open the Back Office menu.

  2. Select Billing Events under System|Change.

  3. Enter a unique Bill Event Code, such as COVID19.

  4. If you want, enter a Description.

  5. Select the appropriate Bill Code to associate with this event.

  6. Save the record.

  7. Repeat for the second billing event.

Setting Up Pay Codes for COVID-19-Related Leave

You must set up pay codes to track COVID-19 “qualified” wages. If you have pay codes for state-mandated sick leave in your system, we recommend using those as a model. The procedure below provides general setup information for an example pay code.

To create pay codes specific to COVID-19 leave:

  1. Open the Back Office menu. Select Pay Codes under System|Change.

  2. On the Pay Codes form, enter a new Pay Code ID, such as COVIDSELF.

  3. Enter a Pay Description.

  4. Set the Pay Type to Regular.

  5. Set the Pay Class to Earnings.

  6. For Cash/Non-Cash, select Cash.

  7. Set the Calculation Method to Standard.

  8. Set the Pay Reason to Hours.

  9. For Subject to Work Comp, select Yes, This Pay Code is Subject to W/C.

  10. Enter the appropriate Factor. We recommend 0.67.

  11. For the Bill Code, enter 000 (Gross Wages).

  12. Save the record.

Repeat this procedure for all the other pay codes.

Exempting Pay Codes from Social Security Tax

You can configure tax exemptions on the Pay Codes form in PrismHR. Under the FFCRA, COVID-19 qualified wage pay codes needed to have a tax exemption for Social Security tax. Under the ARPA, however, this requirement goes away. You do not need to apply any tax exemptions to ARPA-related pay codes.

Important: This exemption still applies after you go over the wage limits. This can be highly problematic. Use extreme caution to avoid exceeding the mandated wage caps.

========================================================================

About MasterTax RTS 39 through 42

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/Background-Content/MasterTax_RTS_Updates.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/Background-Content/MasterTax_RTS_Updates.htm

About MasterTax RTS 39 through 42

To provide support for COVID-19-related changes to the 2020 Form 941, PrismHR developed support for versions 39 and 40 of the MasterTax RTS file. Version 39 of the file includes updates designed to streamline the passage of COVID-19-related data from PrismHR into MasterTax.

Support for MasterTax RTS version 40 was released in October 2020. Support for version 41 was initially released in March 2021 and expanded in May 2021. Support for version 42 was released in July 2021.

To enable version 42 of the RTS file:

  1. Click the Work Centers menu and select MasterTax Processing under Work Centers|Tax. Then, under Setup Forms, select MasterTax Interface Parameters.

  2. Set MasterTax RTS Import File Version to 42.

  3. If you are a Certified PEO or you leverage the PrismHR SBEA features, select Schedule R Breakout.

  4. Click Save.

Important Changes in Version 42

  • The file no longer includes 00-87 data.

  • The file now includes 00-19 data as a positive value, instead of a negative value.

  • For each 00-19 value in a 500 record, the system creates a corresponding 501 record. Statistic 4 of this record reflects the number of employees who contributed to the 00-19 value in the 500 record.

  • The file now splits 00-80 and 00-81 data into “Accrual Before April 1” and “Accrual After March 31” categories. Accrual Before April 1 reports under Statistic 2 of the 501 record while Accrual After March 31 reports under Statistic 1.

  • The file splits 00-84 data similarly. It now reflects pre-April 1 values in statistic 5 of the 501 record . The 501 record displays under code FE0000-116H for sick leave health care expenses, and under FE0000-117H for family leave health care expenses. The file reports these values employee by employee.

Important Changes in Version 41

  • The gross taxable over limit value for New Jersey unemployment tax (31-24) is now reported in statistic 4.

  • The system creates 600 and 601 records for any employee who paid Eugene, Oregon local tax for the quarter.

  • The system creates records for any employee who paid taxes to support Connecticut Paid Family Leave.

Important Changes in Version 40

This version of the RTS file includes several minor updates. The most important change is the addition of a new Statistic, which adds support for year-to-date values in the 501 records for 00-80 and 00-81 credits.

Important Changes in Version 39

Version 39 includes the following updates:

  • Data stored under tax deduction codes 00-80, 00-81, 00-83, 00-84, and 0-85 are passed as positive values, even if they are negative in PrismHR.

  • Data under 00-82 is not passed and does not appear on the file. MasterTax uses the data from 00-80 and 00-81 to calculate the Medicare tax credit, so passing this data would result in double accounting.

  • Tax deduction code 00-84 (Families First healthcare credits) does not appear on the file because this tax deduction code reflects a secondary credit which is dependent on the values in 00-80 and 00-81. Instead, it is divided into two separate tax deduction codes, according to the proportion of sick leave vs. FMLA wages:

  • FE0000-117H - FMLA component of 00-84 credits.

  • FE0000-116H - Sick Leave component of 00-84 credits.

  • On 500 records, CARES Act/Retention credits are recorded under both Quarter-to-Date (positions 95-108) and Year-to-Date (109-122) metrics. The taxable values for these credits are equal to twice the tax amount stored under the credit. For example, if the tax amount under 00-83 was $100.00, the taxable amount in the 500 record is $200.00. This is because MasterTax performs its own calculation on CARES Act creditable wages.

  • For Q2 only, the RTS file also accounts for Q1 retention credits and passes them into the new tax deduction codes listed below. Quarter-to-Date taxable values (positions 109-122) are double the credit values, for the same reason as described above.

  • FE0000-178 - Q1 values for 00-83.

  • FE0000-178H - Q1 values for 00-85.

  • The system creates 501 records for tax deduction codes 00-80 and 00-81. The format resembles that of 501 records for tax deduction code 00-10. MasterTax uses the values in this record to populate boxes 5a(i) and 5a(ii) on Form 941 (capped by the difference between employee and employer Social Security tax). This means that these boxes should reflect only employee-side taxable wages for COVID-19 sick and medical leave, without generating additional tax on over-limit wages.

  • Offshoot federal codes are also added to these tax deduction codes, when applicable. For example GU is added for Guam.

========================================================================

Important Notes About Tax Reporting

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/Background-Content/TS-tax_report_note.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/Background-Content/TS-tax_report_note.htm

Important Notes About Tax Reporting

The COVID-19 legislation defines the ways in which businesses can claim tax credits for wages paid as coronavirus-related sick/medical leave. PrismHR uses several system-defined tax deduction codes to track the accumulation of these “qualified” wages, taxes calculated on based on those wages, and associated benefit plan costs.

2021: Updates to Tax Credit Caps

The new COVID-19 legislation resets the accumulation of tax credits for coronavirus-related paid leave; in PrismHR, this means the caps will reset for the following system-defined tax deduction codes:

  • 00-81 (COVID-19 Paid Sick Leave — Wage credit)

  • 00-82 (Credit for Medicare tax on qualified wages)

  • 00-84 (Benefit plan costs associated with COVID-19 Paid Sick and Family Leave)

PrismHR will begin accumulating the tax credits against this reset cap for all charge dates from April 1, 2021 onward.

The new 00-80 tax credit cap will also be increased to $12,000 from $10,000 as specified by the legislation. Please note that, unlike the credits listed above, the accumulation of this credit will not reset to $0 on April 1.

Updating Medicare and Social Security Tax Credit Settings

When the new regulations take effect on April 1, our understanding is that there will be a new credit for Social Security tax, which can be associated with employee qualifying wages.

Because of variations in interpretation, PrismHR also allows you to specify a pay date upon which 00-82 (Medicare tax) credits will stop accruing. To set this date, use the new Medicare Credit End Date field on the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters form.

[Image: Detail of ARPA Configuration fields: Medicare Credit End Date, SS Credit Start Date, SS Credit Billing Event]

PrismHR also implemented a new tax deduction code: 00-87. This code will track the accumulation of the new Social Security tax credit on payroll vouchers. You can set up a SS Credit Start Date and a SS Credit Billing Event on the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters form. The start and end dates are based on pay dates, not charge dates; this will allow service providers to organize their payroll batches and pay them in such a way that pre- and post-ARPA credit accumulations are handled separately.

[Image: Tax Deductions form open to code 00-87 (Employer SS tax credit), paid by Employer.]

Note: Note: Previously, the instructions below specified that the new pay codes should have a tax exemption set up for Medicare tax. This has now changed. Do not apply any tax exemptions to these pay codes.

Finally, service providers will need to set up new pay codes from which the tax credits will be calculated. You can add these pay codes to the existing tables on the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters form. The Emergency Paid Sick Leave (Self-Care) Pay Code table now supports multiple values to accommodate this.

The new pay codes you create should be exact replicas of the old ones, except with no tax exemptions.

As with the previous pieces of COVID-19 legislation, some of these features and descriptions are subject to change as further guidance becomes available.

New Tax Codes

The system uses the following new tax deduction codes to track the various COVID-19 rebate credits. Note that benefit cost credits are distributed between two tax deduction codes corresponding to the Families First Act and the CARES Act.

Tax Code Description
00-80 Families First Act FMLA Wage Credit
00-81 Families First Act Sick Leave Wage Credit
00-82 Medicare Tax Credit
00-83 CARES Act/Emergency Employee Retention Wage Credit
00-84 Families First Act Benefit Cost Credit
00-85 CARES Act Benefit Cost Credit
00-86 Social Security Tax Deferral
00-87 Social Security Tax Credit (replaces 00-82 under the newer American Rescue Plan Act).

These tax deduction codes populate on the MasterTax PTS download. You can also apply tax adjustments to them in the system.

As with all other federal tax deduction codes, the system tracks these at the employer level. This is a purposeful decision to align the tax credits with their Form 941 tax liability. However, Families First tax credits will apply to — and will ultimately be reported at — the client level. According to IRS Form 7200, all PEOs with clients claiming tax credit must complete Schedule R (client-by-client breakdown) with their Form 941 return.

Additionally, the CARES Act permits certain distressed businesses to defer their employer-side OASDI (Social Security tax) payments. Statutory language clearly establishes this is a client-level election, despite PEO reporting at the employer level.

About Tax Code 00-86

To comply with the August 8th Executive Order, “Memorandum on Deferring Payroll Tax Obligations in Light of the Ongoing COVID-19 Disaster,” PrismHR introduced tax deduction code 00-86 to track employee Social Security tax deferrals, which are permitted under the order between September 1 and December 31, 2020.

For all employees who defer this tax on payrolls between these dates, the system generates a 00-86 entry. The entry is equal to the negative of the employee’s 00-12 tax value.

Values stored under 00-86 accrue on either the client federal entity or employer tax register, whichever is used to store other federal tax data. The system also reports this data on the MasterTax PTS file under an E or F code, as appropriate.

Passing Tax Credits Into MasterTax

Note: Note: Under our traditional PEO configuration, credits accrue in MasterTax on the employer level. This means that aggregate client credits will reduce the PEO employer’s 941 liability and deposits. This could result in billing and deposit inconsistencies on a payroll-to-payroll basis. You will also be unable to track these credits appropriately in MasterTax.

MasterTax created three new core tax deduction codes to support the Families First and CARES Acts:

  • FE0000-116 Federal - Employer Sick Leave Wages Credit

  • FE0000-117 Federal - Employer Family Leave Wages Credit

  • FE0000-118 Federal - Employer Employee Retention Credit

MasterTax users must map the three PrismHR tax deduction codes to the MasterTax deduction codes as follows:

PrismHR Code — Mapping

00-80 — FE0000-117

00-81 — FE0000-116

00-82, 00-84 — Can currently be mapped to either of the above. Our RTS file structure will, for reporting and reconciliation purposes, break out the Medicare tax portion (00-82) to correspond with its associated wage credit.

00-83, 00-85 — FE0000-118

Note: Note: Please contact MasterTax with any questions about setting up 00-87 in that application.

Within MasterTax, users can toggle whether individual clients defer employer Social Security payments under the CARES act. However, under a traditional PEO configuration, the applicable federal tax data will not exist at the client level. As a result, users will be unable to leverage this setting. We recommend the following two solutions to this issue:

PrismHR Solution Options

Use SBEA Certified PEO Setup

In PrismHR, you can enable our CPEO functionality, which was developed to support Schedule R reporting under the Small Business Efficiency Act (SBEA). This configuration enables you to:

  • Break out federal taxes by client, by employer.

  • Pass this federal tax data to MasterTax on that level.

  • Accrue and track credits on the client level and deposit accordingly.

  • Flag clients in MasterTax for employer-side OASDI deferral.

This is the preferred approach if you want the most accurate, consistent, and automated tax management and reporting.

Note: Note: Before you start, please note that it will take time to set up these features. You can consult the PrismHR Customer Support Team and the SBEA User Guide for details about enabling SBEA-compliant functionality. You can find a COVID-specific SBEA setup guide by searching for “SBEA User Guide” in the PrismHR Customer Resource Center.

If you decide to use this solution and you are a non-certified PEO, or if you want to start leveraging this configuration mid-year, we strongly recommend setting the Limit testing on Employer Register flag immediately after flagging the employer as a CPEO.

[Image: Detail of the Employer Details form with “Limit Testing on Employer Register” enabled]

Self-Manage Tax Settings

Users who opt not to leverage CPEO Schedule R functionality can elect to preserve the employer-level configuration within MasterTax and pass these tax credits on that level.

Alternatively, users opting not to leverage CPEO Schedule R functionality, who nonetheless want to preserve a client-centric approach in MasterTax, must then self-manage this process. To prevent certain tax deduction codes from flowing into MasterTax, you can enable the Suppress Reporting to MasterTax setting as shown in the images below.

Neither of these options will organically generate a Schedule R through MasterTax. However, to better support all users, including MasterTax users, PrismHR also developed the 941 Quarterly Return Worksheet Report to provide some insight into the COVID-19/Form 941 reporting process.

[Image: Tax Deductions form open to code 00-80 (emergency FMLA tax credit). The field “Suppress Reporting To Mastertax” is highlighted and enabled.]

COVID-19 Credit Tracking Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID-19_Credit.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID-19_Credit.htm

COVID-19 Credit Tracking Report

Use the COVID-19 Credit Tracking Report to view accrued tax credits, refunds or deferrals associated with COVID-19 paid leave.

To access this form, click Reports. Under Operations|Report, click COVID-19 Credit Tracking Report.

To generate the COVID-19 Credit Tracking Report:

  1. Complete the following fields:
Field Name Description
Start Pay Date This field is optional. If you want to limit your report to a specific pay date range, enter the starting pay date here.
End Pay Date This field is optional. However, if you have entered the starting pay date above, then enter the ending pay date here.
Report Type Select the type of report you want to generate from the following options: - Employee Report - Client Summary (Client Totals Only)
  • Employee Report
  • Client Summary (Client Totals Only) | Break Out 941 Liability Total | This field is optional. Select this option if you want to include the break out of the 941 Liability Total in your report. | | Breakout Tax Credits Accrued Total | This field is optional. Select this option if you want to include the break out of the total for the Tax Credits Accrued under tax deduction codes 00-80 to 00-85 in your report. In the report parameters, this column is further divided into totals for each tax deduction code, as well as the FMLA Medicare, FMLA Healthcare, Sick Leave Medicare, and Sick Leave Healthcare components. | | Include Only Clients with Credits or Deferrals | This field is optional. Select this option if you want to only include clients with credits of deferrals in your report, which is the employer-side Social Security Tax Deferred plus Tax Credits Accrued. | | Include Refunds | This field is optional. You can only select this option for the Client Summary (Client Totals Only) Report. Select this option if you want to only include those clients with refunds in your report. | | Client Ids | This field is optional. If you want to limit your report to specific clients only, you can enter the Client IDs here or click the Client Ids hyperlink and select them from the Client Master Search selector. |

  1. Click Run to generate the selected report with the selected parameters on screen. If you are running this report for all employees, it might take a while for the system to complete.

  2. Click Close to close this form at any time.

Report Columns

This report is organized by clients, then by employers, then by employees. For each employee, the report displays:

Report Column — Description

Total 941 Liability — Liability associated with tax deduction codes 00-10, 00-11, 00-12, 00-15, and 00-16. If you select Break Out 941 Liability Total in the report parameters, this column is further divided into totals for the above tax deduction codes (income tax, employer-side Medicare tax, employee-side Medicare tax, employer-side Social Security tax, and employee-side Social Security tax).

Employer Social Security Tax Deferred — The employer-side Social Security tax liability that accrued between the dates specified in the Cares Act Employer Social Security Deferral table and between the dates specified in the report setup parameters.

Tax Credits Accrued — Tax credits accrued under tax deduction codes 00-80 to 00-85. If you select Break Out Tax Credits Accrued Total in the report parameters, this column is further divided into totals for each tax deduction code, as well as the FMLA Medicare, FMLA Healthcare, Sick Leave Medicare, and Sick Leave Healthcare components.

Total Tax Credits and Deferrals — Employer-side Social Security Tax Deferred plus Tax Credits Accrued.

Net Current 941 Liability — Total 941 Liability minus Total Tax Credits and Deferrals.

The report also displays client totals for each of these values.

Refund columns

If you select Include Refund, the report includes these columns:

Rebate Column — Description

Credit Refund — Total credit rebates.

Form 7200 — COVID credits rebated under Form 7200.

Defer Refund — Refunded employer-side Social Security taxes.

UnRefund SS — Intended Social Security tax deferrals MINUS the actual rebate.

UnRefund CR — Total credits minus Credit Refunds and Form 7200 Refunds.

Pending — Future-dated bill pending items. This total displays at the top of the COVID-19 Refunds form. Please see Viewing Refund Data.

Using the COVID-19 Leave Estimate Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID-19_Report.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID-19_Report.htm

Using the COVID-19 Leave Estimate Report

This report retrieves employee hours and payment information to help you set up emergency paid leave that complies with the Families First Coronavirus Response Act.

To run this report:

  1. If you want to filter by Employee Type or Pay Method, select the appropriate option(s).

  2. If you want to filter by employee, select the appropriate Employee(s).

  3. Click Run. The report generates.

[Image: Report column headers: Hours Worked (Total and 2 Week Average), Standard Hours (2 weeks), Sick Leave Hours (Suggested, Actual, Taken, Remaining), and Rate of Pay (Standard, two-thirds of that value, Average from last pay period, and two-thirds of that value).]

Analyzing the Report

For each employee, the report returns:

  • General employee information (employee number, name, type, pay method, and hire date).

  • Hours Worked (total and two-week average). Does not include sick time hours.

  • Standard Hours (2 weeks). Uses the Standard Hours value on Employee Details > Pay, adjusted based on the employee’s pay method. For example, the value for employees on a monthly pay cycle is:

[Image: Equation showing hours calculation: Standard Hours times 12 months, divided by 52 weeks, and then multiplied by two.]

  • Sick Leave Hours (Suggested, Actual, Taken, and Remaining). Suggested hours default to 80 for full-time employees. For part-time employees, the value is their two-week average unless the employee was hired within the last 6 months. In that case, the value is just the employee’s Standard Hours on Employee Details > Pay. You can override the Actual Sick Leave Hours value using a new field on Employee Details > Other, called COVID-19 Paid Sick Max Hours.

  • Rates of Pay and 2/3 these values, in compliance with the legislation. The Standard value pulls from Employee Details. The Average (Last Pay Period) value is calculated using data from the last pay period. The system divides wages by hours, adjusted according to any FLSA settings on the employee’s pay codes. If no FLSA settings exist, the system simply divides all gross payments to the employee by the number of hours.

941 Quarterly Return Worksheet Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID_941_WSReport.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID_941_WSReport.htm

941 Quarterly Return Worksheet Report

This report displays the system-recommended values to enter in Form 941, using the calculation instructions provided by the Form 941 worksheet.

Important Notes

  • If you use Quarterly Express Plus (QEP), you need to leverage and rearrange the data on your Form 941. Do not provide the SBEA/CPEO version of this download to QEP.

Running the Report

To run the report:

  1. Click the Work Centers menu and select Quarterly Tax Processing under Work Centers|Tax. Then, under Quarterly Tax Reports, select 941 Quarterly Report.

  2. Specify whether you want this build to include the specified employer, all clients, one of the other options. Click the question mark icons for details.

  3. Select an Employer ID, Year, and Tax Quarter.

  4. For Schedule B Format, select Post COVID-19.

  5. Click Build.

About this Report

This report displays suggested values for each box of the 2020 Form 941. It also includes FEIN and employee count information. The following table describes the new and updated report line items, along with their system calculation logic.

Mid-2021 Additions: About the Pre-March and Post-April Categories

The Q3 2021 additions to Form 941 subdivide COVID-19 credits even more finely than before, requiring updates to the PrismHR reporting logic. For reporting purposes, the system now divides “Sick and Family Leave” credits into two categories:

  • Credits accrued on charge dates falling before April 1 (pre-April credits)

  • Credits accrued on charge dates falling after March 31 (post-March credits)

The pre-April category is determined using the following calculation:

  • For any vouchers with pay period start and end dates prior to April 1, include all 00-80, 00-81, 00-82, 00-84, and 00-87 credits in this category.

  • For any vouchers with pay period start dates before April 1 and pay period end dates on April 1 or later (but with the pay date in Q1), determine the ratio of COVID pay codes used on pre-April charge dates versus on post-March charge dates. Multiply this ratio by the values of any 00-80, 00-81, 00-82, 00-84, and 00-87 credits on the voucher, and place each portion of each credit in the corresponding category. For example, if 80% of the COVID credits are associated with charge dates before April 1, then this category receives (0.8 * sum of total COVID credits). The post-March category would then receive the remaining 20%.

The post-March category contains the credits remaining after these calculations, plus credits from any vouchers with pay period start/end dates after March 31.

Number Title Calculation Logic
5a Taxable Social Security Wages Employee Social Security (00-12) Net Taxable amount minus the values in 5a(i) and 5a(ii).
5a(i) Qualified Sick Leave Wages The system uses the lesser of these two values (calculated employee-by-employee): - Accumulated 00-81 credits for the quarter - Difference between employee (00-12) and employer (00-16) Social Security tax costs for the quarter.
  • Accumulated 00-81 credits for the quarter
  • Difference between employee (00-12) and employer (00-16) Social Security tax costs for the quarter. | 5a(ii) | Qualified Family Leave Wages | The system uses the lesser of these two values (calculated employee-by-employee): - Accumulated 00-80 credits for the quarter - Difference between employer (00-16) and employee (00-12) Social Security tax costs, minus the value in 5a(i), for the quarter. |
  • Accumulated 00-80 credits for the quarter
  • Difference between employer (00-16) and employee (00-12) Social Security tax costs, minus the value in 5a(i), for the quarter. | 7 | Adjustment for Fraction of Cents | Displays the difference between taxes calculated from payrolls and taxes calculated for Form 941. | | 11a | Qualified R&D Credits | 0 | | 11b | Nonrefundable Portion of Credit for Qualified Sick and Family Leave Wages for Leave Taken Before April 1, 2021 | Only using credits from the “pre-April” category (see section above). 0 for Q3 and Q4 of 2021. For previous quarters, uses the lesser of the following two values: - Total credits accrued under 00-80, 00-81, 00-82, and 00-84. - Employer Social Security Cap: This corresponds to line 1l of the 941 Worksheet. Equal to the sum of the values in boxes 5a and 5b, times 0.5. This calculated value is also used to calculate the value in box 11c. |
  • Total credits accrued under 00-80, 00-81, 00-82, and 00-84.
  • Employer Social Security Cap: This corresponds to line 1l of the 941 Worksheet. Equal to the sum of the values in boxes 5a and 5b, times 0.5. | 11c | Nonrefundable Employee Retention Credit from Worksheet 1 | The lesser of the following two values: - The Employer Social Security Cap value calculated above minus the value in 11b. - The sum of the credits accrued under 00-83 and 00-85. If this report is run for Quarter 2 of 2020, the Q1 00-83 and 00-85 are also added. |
  • The Employer Social Security Cap value calculated above minus the value in 11b.
  • The sum of the credits accrued under 00-83 and 00-85. | 11d | Nonrefundable Portion of Credit for Qualified Sick and Family Leave wages for Leave Taken After March 31, 2021 | The lesser of the following: - Credits stored under the post-April category. - 0.5 × line 5c value (employer Medicare tax) |
  • Credits stored under the post-April category.
  • 0.5 × line 5c value (employer Medicare tax) | 11e | Nonrefundable Portion of COBRA Premium Assistance Credit | Client-level 00-19 amount, capped at the amount of remaining employer-side Medicare tax. | | 11f | Number of Individuals Provided COBRA Premium Assistance | Number of employees who have a 00-19 tax adjustment voucher associated with the reporting entity. | | 11g | Total Nonrefundable Credits | 11a + 11b + 11c + 11d + 11e + 11f. | | 12 | Total Taxes After Adjustments & Nonrefundable Credits | 10 minus 11g. | | 13a | Total Deposits for Quarter | 0 | | 13b | Deferred Amount of ER and EE SS | Value stored under tax deduction code 00-86. | | 13c | Refundable Portion of Employee Retention Credit for Qualified Sick and Family Leave Wages for Leave Taken After April 1, 2021 | 0 for Q3 and Q4 of 2021. For previous quarters, uses the remainder of the credits in the pre-April category, after the calculations for line 11b. | | 13d | Refundable Portion of Employee Retention Credit | Quarterly credits accrued under 00-83 and 00-85, minus the value in box 11c. The Quarter 2 2020 report also includes credits from Quarter 1. | | 13e | Refundable Portion of Employee Retention Credit for Qualified Sick and Family Leave Wages for Leave Taken After March 31, 2021 | Remainder of credits in the post-March category, after the calculations for line 11d. | | 13f | Refundable Portion of COBRA Premium Assistance Credit | Any 00-19 credits remaining after the calculation of 11e (including all employer-associated 00-19 credits). | | 13g | Total Deposits, Deferrals, and Refund Credits | 13a + 13b + 13c + 13d + 13e + 13f. | | 13h | Form 7200 Advances | 0 | | 13i | Deposits, Deferrals, and Refundable Credits Less Advances | 13g minus 13h. | | 19 | Health Plan Expenses Allocable to Sick Leave Wages (Pre-April) | Applies only to credits in the pre-April category. 00-81 credit amount divided by ((00-80 + 00-81 credits) × 00-84 credit amount). | | 20 | Health Expenses Allocable to Family Leave Wages (Pre-April) | Applies only to credits in the pre-April category. Total 00-84 credits minus the value in box 19. | | 21 | Wages for Employee Retention Credit | Double the 00-83 credits. | | 22 | Health Plan Expenses Allocable to Line 21 | Double the 00-85 credits. | | 23 | Sick Leave Wages for Leave Taken After March 31 | 00-81 values in the post-March category. | | 24 | Health Plan Expenses Allocable to Line 21 | Same logic as line 19, applied to credits in the post-March category. | | 25 | CBA Sick Leave | 0 | | 26 | Family Leave Wages for Leave Taken After March 31 | 00-80 values in the post-March category. | | 27 | Health Plan Expenses Allocable to Line 26 | Same logic as line 20, applied to credits in the post-March category. | | 28 | CBA Family Leave | 0 | ========================================================================

COVID-19 941 Over-Limit Error Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID_Over_limit_report.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID_Over_limit_report.htm

COVID-19 941 Over-Limit Error Report

Use this report to identify employees affected by the employee vs. employer-side Social Security tax over-limit issue.

Background

Because COVID-19 credits (00-80 and 00-81) are exempt from employer-side Social Security tax, an employee who was paid COVID-19-related wages in 2020 may have a higher 00-12 (Employee SS Tax) value compared to their 00-16 (Employer SS Tax) value, for that quarter. If this employee later reached the $137,700 Social Security tax threshold under 00-12, the system continued to accrue Social Security tax on the employer side, since the 00-16 value had not yet reached the limit. This behavior is incorrect and can have significant downstream effects.

This issue was corrected in the October release and 00-16 now stops accruing as soon as 00-12 reaches the Social Security tax limit. That release also introduced this report, which can help you identify any employees affected by this issue and provide data to help you fix the issue for them.

Running the Report

To run the COVID-19 941 Over-Limit Error Report:

  1. Click the Back Office menu.

  2. Under Operations|Report, select COVID-19 OverLimit Report. The setup form displays.

  3. Enter a Year and, if necessary, a Quarter.

  4. Select an Employer if you want to run the report for that employer. To run the report for all employers, leave this field blank.

  5. Click Run. The report generates.

Analyzing the Report

Note: Note: This over-limit issue was corrected in the October release. If none of your employees reached the Social Security tax limit before this release, this report may not pull any data as the issue will not exist for them.

This report returns information about the differences between employer-side and employee-side Social Security tax. It displays the Net Taxable amount, the actual tax amount, and the “over limit” amount for both the employer and employee components of the tax, along with calculated differences between these two components. You can then use this information to correct the records of the affected employees. (Note that employee information was cropped out of the left-hand side of this screenshot).

The report provides client-level and employer-level totals for these values (in bold).
