Paycheck Protection Program Reports

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID_PPP_Report.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Reports/COVID_PPP_Report.htm

Paycheck Protection Program Reports

Note: Note: Several settings on the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters form can influence the output of these reports. Please review the descriptions of these settings to better understand how you can customize the outputs of the reports.

You can use these reports to assist with Paycheck Protection Program (PPP) loan forgiveness. The Payroll Cost Report helps you determine payroll-related costs incurred over the loan forgiveness period. The Full-Time Equivalent Analysis report helps you determine whether your loan forgiveness will be decreased due to workforce reductions.

Known Issues/Things to Know

This section describes aspects of the reports that may cause unexpected outputs. Some of these will be fixed and others are consequences of the reports themselves. Please keep this information in mind when you run and analyze your reports.

  • The Cash Compensation column includes payments flagged as Reported Tips.

  • The FTE Simplified report sometimes does not complete for very large clients. However, the standard calculation now works for clients of all sizes.

Background

Loans received through the Paycheck Protection Program are forgivable up to 100%, but this value can vary based on several factors:

  • The loan can only be forgiven for payroll costs over a specified period.

  • If a client’s full-time equivalent (FTE) count decreases over this period, the loan forgiveness percentage also decreases.

  • The forgiveness amount can also decrease based on employee compensation reductions.

The Payroll Cost Report provides insight into the first bullet point above. The FTE Analysis Report provides insight into the second.

Before You Begin

These reports use a new field on the CARES Act Parameters form, called Paycheck Protection Program Loan Start Date.

Before you run any of the reports, navigate to Client Details >Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

CARES Act Parameters and enter the appropriate date in this field.

If you do not enter a date in this field, you can enter it in the field that displays on the report setup form.

Full-Time Equivalent Analysis Report

Note: Note: You can now exclude specific pay codes from the FTE report logic. On the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters form, enter these pay codes in the new Exclude FTE Pay Code table.

This report includes all employees with hours that fall within the appropriate PPP window. It also calculates the number of full-time equivalents employed during the given time frame and provides insight into whether, and to what extent, a FTE reduction occurred (see the Reduction Quotient description below).

Employee hours are based on charge dates within the associated time frame. The system then divides these hours by the number of days in that period to return a daily hours amount, and multiplies this value by 7 to return a weekly hours amount. Finally, the report divides this value by 40 to return a Full-Time Equivalency value, capped at 1.0 and rounded to the nearest tenth.

Report Background

This report displays a breakdown of employees who have hours in the system within two lookback periods and a Covered Period (specified during report setup). The two lookback periods are:

  • February 15, 2019 – June 30, 2019

  • January 1, 2020 – February 29, 2020

Additionally, please note the following:

  • For all employees, the system calculates hours using the total Hours Paid under Standard-type hourly pay codes and the total Hours Worked for any other pay codes.

  • The report does not include employees with 0 hours.

  • The report does not include 1099 contractors or business owners.

  • The report does not include pay codes with the Exclude from ACA Reporting option enabled.

Finally, this report calculates several total values: Total FTE and a calculated Reduction Quotient, which indicates whether an FTE reduction occurred during any of the time frames. The Reduction Quotient equals the Covered Period total FTEs divided by the total FTEs for the appropriate Lookback Period.

See Analyzing the Report for a description of these calculations.

Running the Report

Note: Note: While the report now runs for very large clients, the Simplified FTE method does not always finish calculating for clients of this size. The standard (non-Simplified) report does run completely for large clients.

To run the COVID-19 PPP Forgiveness Report:

  1. SelectReports menu and then select COVID-19 PPP Forgiveness Report under HR|Report.

  2. In the Report field, choose Full Time Equivalent Analysis.

  3. Enter a Covered Period Start Date if one does not populate.

  4. Select whether you want to run the report for 8 weeks, 24 weeks, or until a Custom End Date.

  5. If you want to filter the report results by location, select a Worksite Location.

  6. If the option displays, select whether you want to use the Simplified Method for FTE calculation. See the description of the system-level parameter in the Cap and Credit settings table of Configuring System-Level Parameters

  7. If the option displays, enter your custom start and end dates for Lookback Periods 1 and 2.

  8. Click Run. The report generates.

Analyzing the Report

The report includes the following columns for each employee:

Column Title — Description

Employee No, Name, ID, Current Status, Status Date, Current Type Class, Type Date, Original Hire Date, and Last Hire Date — All these values can be found on the corresponding Employee Details > Name tab, in the Employment panel.

Covered Period (Full-Time Equivalency) — Full-time equivalent value for the employee over the selected time period, beginning with the Loan Start Date. Calculation: employee’s weekly hours during the covered period divided by 40 and capped at 1.0 and rounded to the nearest tenth. Simplified Method: Active full-time employees receive 1/7th of a unit per day worked. Active part-time employees receive 1/14th of a unit.

Lookback Option 1 — Average weekly employee hours between February 15, 2019 and June 30, 2019, divided by 40 and capped at 1.0 and rounded to the nearest tenth. Simplified Method: Active full-time employees receive 1/7th of a unit per day worked. Active part-time employees receive 1/14th of a unit.

Lookback Option 2 — Average weekly employee hours between January 1, 2020 and February 29, 2020, divided by 40 and capped at 1.0 and rounded to the nearest tenth. Simplified Method: Active full-time employees receive 1/7th of a unit per day worked. Active part-time employees receive 1/14th of a unit.

The report also provides several Average Hours Analysis columns (Excel output only):

Column Title — Description

Covered Total Hours — Employee’s total hours starting from the loan start date and ending after the selected number of days.

Covered Weekly Average — Covered Total Hours divided by the number of days, multiplied by 7.

Lookback 1 Total Hours — Total employee hours between February 15, 2019 and June 30, 2019.

Lookback 1 Weekly Average — Lookback 1 Total Hours divided by 136 days, then multiplied by 7.

Lookback 2 Total Hours — Total employee hours between January 1, 2020 and February 29, 2020.

Lookback 2 Weekly Average — Lookback 2 Total Hours divided by 60 days, then multiplied by 7.

Finally, the report includes the following totals, displayed for both lookback periods:

Total Name — Description

Total — Total FTEs for the column.

Reduction Quotient (Lookback periods only) — Covered Period column value divided by total FTEs for the appropriate time frame. A value greater than or equal 1.0 indicates that no workforce reduction occurred during that period. A value less than 1.0 indicates that there was some reduction in FTEs compared to this period.

Payroll Cost Report

Report Background

This report uses the value entered in the new Paycheck Protection Program Loan Start Date field. If you have not configured this field yet, please see the Before You Begin section above.

The report displays payroll costs over the 8-week, 24-week, or user-entered time period, beginning with the loan start date. The report includes any employee who has existing data for any of the payroll cost columns (defined further in Analyzing the Report

Running the Report

To run the COVID-19 Payroll Cost Report:

  1. SelectReports menu and then select COVID-19 PPP Forgiveness under HR|Report.

  2. In the report setup field, choose Payroll Cost Report.

  3. Enter a Covered Period Date if one does not populate.

  4. Select whether you want to run the report for 8 weeks, 24 weeks, or until a Custom End Date.

  5. Select whether you want to use the Simplified FTE calculation method, as with the FTE Analysis Report.

  6. Select a Worksite Location if you want to limit the report results to only that location.

  7. If you want the report to include health and retirement contributions made by owners, select Include Owner Health and Retirement.

  8. Click Run. The report generates.

Analyzing the Report

Values for Employee Wages and all subsequent report columns are based on data from any payroll batches with pay dates that fall within the chosen time period, as specified by the PPP Loan Start Date value you entered.

The report does not include 1099 contractors, but it currently does include non-resident alien employees owners. If you do not want to include non-resident aliens on the reports, go to the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters and select Exclude NRAs from PPP Reporting. If you want to exclude owners from the report, select Exclude Owners from PPP Reports on the same form.

The report includes the following columns for each employee:

Column Title — Description

Employee No, Employee Name, Employee ID — All these values can be found on the corresponding Employee Details > Name tab, in the Employment panel.

Estimated Table Type — Whether the employee is expected to fall under either Table 1 or Table 2 in the Schedule A Worksheet of the PPP Loan Forgiveness Application. If the employee is the owner, this column populates an O. Subtotals for both table types also display. An employee is considered “Table 2” if their 2019 earnings and reported tips, divided by their active days in 2019 and multiplied by 365, is greater than $100,000 OR If the employee has annual pay at a rate of $100,000 or more for more than one day at any point in 2019.

Full Time Equivalency — Full-time equivalent calculation for this employee based on their hours. This is the same value as the one found in the Covered Period Column in the FTE Analysis Report. Owners display in this column as an O.

Total Cash Compensation — Any employee cash compensation (including Earnings and Reported Tips) recorded under pay dates within the selected time period.

Allowable Cash Compensation — The value in Total Cash Compensation, minus any COVID FMLA/Sick Leave pay codes, and capped at $15,385.

Health Care Expenses — Employer premium contributions toward plans flagged as Medical, Dental, and Vision, minus employee contributions toward these plans. Healthcare-related FSA plans are also included. For these plans, the report just uses the employer contribution. This column does not include any plans where the Do NOT Include in ACA Reporting option is selected.

Retirement Contributions — The total of all employer match amounts (including Regular Employer Match, Safe Harbor 1 Match, Safe Harbor 2 Match, and Non-Elective Match).

Employer State and Local Taxes — All employer-side tax liability, except for Federal Government (00-XX) tax deduction codes and tax deduction codes for U.S. territories, such as Guam and the Virgin Islands (52-XX and above).

Total Payroll Costs — Allowable Cash Compensation + Health Care Expenses + Retirement Contributions + State and Local Taxes.

Salary Reduction Report

The PPP Salary Reduction Report provides information about COVID-19-related employee pay reductions during the specified PPP covered period. It includes comparison calculations between pre-pandemic pay rates and covered period pay rates, along with indications about whether the employee’s pay was restored according to PPP guidelines.

Report Background

The SBA’s Paycheck Protection Program Loan Forgiveness Application describes the calculations you need to complete in order to determine your PPP loan forgiveness eligibility. Forgiveness eligibility is based on several factors, including the use of the loan funds and maintaining PPP staffing requirements. This report is designed to help you quantify your compliance with those staffing requirements.

In Table 1 of the Schedule A worksheet, you must specify each employee’s “Salary/Hourly Wage Reduction” if their pay during the loan’s Covered Period was at least 25% lower than their pay during the reference Lookback Period. You must also take into account whether the employee’s pay reductions qualified for any PPP safe harbor provisions before determining the final salary reduction.

This report provides:

  • Calculated employee pay rates for both the Covered Period and the Lookback Period.

  • A fractional comparison between the two rates.

  • A determination of whether the employee’s pay rate was restored by the date of submission of the loan forgiveness application.

  • An estimated final total for the employee’s pay reduction.

Running the Report

Note: Note: Depending on how your organization keys in payroll data, you may need to adjust the report setup dates so that you can capture data from full pay periods. In other words, report calculations may be “off” if you enter the correct covered period start and end dates, because they may not capture the entirety of the pay period data from the beginning or end of the covered period.

To run the report:

  1. SelectReports menu and then select COVID-19 PPP Forgiveness under HR|Report.

  2. In the report setup field, choose Salary Reduction Report.

  3. Enter Covered Period start and end date information as you would for the other PPP reports.

  4. Enter the SBA Loan Forgiveness Application Submission Date. The report uses this date in its calculations (see below). If you leave this field blank, the system uses today’s date OR December 31, 2020 (whichever is earlier).

  5. The report breaks out employees as either “Salaried” or “Hourly” based on certain factors. If you want the system to group “Commission” employees as Salaried, select the Treat Commissioned as Salaried? field. (“Commission employees” = employees whose Pay Method on Employee Details is set to Commission).

  6. If you need to use a custom lookback period, enter the Lookback 1 Start Date and End Date.

  7. Click Run. The report generates.

Analyzing the Report

Employee Information

The report sorts employees based on two criteria:

  • Estimated Table Type (1 or 2)

  • Salaried/ Hourly

The second category is based on the employee’s Pay Method on the Employee Details form. “Hourly” and “Driver” employees are considered Hourly (H). “Salary” employees are considered Salaried (S). “Commission” employees are grouped based on whether or not you selected the Treat Commissioned as Salaried? field on the report setup form.

Covered Period Average Annualized Salary/Hourly Rate

The Covered Period begins and ends on the dates specified in the report setup parameters.

The calculation involves the following steps:

  1. Calculate employee compensation using charge dates within the Covered Period (paid under pay codes flagged as Earnings or Tips).

  2. Divide compensation by other figures, depending on Pay Method:

  • Salaried: Compensation / Active Days * 365

  • Hourly: Compensation / Hours, where Hours = hours paid if the pay code is set to Standard and Hourly. Otherwise, hours worked is used. Hours worked are also used if the Pay Reason is set to Units.

  • The system excludes any pay codes flagged for exclusion for ACA or PPP purposes.

Lookback Period Average Annualized Salary/Hourly Rate

This is calculated using the same logic as in the section above, but in this case the Covered Period is replaced by the period from Jan 1 through Mar 31, 2020, unless an override period is entered in the report setup parameters.

Lookback Period Average Hours Worked

This column value = (hours worked in the lookback period/ number of days in the lookback period) * 7

Reduction Comparison

This column value = Covered Period Average Annualized Rate/ Lookback Period Average Annualized Rate

Feb 15 Rate Restored by: [DATE]

[DATE] = Application Submission Date entered during report setup. If you left this field blank, the report defaults to either today’s date or December 31, 2020 (whichever is earlier).

The value in this column can be Y, N, or NA.

The system prints NA if:

  • The Reduction Comparison calculation ≥ 0.75.

  • There is no employee compensation data for February 15, 2020.

  • The employee is not active on the Application Submission Date.

The system prints Y if:

  • The Reduction Comparison calculation is less than 0.75

  • AND

  • The employee’s annualized pay on the Application Submission Date ≥ their annualized pay on 2/15/20.

Otherwise, the system prints N.

Estimated Step 3 Reduction Amount

This is the final system estimation of the employee’s Schedule A, Table 1 pay reduction amount.

The report prints 0 if the Reduction Comparison calculation ≥ 0.75

The report also prints 0 if the value in the Feb 15 Rate Restored by: [DATE] column is Y.

Otherwise, the report uses the following formula: ((Lookback Average * 0.75) - Covered Period Average), and then adjusts that value based on whether the employee is hourly or salaried:

  • For Hourly (H) employees, multiply by Lookback Period Average Hours Worked, then multiply by 8 (weeks) OR 24 (weeks), depending on the length of the covered period. Any covered period longer than 8 weeks is treated as 24 weeks.

  • For Salaried (S) employees, divide by 52 (weeks), then multiply by either 8 or 24 weeks, according to the same logic described above.

Employee Retention Credit Recalc Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Reports/ERC_Report.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Reports/ERC_Report.htm

Employee Retention Credit Recalc Report

Note: Note: When applying Employee Retention Credit (ERC) “caps” or limits, both this report and the COVID-19 Recalculation Utility will take into account the fact that the 2020 credit limit was raised for 2021 (from $5000 per employee to $7000 per employee).

In December 2020, the CARES Act legislation was updated, introducing the possibility for businesses to have periods wherein they do not qualify for 00-83 or 00-85 credit accumulation (remember: 00-83 tracks the wage component of the Employer Retention Credit and 00-85 tracks the benefit cost component). Other scenarios are also possible: businesses might retroactively discover that their ERC credit dates overlap with their PPP forgiveness period. In this case, they would need to make adjustments to their reporting periods in order to claim both benefits to the fullest extent possible.

To support these changes, there are new exclusion date setup fields on the COVID-19 Client Parameters form (see Setting Up CARES Act Compliance).

This new ERC exclusion date feature significantly impacts three parts of the system:

  • Payroll - ERC credits will not calculate on any pay dates within the specified period.

  • COVID-19 Recalculation Utility - As noted elsewhere, running this utility after setting up the ERC exclusion dates will cause the utility to zero out ERC credits if those credits are associated with pay dates falling within the exclusion range.

  • ERC Recalc Report - This report was created to provide more insight into the behavior of this feature.

The Employee Retention Credit Recalc Report can help you independently determine any retroactive calculations related to ERC credits. If you need to test several date ranges to maximize your PPP loan forgiveness and ERC credit claims, you can run this report multiple times while adjusting the date range. Compare the outputs of these test reports and use your observations to inform any decisions about your reporting period dates.

Alternatively, you can use the report to preview the effects of the COVID-19 Recalculation Utility. PrismHR highly recommends doing this if you plan to run the utility after setting up your exclusion dates.

Running the Report

To run the Employee Retention Recalc Credit Report:

  1. ClickReports menu and select ERC Recalc Report under HR|Report. A setup form displays containing the name and ID of the selected client.

  2. Click Run. The report generates.

Report Output

The report data is displayed by employee. It lists the employee’s name, ID, associated Employer ID, and associated Client entity ID. After this general information, the report displays quarterly before and after calculations for both 00-83 and 00-85 credits, extending from Q1 2020 to the current quarter.

Setting up System-level COBRA Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Settings/ARPA_COBRA_System.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Settings/ARPA_COBRA_System.htm

Setting up System-level COBRA Parameters

Use the Cobra Credit Parameters form, located off the menu of the System Parameters form, to configure system-level COBRA settings in compliance with the American Rescue Plan Act (ARPA).

To configure system-level COBRA parameters for ARPA compliance:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. Under System|Change, select System Parameters.

  3. Click the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select COBRA Credit Parameters.

  4. Set your Cobra Billing Event Code, which will track 00-19 credits as they accrue.

  5. In the Creditable Clients table, specify all the clients to whom you want to provide a billing refund of 00-19 credits.

  6. Click Save.

Setting Up CARES Act Compliance

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Settings/COVID_CARES_Setup.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Settings/COVID_CARES_Setup.htm

Setting Up CARES Act Compliance

Note: Note: If you are a large employer as defined by the act, you must set up separate pay and billing codes to pay employees who otherwise would not be paid. See the steps above for details on this setup. If you are a small employer, you can use your standard pay codes to track CARES Act credits. You only need to set up a new billing code and billing event and then assign the billing event at the system level.

Important Notes

Please note the following about this feature:

  • You can use this form to assign CARES Act-specific pay codes to the client.

  • Tax credits will accrue on employees who are paid with the specified pay codes.

  • They stop accruing once they reach the $5,000 employee limit.

  • Medicare tax is not credited.

  • These tax deduction codes populate on both the employer registers and (if SBEA functionality is enabled) the client federal entity registers.

  • Wage and credit limits are not applied.

  • If you select the 941 Liability rebate option on the system-level COVID-19 settings, CARES Act credits will be rebated along with Families First Act credits. All these credits are compared with the client’s 941 liability during rebate calculation.

  • If you assign pay codes with a non-Standard Calculation Method, the system uses Hours Paid (instead of Hours Worked) to calculate CARES Act credits for that employee.

  • Credit limits were adjusted to conform with new legislation.

  • Use the ERC Exclusion Dates to disable credit accrual for one or more date ranges.

  • The COVID-19 Credit Recalculation Utility also respects ERC Exclusion Dates. It zeros out any CARES credits on vouchers found within those date ranges.

  • The system no longer calculates employer retention credits on employees flagged as business owners. If you still want to perform these calculations, you can enable the Calculate ERC for Owners setting.

Procedure

To configure the system for CARES Act compliance:

  1. On the system-level COVID-19 settings, assign a billing event to the new Emergency Employee Retention Credit Billing Event field.

  2. Click Client and select Client Details under Client|Change.

  3. Click the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select COVID-19 Client Parameters.

  4. In the Emergency Retention Credit Pay Codes list, select all pay codes you set up to track CARES Act compliance.

  5. If you need to disable 00-83 and 00-85 credit accrual for certain date ranges (for example, your organization took out a PPP loan), enter the appropriate date ranges in the ERC Exclusion Start and End Date fields. Set the Category for each date range to Cares.

  6. Save the record.

========================================================================

Deferring Employer Social Security Taxes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Settings/COVID_Deferring_Employer_SS.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Settings/COVID_Deferring_Employer_SS.htm

Deferring Employer Social Security Taxes

You can use a new table on the Client Overrides form to defer employer-side Social Security taxes. To track deferrals, you must set up a unique billing code and a billing event. You must then assign that billing event on the COVID-19 system parameters form.

Note: Note: If you set up this feature for a client with no rebate cap logic set, all employer-side Social Security tax deferrals for that client are rebated back using a client bill pending entry. Alternatively if you set up this feature and the client has a 941-based rebate cap set, the client’s rebate will exclude deferred employer-side Social Security tax. In other words, the client rebate = the client 941 liability minus Social Security deferrals.

To set up employer-side Social Security tax deferrals:

  1. Ensure that you are on the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters Client Overrides form.

  2. Locate the CARES Act Employer Social Security Deferral table.

  3. Select a Client ID for each applicable client.

  4. Enter the appropriate start and end dates for each client. The system will defer employer-side social security taxes for any client payroll batches that have a pay date in this range.

  5. Save the record.

Configuring Employee-Level Settings

The system-level and client-level settings may not apply to all employees. Specifically, you may want to cap employee paid sick leave at a value other than the default 80 hours. A new field on the Employee Details > Other tab allows you to configure this setting.

Note: Note: The default value for COVID-19 Paid Sick Leave Max Hours is 80. If the value in this field is reached during payroll initialization, the system displays a payroll warning.

To configure employee-level COVID-19 settings:

  1. Click HR and then select HR|Change > Employee Details.

  2. Enter the appropriate Employee ID.

  3. Select the Other tab.

  4. In the Miscellaneous panel, enter the employee’s COVID-19 Paid Sick Leave Max Hours. This overrides any client-level settings or system-level defaults. This value also displays in the Actual Sick Leave Hours column of the COVID-19 Leave Estimate Report.

  5. Save the record.

Tracking Healthcare Payment Credits

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Settings/COVID_Healthcare_Credits.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Settings/COVID_Healthcare_Credits.htm

Tracking Healthcare Payment Credits

Logic

PrismHR uses two tax deduction codes to track healthcare credits:

  • 00-84: Families First Act healthcare credits

  • 00-85: CARES Act healthcare credits

Because these credits accrue under two different pieces of legislation, the system uses special logic to calculate and allocate them:

  1. Calculates standardized healthcare costs and hours across a given payroll.

  2. Adds FSA plan costs for that payroll to the standardized value.

  3. Determines the percentage of hours worked under each act (vs. Standard Hours) and allocates credits to the appropriate tax deduction codes, according to these percentages.

The table below includes descriptions of each calculation step, along with the credit cap logic for each act. Note that aspects of this calculation may be impacted by the settings you configured on the system-level COVID-19 parameters form (see above).

Calculation Element — Description

(Step 1) Standardized Healthcare Cost Calculation — The system compiles all monthly billing-side costs of any employee plans with an insurance class of Medical, Dental, Vision, Life Insurance, and Short/Long-Term Disability. If the plan is flagged as a Section 125 plan, the system includes the full billing amount (employee-side and employer-side). Otherwise, the system only uses the employer contribution. It then multiplies this sum by 12 to get an annual standardized cost, and then, to get a per-batch cost, divides this value by the number of pay cycles per year for that employee (for example, 52 for a weekly employee or 26 for a biweekly employee).

(Step 2) FSA Plans — This value is not standardized. Instead, it is handled on a batch-by-batch basis. For a given payroll batch, if the employee has an FSA plan flagged as a healthcare plan, the system takes the standardized healthcare cost (calculated above) and adds the FSA plan’s cost for that payroll (both employer and employee contributions).

(Step 3) Allocating Costs to the Appropriate Tax Codes — The system calculates the percent to allocate to each tax deduction code, based on the hours worked under Families First pay codes and CARES Act pay codes. For each act, the system divides the hours worked under that act’s pay codes by the employee’s Standard Hours (found on Employee Details). The resulting fraction is multiplied by the total healthcare costs for the payroll, and that amount is allocated to the appropriate act’s tax deduction code.

Cap Logic (Families First Act) — For tax deduction code 00-84 (Families First healthcare credit), there is no cap on the amount of healthcare credits you can accrue. However, healthcare credits stop accruing once you reach the employee’s wage cap as defined under the act.

Cap Logic (CARES Act) — Healthcare credits stop accruing once the combined amounts in tax deduction codes 00-83 and 00-85 reach the $5000 limit ($7000 in 2021). Note that this limit includes accrued healthcare credits.

Setup

To set up healthcare credit tracking:

  1. Create two unique billing codes and billing events for this feature (one for Families First and one for the CARES Act).

  2. Navigate to the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters form.

  3. Assign the billing event to the Families First Health Care Credit Billing Event field.

  4. Assign the other billing event to the CARES Act Health Care Credit Billing Event field.

  5. Select the Default Calculate Health Care Credit option. If you want to set this up client-by-client, you can set the corresponding setting on the COVID-19 client override form.

  6. Click Save.

Configuring System-Level Parameters

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Settings/COVID_System_Settings.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Settings/COVID_System_Settings.htm

Configuring System-Level Parameters

Note: Note: We strongly recommend setting your Default Wage Cap Logic and Default Tax Credit Cap Logic to the same value. Using different settings can cause serious irregularities.

If you completed the steps in the previous sections, you have already created pay codes and billing events to track coronavirus-related leave, and you also understand how client rebate credits accrue under the new tax deduction codes. Use the settings below to associate those pay codes and billing events with the appropriate leave type. You can also configure your wage and credit capping logic, along with many other global COVID-19 calculation and reporting settings.

To access this form, click the Back Office menu and then select System Parameters > Actions menu > COVID-19 Emergency.

Setting Up Pay Codes and Billing Events

The system uses the pay codes and billing events entered here to track COVID-19-related tax credits and calculate client rebates for these credits. You must create unique pay codes and billing events for each field on this form.

Field — Description

Emergency Tax Credit Rebate Billing Event — Billing event you set up to track COVID-19 paid leave credits.

Emergency Medicare Credit Rebate Billing Event — Billing event to track employer-side Medicare tax credit rebates for COVID-19 leave.

Emergency Employee Retention Credit Billing Event — Billing event to track credits accrued in compliance with the CARES Act.

Employer Social Security Deferral Credit Billing Event — Billing event to track credits for deferred employer Social Security taxes.

Families First Health Care Credit Billing Event — Billing event to track credits for healthcare-related benefits payments allocated under the Families First Coronavirus Response Act.

CARES Act Health Care Billing Event — Billing event to track credits for healthcare-related benefits payments allocated under the CARES Act.

COVID-19 Emergency Paid Sick Leave (Self-Care) Pay Code — One or more pay codes to use for COVID-19-related sick leave payments when the employee takes time off to care for themselves.

Emergency Paid Sick Leave (Dependent-Care) Pay Code — One or more pay codes to use for COVID-19-related sick leave payments when the employee takes time off to care for a dependent.

Emergency FMLA Pay Code — One or more pay codes to use for COVID-19-related FMLA payments. For example, you can enter one pay code with a Factor of 1.00 and another with a Factor of 0.67.

Establishing Tax Credit Caps and Other Settings (Updated for 2021)

You can configure credit cap settings according to your organizational needs. Refer to the following table for descriptions of the fields and their settings. See the sections below for an outline of the FFCRA’s tax credit limits.

CARES Act Employer Retention Credit Rules

Please note the following important details about the Employer Retention Credit under the CARES Act:

  • In 2020, the CARES Act allowed businesses to claim tax credit on 50% of employee wages and healthcare benefit costs paid between March 13 and December 31, 2020. The cap per employee was $5,000 (equivalent to $10,000 in wages paid between these dates).

  • In 2021, new legislation updated these values to 70% of employee wages and $7000 total. The system performs these calculations automatically for 2021 pay dates, while 2020 pay dates retain the calculation logic described above.

The system also tracks credits related to health care benefit payments and employer-side Social Security tax deferrals. For more information, see the field-level descriptions below, as well as the sections below called Deferring Employer-Side Social Security Taxes and Note About Health Care Credits.

2021 FFCRA Credit Rules

In March 2021, the legislation governing FFCRA credits was extended. Beginning on April 1, 2021, the system will respect the following qualified wage caps:

  • FMLA: $12,000 (credit accumulation does not reset)

  • Sick (Dependent-Care): $2000 or $5110 minus credits from Sick (Self-Care), whichever is less

  • Sick (Self-Care): $5110 minus credits from Sick (Dependent-Care)

2020 FFCRA Credit Rules

Aggregate

  • FMLA: $10,000

  • Sick (Dependent-Care): $2000, or $5110 minus credits from Sick (Self-Care), whichever is less

  • Sick (Self-Care): $5110, minus tax credits associated with Sick (Dependent-Care)

  • If an employee works 8 hours or fewer in a day, there is a cap of $200 per day

Daily (2020)

  • FMLA: $200 per time sheet line item

  • Sick (Dependent-Care): $200 per time sheet line item

  • Sick (Self-Care): $511 per time sheet line item

Field Description
Default Wage Cap Logic COVID-19 legislation specifies the legal extent to which employers must compensate workers for coronavirus-related leave. Employers can pay employees more than the specified amounts, but they will only receive credit up to the defined limits. Select whether to Cap Aggregate Limits Only or to Cap Daily and Aggregate Limits , both of which are specified in the legislation (see the summaries above). These settings reduce employee pay to the appropriate limits if it exceeds those limits. While we strongly recommend against using this setting in general, you can also select No Limits if you don’t want employee pay reduced in this way.
Default Tax Credit Cap Logic Select whether to enforce the federally defined tax credit caps. You can limit based on either the daily and aggregate or daily cap. You should use the same setting as Default Wage Cap Logic.
Default Suppress Client Rebate The client’s tax credit rebate is reflected as a separate Client Bill Pending entry in an invoice line item. The line item shows the total credits accumulated this pay period, along with the client rebate associated with this invoice. If the entire rebate is not owed at once, the system tracks rebates that are owed and that can presently be taken, based on the limit logic you defined in the above fields. If you don’t want the system to automate the rebate process as described above, select this option. Otherwise, leave this field alone and select an option for Default Client Tax Credit Rebate Cap Logic below.
Default Client Tax Credit Rebate Cap Logic Use this setting to determine the logic the system employs when calculating client credit rebates rebates. There are three options: No Limits, Cap at Client QTD 941 Liability, and Cap at Payroll Batch 941 Liability. The first option simply applies the full rebate to the client. However, we recommend using one of the other two settings. These settings only rebate clients up to their 941 liability (either quarter-to-date or for the current payroll batch). In other words, the amount available to rebate for a payroll = 941 Liability minus system-generated rebates, for the quarter or batch. Medicare tax calculation is included in the total client 941 liability, but is rebated separately. If the rebate for a payroll batch is higher than the 941 limit, the system creates a client bill pending entry with a date of 10/14/2241 for the amount left over after reaching the limit. The choice of date is meant to completely prevent the entry from being pulled into the next payroll run. - No Limits: Rebate the client for the full claimable credit amount for this payroll. The rebate is not reduced according to any rules or restrictions. - Cap at Client QTD 941 Liability: The system will only rebate the client up to the amount calculated for their 941 liability (quarter-to-date). - Cap at Payroll Batch 941 Liability: The system only rebates the client up to the amount calculated as 941 liability for that payroll batch. For example, if you select the third option and you have $1,000 in creditable wages/ $50 in Medicare tax for a payroll and, in this payroll, you have $2,000 in un-rebated 941 liability, you can rebate the full $1,000 for the payroll because there is enough liability to rebate against. The $50 Medicare rebate is deducted separately.
  • No Limits: Rebate the client for the full claimable credit amount for this payroll. The rebate is not reduced according to any rules or restrictions.
  • Cap at Client QTD 941 Liability: The system will only rebate the client up to the amount calculated for their 941 liability (quarter-to-date).
  • Cap at Payroll Batch 941 Liability: The system only rebates the client up to the amount calculated as 941 liability for that payroll batch. | Default Calculate Health Care Credit | Select this option if you want the system to calculate healthcare credits for all clients as defined by the CARES Act. | | Medicare Credit Limited to Tax Amount | > Note: Note: This option is enabled by default. Leave this option enabled if you want to continue to use the system’s default logic. However, the draft of the new Form 941 implies that Medicare tax credits should equal a straight 1.45% of accrued credits under 00-80 and 00-81. If you want to follow this guidance instead of using the system logic, deselect this option. Select this option if you want the system to calculate Medicare tax credits (00-82) as either 1.45% of the accrued Families First credits OR the actual employer Medicare tax amount on the associated voucher (whichever is less). This is the default system logic. If you do not select this option, the system calculates this credit only using the first strategy, taking the credits accrued under 00-80 and 00-81 and multiplying them by 1.45%. This is the logic implied by the draft Form 941. | | Exclude EE S125 Contributions | As federal guidance does not yet clarify whether to include these costs in the healthcare credit calculation, you can toggle this setting yourself. In the system, 00-80 and 00-81 credits accrue on sheltered wages. This can affect the values in boxes 5a(i) and 5a(ii) of Form 941, potentially implying that the employee went over the tax limit when they did not. You can use this option to determine how the system handles sheltered wages related to COVID-19 payments. Select None if you want to preserve previously described system behavior and continue including sheltered wages in these calculations. Otherwise, select one of the other options below. - Cap Wage Credits if Sheltered: This option caps COVID-19 wage credits at qualified wages by limiting the credit to 00-12 taxable wages. Generally, this may result in a reduction for Sheltered Earnings. More specifically, the system first takes employee COVID wages and compares them to taxable earnings on the employee voucher. The lesser of these two values becomes the new 00-81 credit amount. The system then subtracts the employee’s 00-81 credits from their taxable earnings and compares this value to their 00-80 credits. The lesser of these two values becomes the new value for 00-80. For 00-83 credits, the system subtracts the employee’s 00-80 and 00-81 credits from their taxable earnings and multiplies this value by 0.5. If this value is less than the employee’s current 00-83 credits, it replaces that value as the 00-83 credit. - Cap and Allocate to Health Care: The same calculation as described above, but the system also takes the amount reduced from the qualified wages above and adds that amount to the newly calculated 00-84 and 00-85 values, as appropriate (e.g. the Families First credit adjustment is added to 00-84 and the CARES credit adjustment is added to 00-85). |

Payroll warning will also reflect this. | ## ARPA Configuration

Note: Note: The date that automatically populates in the Medicare Credit End Date field is incorrect. Please set this field to 12/31/21.

The 2021 American Rescue Plan Act seems to state that employers can claim credit on Social Security taxes that were paid on COVID-19 qualified wages. In PrismHR, these credits are tracked under tax deduction code 00-87. Use this section to set up dates and billing events associated with the provisions of this act.

The two date fields are designed to increase your flexibility when handling payrolls that extend across the April 1 changeover date. You can adjust these dates as needed to ensure that the proper credits calculate on the proper payroll vouchers. For details, please review the suggestions and workarounds in the Introduction.

Setting — Description

Medicare Credit End Date — On any pay dates after the date entered here, the system will no longer calculate 00-82 credits for Medicare tax associated with FFCRA qualified wages. The default value is 03/31/21, but this is incorrect. To ensure compliance with the provisions of the ARPA, PrismHR strongly recommends setting this field to 12/31/21. > Note: Note: To avoid field validation errors, enter a value in the SS Credit Start Date field before you change the date in this field.

SS Credit Start Date — On any pay dates after the date entered here, the system will start accruing 00-87 credits for Social Security tax associated with FFCRA qualified wages. The system will not calculate 00-87 credits when this field is blank.

SS Credit Billing Event — Specify the billing event you want to associate with the new Social Security tax credit. If you do not have a billing event for this credit, you can create one on the Billing Events form.

COBRA Settings

See See Setting up System-level COBRA Parameters and See Manually Creating Client-level COBRA Subsidy Credits

2021 W-2 Box 14 Settings

Note: Note: To use these fields, you must enable the COVID-19 Wages on W-2 option (see above).

In 2021, the American Rescue Plan Act introduced a split in COVID-19 credit rules based around April 1, 2021. As a result, credits accrued prior to this date must display separately from credits accrued after this date, in Box 14 of the 2021 Form W-2.

Box 14 includes extra space for identifying the “other” amounts included there. Use the 2021 Sick and FMLA Box 14 Entry fields to enter custom identifiers for each of these credits. If you leave these fields blank, the system inserts preset, default text labels instead.

Note: Note: If you enter more than 12 characters in these fields, the remaining text will be cut off during in-house printing.

If you do perform in-house printing, PrismHR highly recommends performing test prints to ensure that all Box 14 entries display correctly, especially if you override the default text.

Reporting ARPA COBRA Subsidy Credits on the Employer Level of PTS and RTS Files

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/ARPA_COBRA_MasterTax.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/ARPA_COBRA_MasterTax.htm

Reporting ARPA COBRA Subsidy Credits on the Employer Level of PTS and RTS Files

You can use MasterTax interface settings to determine whether ARPA-related COBRA subsidy credits (tracked under tax deduction code 00-19) report on the employer level or the client level.

By default, the system displays these credits on the F-code/client/client federal entity level of these files. You can use the procedure below to override this.

To report 00-19 credits on the employer level of the RTS and PTS files:

  1. Click the Work Centers menu and select MasterTax Processing under Work Centers|Tax.

  2. Under Setup Forms, select MasterTax Interface Parameters.

  3. Locate the SBEA Tax Code Reporting Override panel.

  4. In the next available row of the SBEA Tax Codes table, select 00-19.

  5. Click Save.

========================================================================

Generating COBRA Subsidy Credits Automatically

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/Auto-Generate_COBRA_Credits.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/Auto-Generate_COBRA_Credits.htm

Generating COBRA Subsidy Credits Automatically

You can still create 00-19 tax adjustments manually, if necessary. But you can also configure the system-level COBRA settings and Participant Record settings so that the system automatically generates 00-19 credits when you create COBRA invoices.

To use these new features:

  1. Go to COBRA Administration > File > COBRA Global Setup Record.

  2. Select F9-Subsidy. The COBRA Subsidy Set-up form displays.

  3. Enter a Start Date of 4/1/2021 and a Stop Date of 9/30/2021.

  4. Enter 100 in the Subsidy Percent field.

  5. Click F2-Save.

  6. Next, go to COBRA Administration > File > Participant Record.

  7. Enter the appropriate Participant ID.

  8. Set Subsidy Percent to 100 and click F2-Save.

  9. Repeat for all applicable participants.

Next time you create COBRA invoices, the system will automatically generate 00-19 tax adjustments for all the affected participants who have this setup, and will then create a corresponding invoice line item to reflect the subsidy.

Viewing Refund Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/COVID_Bill_Pending.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/COVID_Bill_Pending.htm

Viewing Refund Data

The COVID-19 Refunds form displays a breakdown of all COVID-19-related client rebate credits. More specifically, this form reflects all COVID-19-related Client Bill Pending entries. It also allows you to add and edit existing entries to correctly account for COVID-19-related leave.

Note: Note: For reporting purposes, you can enter new data on this form without creating an associated client bill pending entry. To do this, enter all appropriate data except for the Billing Event.

To access this form, go to Client Details >Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

COVID-19 Refunds.

The form displays the total client bill Pending Amount at the top.

Adding an Entry

To add a new client bill pending entry:

  1. Click Add. The COVID-19 Refunds Maintenance form displays.

  2. Enter the Bill Date.

  3. Select the Type of COVID-19 credit for this entry.

  4. If you want to create a Bill Pending entry, enter the appropriate Billing Event Code. If you only want this data to display on reports without creating a true bill pending item, leave this field blank.

  5. Select a Location to associate with the entry.

  6. Enter the Amount of the entry.

  7. Click Save.

Editing an Entry

To edit an entry in this list:

  1. Select the Bill/Pay Date of the item you want to edit. The COVID-19 Refunds Maintenance form displays.

  2. Update the credit Type and Amount as needed.

  3. Click Save, then Save again.

Generating a Form 7200 in MasterTax

Service providers who use MasterTax and who have Form 941 liability carryover can generate a Form 7200 in MasterTax for this liability amount.

If you choose to do this, PrismHR recommends taking the following steps to ensure most accurate reporting:

To configure the system after creating a Form 7200 in MasterTax:

  1. Note the Bill Pending Amount at the top of the form. Record this value (write it down if necessary) and then delete it.

  2. Click Add to add a new line item.

  3. Set a Bill Date and Location as appropriate.

  4. Set the Type to Form 7200 Credit.

  5. Do not select “Bill” as this is a non-billed line item.

  6. Set the Amount equal to the Pending Amount you deleted in step 1.

  7. Click Save and then Save again.

Viewing Payroll Batch Calculation Variables

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/COVID_PayrollSteps.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/COVID_PayrollSteps.htm

Viewing Payroll Batch Calculation Variables

You can view each COVID-19 variable used in payroll batch calculations.

To display this form:

  1. Click the Payroll menu and select Payroll Control under Payroll|Change.

  2. Select a Payroll Number.

  3. Open the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select COVID Rebate Audit.

[Image: COVID Tax Credit Rebate Calculation Audit form, showing a breakdown of calculation steps. These include 941 tax liabilities, batch payroll credits for the various COVID credits, rebate amounts, pending rebates, and other calculations.]

The form displays a breakdown of COVID-19 credits and Form 941 liabilities, including the Pending Rebate amount displayed at the top of the COVID-19 Refunds form. Below these totals, the form displays all the major variables used to calculate the client rebate and carryover amount for the batch. Finally, it displays the total calculated rebate, any change to the pending rebate amount, and the final pending rebate amount.

What do these variables mean?

Variable — Description

Form 941 Tax Liability — Equal to the Tax Liability total displayed by the client Quarterly Tax Summary form. 941 tax liability = the tax amounts stored under the following codes: 00-10 (Federal Income Tax), 00-11 (FICA - Medicare), 00-12 (FICA - OASDI), 00-15 (Medicare - Employer side) and 00-16 (OASDI - Employer side).

Batch 941 Tax Liability — The sum of employee federal income tax, employee & employer Social Security tax, and employee & employer Medicare tax for the given payroll batch. You can view these deductions on the Payroll Check Journal Report.

Batch Medicare Credit — Value stored in tax deduction code 00-82. Equal to COVID wages multiplied by 0.0145 (unless altered by some other system-level calculation setting).

Batch Family First Payroll Credit — Value stored in tax deduction codes 00-80 and 00-81.

Batch CARES Credit — Value stored in tax deduction code 00-83. Equal to 50% of qualified wages as determined by the IRS. These qualified wages are paid through pay codes specified in the COVID-19 CARES Act Parameters form.

Batch Family First Health Credit — Value stored in tax deduction code 00-84.

Batch CARES Health Credit — Value stored in tax deduction code 00-85.

Total Credit AMT — The total of all credit amounts above.

QTD Form 941 Tax Liability — The Form 941 Tax Liability plus the Batch 941 Tax Liability, minus any quarter-to-date Medicare credit.

QTD Rebate — Quarter-to-date tax rebate total.

Batch Employer OASDI — This value can also be found on the Payroll Check Journal Report.

Pending Rebate — Calculation (with current payroll amount) of what is not yet rebated.

Available to Rebate — Pending rebate minus tax liabilities.

Calculated Gross Rebate — The total available rebate.

Calculated Add to Pending — The amount that should be added to the Pending Rebate. This value varies depending on the Default Tax Credit Cap Logic, which is set on the COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters form.

Calculated Carry Over — The amount that will be added to the carry-over bill pending line item (the item with the date 10/14/2241).

CARES Credit Rebate — Social Security credit based on COVID-19 qualified wage payments.

Recalculating COVID-19 Credits Retroactively

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/COVID_Recalc_Utility.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/COVID_Recalc_Utility.htm

Recalculating COVID-19 Credits Retroactively

WARNING: We strongly recommend running this utility in a test or demo environment first! This utility clears your entire COVID-related billing history and creates new, recalculated billing amounts. Please read the entire section carefully before running this utility. Contact PrismHR Support if you have questions or concerns.

Note: Note: As of the October 2020 off-cycle release, you can now use this utility to recalculate on vouchers that were paid when COVID-19 functionality was disabled.

Background

Some organizations might have implemented COVID-19 features after the official start of the legislation’s requirements. You can use a new tool to retroactively recalculate COVID-19 credits on these missed payrolls.

For a specified client, the recalculation utility cycles through the client’s payroll batches, starting with any batch that contains the Starting Pay Date. For each batch, the utility calculates the appropriate sick hours, credit accruals, and tax credits.

Important Considerations

If you choose to recalculate (and therefore delete) all prior COVID billing data, we recommend the following workaround strategies:

  • Using a screenshot or similar, make a note of all your existing refund data before running the utility. Then, after you recalculate, use the COVID-19 Refunds form (Viewing Refund Data) to re-enter your billing data as needed.

  • Run this utility in a test environment and then run the COVID-19 Credit Tracking Report in the test environment. Run the same report in your live environment and then compare the two reports and note any differences. Make adjustments to your live environment as needed.

Because this utility does not create tax adjustments, the data on your MasterTax RTS file may not match up with the data you passed into MasterTax over the course of the quarter. There are two major workarounds for this issue:

  • Manually make the appropriate tax adjustments based on the results of the recalculation utility.

  • Pass the data to MasterTax using your RTS file and then reconcile at the end of the quarter.

Recalculation Options

If you do not want the utility to recalculate everything as describe above, you can use several options to limit the extent of the work done during recalculation. Enter Y in any of these fields, as appropriate, if you want the utility to recalculate that data.

Note: Note: Messages will only display in the Rebate Calculation Audit Results window if you choose to recalculate Billing. By design, messages do not display in this window if you set Billing (rebates) to N, even if you select any of the other recalculation options.

Option Description Affected Tax Codes
Billing (rebates) You must confirm that you want the system to delete all COVID-related billing data as described above. Otherwise, the data is not deleted. N/A
CARES Retention The system recalculates CARES Act Employee Retention credits and CARES Act Healthcare credits. If you are running this utility to account for one or more ERC exclusion periods (see Setting Up CARES Act Compliance), PrismHR recommends first running the ERC Recalc Report. See Employee Retention Credit Recalc Report for details. 00-83 and 00-85
Sick & Family Leave The system recalculates credits associated with COVID-19 FMLA and sick leave (self care and dependent care). 00-80 and 00-81
Medicare The system recalculates credits associated with Medicare taxes. 00-82
Families First The system recalculates credits associated with Families First Act healthcare costs. 00-84

Running the Utility

Note: Note: If you are using this utility to recalculate CARES Act credits after setting up ERC exclusion dates, PrismHR recommends running the ERC Recalc Report in preparation. See Employee Retention Credit Recalc Report.

Note: Note: This utility updates existing vouchers and does not create new tax adjustments. If you want the tax registers to reflect these updated tax values, you must rebuild client tax registers AND (if you enabled the SBEA feature set) Client Federal Entity tax registers.

To run the recalculation utility:

  1. Navigate to System Administration > Process > Utility Processing > Payroll Utilities > Recalculate COVID-19 Tax Credits/Client Rebates.

  2. Enter a Company ID. You must run this utility one client at a time.

  3. Enter a Starting Pay Date.

  4. Enter a Y the appropriate recalculation options (see the table above).

  5. Click F5-Process to run the utility.

Deferring Employee Social Security Tax

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/COVID-19%20Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/COVID_Social_Security_Deferral.htm Path: CMP/COVID-19 Docs/COVID-Utilities-and-Tools/COVID_Social_Security_Deferral.htm

Deferring Employee Social Security Tax

Note: Note: Known Issue. The Garnishment Report currently incorporates 00-86 values into the Disposable Income calculation for any employee who defers this tax. However, this does not impact the actual garnishment calculation.

On August 8, President Trump authorized “Memorandum on Deferring Payroll Tax Obligations in Light of the Ongoing COVID-19 Disaster”. This executive order enables employees to defer Social Security tax payments between September 1 and December 31, 2020, with some exceptions. Because the language in this order is brief and nonspecific, PrismHR developed new features to support this new requirement as generally as possible.

These features may be developed further as more concrete information becomes available, especially regarding downstream reporting (such as changes to Form 941 and Form W-2). We will address any such changes once new formats and/or requirements are established.

You can use PrismHR to defer this tax on either the client or employee level:

  • Payroll vouchers within this time period include a new tax deduction code entry, 00-86, for any employee who defers the tax.

  • 00-86 stores a value equal to the negative of the employee’s 00-12 tax value.

  • This data accrues in Client Federal Entity or Employer tax registers (whichever stores federal tax data for your organization) and appears on the MasterTax PTS and RTS files under the appropriate E or F code.

Background

Settings for employee Social Security tax deferral exist on both the employee and client level. The client-level setting is located under Client Details >Actions menu

[Image: screenshot]

COVID-19 Client Parameters. The employee-level setting is located on the Employee Details > Other tab. If you do not alter these settings, they default to “no deferral” (normal withholding).

On the client level, you can elect to defer the tax for all employees. On the employee level, you can elect to respect the client-level setting (this is the default employee setting), or you can override the client-level setting for any given employee: namely, you can either defer the tax or withhold as normal, regardless of the setting on the client level.

In other words, the employee-level setting controls whether or not that employee’s Social Security tax will be deferred. And one of the employee-level options (Use Client Level Setting) just respects the client configuration.

Employee-Level Deferral

Employees default to the client-level setting unless they are configured otherwise.

To defer Social Security tax for an individual employee:

  1. Navigate to the employee’s Employee Details > Other tab.

  2. In the new 2020 COVID-19 Social Security Deferral field, select whether you want to

  • Defer 2020 EE [Employee] SS Tax Accruals

  • Withhold 2020 EE SS Tax Accruals

  • Use Client Level Setting (see below)

  1. Save the record when you finish.

[Image: The field “2020 COVID-19 Social Security Deferral” is highlighted. The field is set to “Use Client Level Setting”.]

Client-Level Deferral

Client-level settings apply to all employees associated with that client, except for any employees configured otherwise (see above).

To defer Social Security tax for all employees of a particular client:

  1. Open the Client menu and select Client Details under Client|Change.

  2. Open the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select COVID-19 Client Parameters.

  3. Select 2020 Executive Order Social Security Deferral.

  4. Click Save.

[Image: System/Change/COVID-19 Client Parameters form with focus on the field “2020 Executive Order Social Security Deferral”]

Payroll Voucher Example

Note: Note the addition of the 00-86 entry on the payroll voucher below. The value associated with this line item is the negative of the value in the line above it, which represents the employee’s Social Security tax.

[Image: Employee Tax panel of payroll voucher. Tax codes 00-12 (OASDI EE) and 00-86 (SS Payroll Tax Deferral) are highlighted.]

Setting the $4000 Employee Social Security Deferral Limit

New guidance from the Department of the Treasury and the IRS states that employees who make $4000 or more in a bi-weekly pay period are not eligible for Social Security tax deferral under the new executive order. You can use a system-level setting to enforce this limit.

Note: Note: This setting only effects employees who are flagged to default to the client-level Social Security deferral setting (and where the client-level deferral setting is enabled). It does not affect employees who are set to defer or withhold this tax on the employee level (see example image below).

Client-level setting

[Image: COVID-19 client parameters form, with the “2020 Executive Order Social Security Deferral” field enabled.]

Employee-level setting

[Image: Another screenshot of the employee-level setting, “2020 COVID-19 Social Security Deferral”]

Background

This setting takes effect during payroll processing. After you enable it, the system does not create 00-86 entries if an employee’s payroll voucher exceeds a system-calculated threshold. This threshold varies based on the employee’s Pay Period:

Pay Period Amount
Weekly $2000
Bi-weekly $4000
Semi-monthly $4333.33
Monthly $8666.66

Procedure

To set the Social Security tax withholding limit:

  1. Click the Back Office menu.

  2. Go to System|Change > System Parameters.

  3. Open the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and select COVID-19 Emergency Processing Parameters.

  4. Select EE Deferral 4K Check.

  5. Click Save.

SBEA Overview

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Background/About_SBEA.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Background/About_SBEA.htm

SBEA Overview

Introduction to Certified PEOs and the Small Business Efficiency Act

The IRS provides a voluntary program through which PEOs can apply for certification, under the Tax Increase Prevention Act of 2014. This act includes within it the Small Business Efficiency Act, or SBEA. A certified PEO under this system is called a CPEO.

Registering as a CPEO provides additional institutional legitimacy to PEOs by requiring them to:

  • Maintain a bond with the IRS for the year

  • Submit annual, independent CPA audits to the IRS

  • Submit independently certified, quarterly assertions to the IRS demonstrating payment of employment taxes

  • Pay an annual fee to remain certified

The act also provides benefits for both CPEOs and their clients:

  • Guarantees that PEO clients still qualify for any applicable federal tax credits

  • Removes any federal payroll tax liability from the PEO’s clients

  • Guarantees that clients are not double-taxed if they join or leave a CPEO mid-year

  • Provides the CPEO with a federal tax credit for the SUTA taxes they pay

SBEA Reporting Requirements

Every year, CPEOs must submit two additional forms to the IRS. These forms detail PEO tax liabilities, broken out client by client:

  • Form 940 Schedule R: specifies federal unemployment tax (FUTA)

  • Form 941 Schedule R: specifies federal income tax, OASDI (Social Security) tax, and Medicare taxes

For more information about these forms, consult this page on the IRS website.

========================================================================

Form W-2 for SBEA

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Background/Form_W2_for_SBEA.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Background/Form_W2_for_SBEA.htm

Form W-2 for SBEA

Note: Note: If your organization decides to create Form W-2 files using the setup for certified PEOs, you must contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team and request that your system be enabled for SBEA W-2s.

The Form W-2 features in the system can support SBEA-certified PEOs, breaking out the information by employer and then by client federal entity. They also support internal clients and clients not in a certified relationship, breaking the information out by employer only.

Important Notice About Enabling SBEA Features

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Background/Important_Notice_About_Enabling_SBEA_Features.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Background/Important_Notice_About_Enabling_SBEA_Features.htm

Important Notice About Enabling SBEA Features

Make sure you and your organization understand the implications of enabling the SBEA feature set in the system.

Note: Note: Only perform SBEA setup and client-level Form W-2 processing if your organization has a total understanding of all components of the SBEA features.

  • Before enabling the SBEA feature set, and especially before any register rebuild, PrismHR strongly advises that you test everything in a demo environment.

  • Perform the setup on a “quiet system” (during off-peak use hours).

  • Setting up SBEA features will transfer accrued federal tax liability from the employer level to “client entity” level. Be extremely cautious if you need to make such a change mid-year.

  • If you do make a change mid-year, note that your organization must rebuild the client entity registers. Consult PrismHR before making such a change.

  • Note that inserting any retroactive employer-certified start dates or client entity-certified contract start dates does not adjust payroll data for the year prior to the current date. Your organization must rebuild the register in that instance; be sure that this is what you should to do.

  • SBEA setup makes no changes to your current billing structure. PrismHR strongly recommends that you use client-level billing in conjunction with your SBEA setup.

  • If your organization intends to break out Forms W-2 by client entity:

  • Note that the system separates the forms by entity by employer, displaying the client entity name and FEIN.

  • If you will use a third-party download or e‑File, note that you might need to adjust the files to group together client records across employers or to add some employer indicator to each client entity group.

Setting Up SBEA Compliance in PrismHR

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Background/SBEA_Procedure_and_CFE.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Background/SBEA_Procedure_and_CFE.htm

Setting Up SBEA Compliance in PrismHR

Before setting up SBEA functionality, make sure you understand the following important concepts and features.

Client Federal Entities

The SBEA requires CPEOs to break out federal taxes client by client during IRS reporting. PrismHR provides a number of features to help you comply with these reporting requirements. After completing the setup process, this functionality will be implemented in your system:

  • Federal taxes are broken out by client.

  • The gross-to-net calculation on an employee’s payroll voucher uses a new register for limit testing: the Client Federal Entity Tax Register. Originally, the system used employer tax registers during this calculation.

In the PrismHR system, a client federal entity (CFE) represents a client of your organization that is included in SBEA reporting. Each client federal entity corresponds to a specific client FEIN used in this reporting. During SBEA setup, the system creates a new CFE ID for each of these clients.

During the year, federal taxes accrue on the appropriate Client Federal Entity Tax Registers. Since SBEA-related reports pull data from these registers, you can break out federal taxes client-by-client for IRS reporting.

Overview of SBEA Setup in PrismHR

SBEA feature setup is a multi-step process, but PrismHR provides several automated utilities to simplify this process. Refer to the following table and diagram for a high-level overview of the process.

[Image: A flowchart of the SBEA setup process, which is described in detail below]

Complete the following steps to set up SBEA functionality in PrismHR:

Step Description Notes Link
1 Enable the SBEA feature set New fields become available on the Employer Details form. Enabling the SBEA Feature Set
2 Flag employers as certified Update employer information to prepare for inclusion in client federal entity tax registers. Flagging Employers as Certified PEOs
3 Create and assign client federal entity IDs to certified employers Ensures that federal taxes are associated with the correct client. Creating Client Federal Entities
4 Run the SBEA Voucher Update Utility. One-time procedure to correct any vouchers that need to be included in SBEA reporting for the year. Updating Vouchers with SBEA Information
5 Rebuild the client federal entity tax register. This ensures accurate tax reporting that includes the updated vouchers from step 4. Rebuilding SBEA Client Federal Entity Tax Registers

Using SBEA Features

After you run the voucher update utility and rebuild client federal entity tax registers, the system will be ready to process tax information according to SBEA guidelines.

During payroll initialization, the system posts the CFE ID and an SBEA wage code to each payroll voucher. It then copies that voucher’s federal tax data to the appropriate client federal entity tax register. Later, when you run SBEA-related reports, PrismHR uses the different CFE IDs to break out this tax data properly.

Client Federal Entity Tax Register Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Reports/CFE-Tax-Register-Rpt.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Reports/CFE-Tax-Register-Rpt.htm

Client Federal Entity Tax Register Report

Note: Note: This report is only for CPEO service providers using the SBEA feature set.

During reconciliation and quarterly tax processing, use the Client Federal Entity Tax Register Report to output client entity tax registers and identify whether or not values are spread across one or more employers.

Procedure

To run the report:

  1. Enter a Year.

  2. If necessary, select a Quarter to view data from that quarter, or select YTD Only to display all data from the start of the year to the current date.

  3. Enter a Tax Code:

  • 00-10: Federal Income Tax

  • 00-11: Medicare – Employee

  • 00-12: Social Security – Employee

  • 00-15: Medicare – Employer

  • 00-16: Social Security – Employer

  • 00-17: FUTA – Employer

  1. In the Include SBEA Wage Code Breakout field, select:

  • No: does not display F codes on the report

  • Yes: displays F codes on the report

  1. Specify an Employer ID to restrict the report to Client Federal Entities under that employer.

  2. In the Report Type field, select either Employee Detail to generate the full report, or Summary to generate only the F code subtotals by tax code.

  3. In the Client Federal Entity (CFE) field, enter specific CFE IDs to include in the report. If this field is blank, the report includes all CFE IDs.

  4. Select Run. The report displays.

Form W-2 Reconciliation Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Reports/Form_W2_Reconciliation_Report.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Reports/Form_W2_Reconciliation_Report.htm

Form W-2 Reconciliation Report

If your organization reports on SBEA-certified employers and files Forms W-2 at the employer level, you might see some unexpected results. This is because, under the SBEA, tax accruals and limits are respected at the client federal entity level. If you create Forms W‑2 at the employer level and an employee worked for two clients under the same employer, their data will be combined on a single Form W‑2.

The W-2 Reconciliation Report enables you to find employees who have Medicare or Social Security taxes greater than expected, based on their taxable amounts.

To run the Form W-2 Reconciliation Report:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu and select W-2 Processing under Work Centers|Tax.

  2. Under W-2 Reporting, select W-2 Reconciliation Report.

  3. Enter the Employer ID.

  4. Enter the Tax Year.

  5. Click Run. The report generates.

The report displays any employees whose withholdings exceed the limits. Your organization can then determine how best to reconcile the data on those forms.
