MasterTax Files

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Reports/MasterTax_Files.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Reports/MasterTax_Files.htm

MasterTax Files

Customers who enabled the SBEA feature set can also use the MasterTax PTS and RTS files to report their tax data in accordance with the act. When properly configured, the system will create F codes on the MasterTax reporting files, identifying the federal tax data based on the associated client federal entity.

Note: Note: Before submitting your files with MasterTax, you must alert MasterTax Support that you are a PrismHR customer and that you are planning to submit SBEA files. If you do not, you could be charged additional fees by MasterTax.

PTS File

The PTS file breaks out federal tax data by client. If the client is associated with multiple CPEO employers, the client will be associated with multiple F codes on the reporting files. This helps to link voucher data based on the common pay agent (the employer).

Important MasterTax Interface Settings

  • Provide SBEA Payroll Breakout: If this field is enabled, the PTS file will break out the federal tax basis and liability amounts by client federal entity.

  • Include Certified Relationship Setups: If this field is enabled, the PTS file output will include the 210 (company setup) records that link the client federal entity to the employer. The system creates the 210 record specifically for employers. On the 210 record for each employer, position 45 indicates the EIN Type. When this value is P (as shown below) it indicates that the employer is a common pay parent.

  • The 9-digit number after the P is the employer FEIN.

  • Likewise, on the 210 record for the client federal entity (F code), position 45 will be a C, which denotes the client as a common pay child. The next 9 digits are the EIN for the associated employer.

[Image: Detail of MasterTax PTS file showing the 000, 100, 201, and 210 records. Positions 45 through 54 are highlighted, indicating common pay parent or child status, and the FEIN of the common pay agent.]

Note: Note: In order for the system to associate the 210 records with the F codes, you must enable Send Company Tax Record in PTS File on the MasterTax Interface Parameters. Activating this setting can cause issues in MasterTax if the FEIN and Unemployment ID numbers in PrismHR do not match the FEIN and unemployment IDs in Mastertax. If this describes your situation, PrismHR recommends using the SBEA Relationship file instead of performing company setup on your daily PTS file (see below).

RTS File

The MasterTax RTS (reconciliation tax summary) file accommodates client federal entities for 941 R reporting.

Note: Note: PrismHR is working with MasterTax to support the 940 R; MasterTax does not support the 943 R.

To support the RTS file, you must set your MasterTax RTS version to 36 or higher. see Setting MasterTax Interface Parameters for more information.

About the File

Effective January 2020, SBEA employers can enable the Build W-2 Records? parameter to break out Form W-2 reporting records on the RTS download.

The Build FUTA Exempt records? field can only be used with MasterTax versions 37 and above.

To build the RTS file:

  1. On the Work Centers menu and select MasterTax Processing. Then, under File Exports, select Mastertax RTS File Build.

  2. Complete these fields as required:

Field Description
Tax Year Tax year for this build.
Tax Quarter Tax quarter for this build.
Limit to Employer Filters so that the file generated only contains tax information for a specific employer.
Include client reportable taxes for PEO clients Client-reported taxes that are under the client’s FEIN or unemployment ID. This only displays when the Limit to Employer field has a set value.
Limit to Client Client ID that this build is limited to. This only applies to client SUTA reporting states.
Build FUTA Exempt Records This only applies when running an RTS build for the fourth quarter.
Exclude Pennsylvania local taxes when no tax has been withheld Enable to exclude PA local taxes if no tax has been withheld.
Include zero taxable wages for Ohio non-resident local tax Enable to include zero taxable wages for Ohio non-resident local tax.
Limit to ASO/ASA/PAY service types Enable to limit this build to ASO/ASA/PAY service types.
Build SBEA certified relationship file Enable to build an SBEA certified relationship file.
Use tax code 00-87 to determine SS credit within MasterTax? > Note: Note: This setting applies to 2021 COVID-19 tax reporting. If selected, the system performs the following calculation during the RTS build: (00-87 tax amount associated with line item) / (0.062). After computing this value, the system uses this value to fill in Statistic 1 of the 501 record for tax code 00-81, up to the value reported under 00-81 on the 500 record. If any amount remains after reaching the 00-81 reporting value, the system enters the remainder into Statistic 1 of the 501 record for tax code 00-80. For more details about these tax codes, see the COVID-19 Feature Guide.
Use COVID Credits as post March 31? > Note: Note: This setting applies to 2021 COVID-19 tax reporting. The federal government makes a distinction, for reporting purposes, between COVID-19 credits accrued before and after April 1 2021, since this is the date the American Rescue Plan Act (ARP or ARPA) took effect. Select this option to consider any COVID-19 credits accrued in Q2 2021 as “post-March 31” credits, which means they were accrued subject to the American Rescue Plan Act. These credits will be reported accordingly on the RTS file. On the file itself, this means: - On 501 records for 00-80 and 00-81, the full value under these tax codes populates under statistic 1. - On FE0000-116H and FE0000-117H records, statistic 1 is set to 0. For more details about reporting COVID-19 tax credits, see the COVID-19 Feature Guide.
  • On 501 records for 00-80 and 00-81, the full value under these tax codes populates under statistic 1.
  • On FE0000-116H and FE0000-117H records, statistic 1 is set to 0. | RTS File History | You can save and download historical files with the following information: - The date and time the process was started - The user who started the process - A hyperlink to download the file - SBEA Relationship file - RTS file summary report - RTS file detail report |
  • The date and time the process was started
  • The user who started the process
  • A hyperlink to download the file
  • SBEA Relationship file
  • RTS file summary report
  • RTS file detail report | Configuration Information | Displays the MasterTax version number. You cannot build an RTS file unless you are using MasterTax Version 39 or greater. |
  1. Click Build. A progress window displays. You can download an RTS file, or view an audit detail or summary report.

Note: Note: The SBEA Certified Relationship File includes only the 100, 200, and 210 records. The file has the payroll codes for the client federal entity (a, b, or c designation) as well as all federal tax codes, including AG codes.

CPEO e-Filing

Note: Note: To file your 940/941 Schedule R correctly, MasterTax now requires Federal Withholding and Unemployment tax data at the client level.

To file as a CPEO in MasterTax, you must contact MasterTax Support to verify that you have a MasterTax PEO license. If you do not, you will need to convert your MasterTax license to a MasterTax PEO license. MasterTax will make every attempt to ensure your invoice does not increase as a result of switching to this new license type. Please have the following information ready when you contact MasterTax Support:

  • Number of Employer/PEO companies

  • Number of total employees

  • Number of clients.

MasterTax will provide you with the new monthly fee (if it is not the same as your existing monthly fee) and the additional client setup fee associated with adding new clients to MasterTax.

The PTS import file creates the requisite client structure within MasterTax. The import assigns new client payroll codes (which begin with the letter F) to existing clients that were previously set up within MasterTax, and creates new companies for clients that have not been set up. A client company record would typically already be set up in MasterTax for any client in a client reporting state. The file will also create the Common Pay Agent link to link the Employer to the Client for Federal tax codes so that when MasterTax creates the 941 Schedule R for the Employer, it will have the appropriate client information.

Before importing this file into MasterTax, we strongly recommend reviewing your current configuration within MasterTax by navigating to Company View, selecting a Company, and clicking the FEIN link under the Company tab

Note: Note the following implications of the import:

  • All new client payroll codes on the file with the same FEIN will link together under a single company record within MasterTax;

  • This company record will have a Common Pay Agent link to 1 employer;

  • If a client company with the same FEIN already exists in MasterTax, any new client payroll code(s) with that FEIN will link under this existing client company record;

  • If a client company with the same FEIN does not already exist in MasterTax, any new client payroll code(s) with that FEIN will create and link under a new client company record.

e-Filing with Quarterly Express Plus

As an alternative to e-filing with MasterTax, PrismHR offers the ability to e-file your Schedule R Form 941 return with Quarterly Express Plus (QEXPlus).

To export and process the QEX Plus 941 file:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, click Work Centers|Tax > Quarterly Tax Processing, and under 3rd-Party Exports select 941 File Processing.

  2. Complete these fields as required:

Field — Description

Employer ID — Click the link and select the ID of the employer whose information is being uploaded and processed.

Year — Year for the tax information.

Quarter — Quarter of the year for this tax information. For example, 1 indicates first quarter, and so forth.

Include Schedule R? — Generates a third, optional file that breaks out federal taxes by client. Enabling this field displays the Schedule R Report field.

Deposit Schedule — Specifies the frequency at which the employer makes deposits. Options are Monthly and Semi-Monthly.

941 Worksheet — Builds the Form 941 worksheet.

941 Schedule B — Builds Form 941 Schedule B.

Schedule R Report — Builds the Schedule R report.

  1. Click Build.

To use this download for future Schedule R Form 941 filings, contact N. Wayne Lewis of Lewis Software Associates, LLC.

  • Email: nlewis@qexplus.com

  • Phone: 1-877-854-0100

  • Company Website: www.qexplus.com

Build W-2s From Client Federal Tax Registers

Build W-2s From Client Federal Tax Registers

Note: Note: Important! The Form W-2/SBEA breakout feature was designed for a very specific use case that originates from vagueness in IRS reporting guidelines for CPEOs. Only enable this feature if you encounter this use case and need to account for it. If you want to leverage this feature, PrismHR highly recommends performing appropriate testing first, to ensure that it will have no adverse effect on your data in the context of reporting or E-filing. If you need more information, please contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

If your organization is a Certified PEO (CPEO), you can declare the entity under which Forms W‑2 and Forms 1099 are processed in PrismHR. Your organization can select the Name, Address, and EIN for the client, employer, or both. These changes reflect on Form W‑2 PDF generation, BDS/CIC downloads, printed Forms W‑2, and printed Forms 1099.

Note: Note: This functionality only works if SBEA features are enabled. See Enabling the SBEA Feature Set.

To build W-2s from client federal tax registers:

  1. Open System|Change > Global Configuration from the Back Office menu.

  2. Select Build W2s From Client Federal Tax Registers.

  3. Click Save.

  2. In the SBEA W2/1099 Name Control panel:

  • Enter the appropriate Year.

  • Select the Name, Address, and EIN you want. These values will display on Forms W‑2 and Forms 1099.

Flagging Employers as Certified PEOs

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Certified_PEO_Employer.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Certified_PEO_Employer.htm

Flagging Employers as Certified PEOs

After you enable SBEA features, you can designate employers as certified PEOs and specify a range of dates for collecting SBEA-related tax information. After you indicate that an employer is SBEA-certified, you must complete the setup process to run any payrolls that include a client or employee assigned to this employer — either as primary or alternate.

To designate an employer as a CPEO:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. Under Employer|Change, select the Employer Details form.

  3. Enter the Employer ID. Employer information populates the form.

  4. Select Certified PEO. This indicates that the employer is certified as a PEO by the IRS.

  5. Enter the Certified Start Date. This is the date the certification went into effect.

SBEA tracking begins with payrolls where the pay date is on or after this date.

  1. If the certification expires, you can also enter the Certified End Date.

  2. Click Save.

Limit Testing on Employer Register

After you enable the SBEA feature set, the system automatically performs limit testing using data from the client federal entity tax registers. However, if an employee works for multiple clients under the same employer, the limit testing does not take this into account.

Use the Limit Testing on Employer Register field to adjust this behavior so that the system checks the employer register, rather than the client federal entity register, during this testing.

Consider the following example:

  • John Doe works for both Client A and Client B, both under Employer 1.

  • John’s federal tax cutoff is $7000.

  • John earns $5000 at Client A and $5000 at Client B.

The portion of John’s taxed income will depend on which register the system checks. This depends on how you have set up the system:

Employer is Not Marked as a CPEO

If Certified PEO is not selected, the system calculates taxes for all $5000 of John’s Client A wages and only $2000 of John’s Client B wages, or $7000 total. This is the correct tax limit.

Employer is a CPEO, Limit Testing on Employer Register is Disabled

If Certified PEO is selected and Limit Testing on Employer Register is not selected, John pays taxes on all $5000 from both clients. The system does not consider Client B’s wages along with Client A’s wages because they are stored in different client federal entity tax registers. Therefore, the system does not take into account that John has reached his tax limit.

Employer is a CPEO and Limit Testing on Employer Register is Enabled

If Certified PEO and Limit Testing on Employer Register are both selected, the system calculates taxes for all $5000 of John’s Client A wages and $2000 of John’s Client B wages, or $7000 total, the same as if Certified PEO were not selected.

Creating Client Federal Entities

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Client_Federal_Entities.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Client_Federal_Entities.htm

Creating Client Federal Entities

When SBEA features are enabled, you must have a client federal entity associated with each client FEIN serviced by your certified PEO. Tax liabilities accrue on client federal entities; these entities also determine relevant client tax limits during payroll calculations.

Client federal entities represent a separate level of reporting, different from the employer- and client-level reporting that otherwise exists in the system.

Relation to Client Entities

In most cases, a client federal entity is a reflection of one client record in your system. However, there are some exceptions:

  • If multiple client records share the same FEIN, they would be associated with one client federal entity.

  • If a client’s various worksite locations have their own FEINs, they would each need a different client federal entity.

Procedure

Note: Note: If your organization has just enabled the SBEA feature set and needs to set up client federal entities for the first time, there is a utility you can use: see Creating Client Federal Entities. After enabling the SBEA feature set, each new client record will automatically generate a new client federal entity.

To set up a client federal entity:

  1. Open Employer|Change|Client Federal Entities from the Back Office menu .

  2. Enter the numeric Entity ID. This can match the ID of the client to which it will be assigned (unless that number is already in use).

  3. Enter the Entity Name.

  4. Enter the nine-digit Federal EIN (employer identification number) assigned to the employer by the IRS. Use the format ##-#######.

  5. Enter the address associated with the FEIN in the Address, City, State, and ZIP Code fields.

Users can indicate the time periods when the client company was in a certified relationship with the PEO, as defined by the SBEA.

  1. In the SBEA Certified Relationship Periods panel:

  • Enter the Starting Date when the client entered into a certified relationship with the PEO.

  • Enter the Ending Date of the period during which the client was in a certified relationship with the PEO, if appropriate.

  1. Click Save.

========================================================================

Important Note About Creating New Clients

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Client_Federal_Entity_Created_For_New_Clients.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Client_Federal_Entity_Created_For_New_Clients.htm

Important Note About Creating New Clients

If your organization has enabled the SBEA feature set, the system automatically creates a client federal entity when users create a new client record, and automatically associates the entity with the new client. If the new client shares an FEIN with an existing client, you must edit the new client record and set it to the same client federal entity ID as the existing client.

Note: Note: The system does not populate the Starting Date in the SBEA Certified Relationship Periods table. Users must manually enter the date on which the client entered into the certified relationship with the employer in the Client Federal Entities form.

About Client Federal Entity Tax Registers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Client_Federal_Entity_Tax_Register.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Client_Federal_Entity_Tax_Register.htm

About Client Federal Entity Tax Registers

Client federal entity tax registers track tax information for a specific federal entity, for SBEA reporting purposes.

PrismHR supports SBEA requirements for updating the client federal entity tax registers.

  • The Transfer Tax Basis and Transfer Tax Basis Import utilities support the SBEA feature set. These tools adjust employee tax bases and amounts, and they account for successor wages and the adjustment data flows to the client federal entity.

  • Transfer Tax Basis Entry is in the Payroll menu under Payroll|Change.see Transfer Tax Basis Entry.

  • Transfer Tax Basis Import (From Successor) is in the Back Office menu under Operations|Import Processing. See Importing Employee Tax Basis from Successor.

  • Tax Adjustment Entry also adjusts client federal entity registers, correctly apportioning the changes. This form is in the Payroll menu under Payroll|Change. see Processing Tax Adjustments to Tax Register.

Creating Client Federal Entities

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Create-Client-Fed-Entities.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Create-Client-Fed-Entities.htm

Creating Client Federal Entities

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

During first-time SBEA implementation, you can use the SBEA Auto Create Client Federal Entities form to create and assign client federal entity tax registers. This process assigns the “Certified PEO” flag on Employer Details. After this, the system creates and assigns client federal entity IDs to clients.

These fields are identical to what is defined under the client ID: name, address, city, state, ZIP code, and FEIN. The client federal entity ID is the same as the client ID.

To create client federal entities:

  1. Click Work Centers, then go to Tax > SBEA Processing > Setup > SBEA Auto Create Client Federal Entities.

  2. Select an Employer ID (the employer must be a Certified PEO).

  3. Enter a Certified Relationship Start Date, which is the date the contract was signed.

This controls the SBEA wage code. Wage code A will be on any voucher after this start date. If the date is left blank, Wage code C is used.

  1. Click Run. A report displays with the processing steps, number of records created, and the date and time the process ran.

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Enable_SBEA_Feature_Set.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Enable_SBEA_Feature_Set.htm

Enabling the SBEA Feature Set

To begin setting up SBEA features in PrismHR, you must enable SBEA functionality on the global level. After enabling these settings, the system adds new fields to the Employer Details form. Use these fields to designate an employer as certified and set up basic SBEA reporting information.

[Image: Detail of the Global Configuration form. The “Enable SBEA Feature Set” field is highlighted and enabled.]

To enable the SBEA features in PrismHR:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. Under System|Change, select Global Configuration.

  3. Select the Enable SBEA Feature Set option. This turns on the features that support SBEA compliance.

  4. Click Save.

After saving, proceed to Flagging Employers as Certified PEOs

Exclude Internal Employees from SBEA Reporting

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Exclude_Internal_EEs_from_SBEA_Reporting.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Exclude_Internal_EEs_from_SBEA_Reporting.htm

Exclude Internal Employees from SBEA Reporting

Your organization can exclude clients from SBEA reporting. For example, a PEO can exclude its own internal (or “corporate”) employees.

Employees of excluded clients will not appear in any client federal entity register. Note that, on the MasterTax files, employee data for internal clients displays as employer data and also displays on line 13 of Form 941 Schedule R.

To specify the clients to exclude:

  1. Open System Parameters from the Back Office menu under System|Change.

  2. Open the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and then select Miscellaneous Parameters. The Miscellaneous Global Parameters form opens.

  3. Enter the Internal Client ID for each client used for internal employees.

  4. Click Accept.

  5. Click Save at the bottom of the main System Parameters form.

[Image: Miscellaneous Global Parameters form, with the Internal Client IDs grid highlighted. An internal client ID is listed in the grid.]

PrismHR recommends setting this to the highest possible value, which corresponds to the most recent supported version. | | Mastertax File ID | Enter the unique three-digit file identifier provided by MasterTax. | | Mastertax Customer ID | Optional: Enter the Access ID provided by MasterTax. | | Mastertax Group Code | Use this code to differentiate PTS files when you save them in multiple accounts. The structure of the code is: MTAXPTS-XXXX-YYYYMMDD where XXXX is the account or directory where you saved the PTS file. | | Mastertax Account ID | Optional: Enter a prefix that appears in company and employer IDs in the MasterTax download files. This prefix differentiates between clients and employers that have matching IDs across different databases. | | Send Company Tax Record In PTS File | Enable this item to include a company 210 record in the PTS file. | | Send Company Accounting Record in PTS file | If you are a PEO managing multiple client accounts, you can now populate the Company General Ledger record 105 within the PTS file for both SBEA and non-SBEA clients. - This field is unchecked by default. - Check this field to report the 105 record within the PTS. Once checked the following two fields display: - MasterTax Chart of Account Code - Enter the 2 digit alphanumeric code used to identify the company’s G/L chart of accounts. - MasterTax G/L Payroll Code - the only drop-down option available at this time is Sub Cost Center. |
  • This field is unchecked by default.
  • Check this field to report the 105 record within the PTS. Once checked the following two fields display:
  • MasterTax Chart of Account Code - Enter the 2 digit alphanumeric code used to identify the company’s G/L chart of accounts.
  • MasterTax G/L Payroll Code - the only drop-down option available at this time is Sub Cost Center. | Mastertax Wage Reporting Utilized | Optional: Enable this item to use the MasterTax Wage Reporting module. If this item is not enabled, the system does not send employee detail records with the RTS file. | | Build FUTA Taxable Register by State | Enable this item to build FUTA taxable wage records by state (tax code XX-17). This was used for the FUTA credit for the federal government after the recession. If left unchecked, register records are built at the end of the year. | | Override Payment Method | Select the appropriate method: - No Override Payment Method - Check - EFT Credit - EFT Debit (sent in positions 123-124 on the PTS 210 record) |
  • No Override Payment Method
  • Check
  • EFT Credit
  • EFT Debit (sent in positions 123-124 on the PTS 210 record) | Additional Medicare Calc Method | This option is only intended for organizations that have encountered issues reconciling Additional Medicare taxes for employees who worked under multiple employers, but a single Client Federal Entity. If this describes your use case, enable this setting to implement alternative Additional Medicare tax calculation logic, specifically designed to avoid this issue. | | Include Pennsylvania Work State Flag (Y/N) | This field is optional. This field is a checkbox. It is selected by default. - Checked: - The MasterTax (v47 and above) RTS will include the Work Out of State Flag as a Y or N in position 15 for the 500 (Reconciliation Amounts), 501 (Employee Statistics), 601 (Employee Wage), and 616 (Employee State and Local W-2) records for Pennsylvania resident tax codes not including Philadelphia. - The MasterTax (v29 and above) PTS will include the Work Out of State Flag as a Y or N in position 15 for the 210 (Company Tax), 500 (Payroll Tax Detail) records for Pennsylvania resident tax codes not including Philadelphia. This does not apply to Philadelphia residents, and the position will remain blank for Philadelphia and all other locality taxes. - Unchecked: - The MasterTax (v47 and above) RTS will NOT include the Work Out of State Flag in the download. - The MasterTax (v29 and above) PTS will NOT include the Work Out of State Flag in the download. > Note: Note: This will apply for both SBEA and non-SBEA clients. |
  • Checked:
  • The MasterTax (v47 and above) RTS will include the Work Out of State Flag as a Y or N in position 15 for the 500 (Reconciliation Amounts), 501 (Employee Statistics), 601 (Employee Wage), and 616 (Employee State and Local W-2) records for Pennsylvania resident tax codes not including Philadelphia.
  • The MasterTax (v29 and above) PTS will include the Work Out of State Flag as a Y or N in position 15 for the 210 (Company Tax), 500 (Payroll Tax Detail) records for Pennsylvania resident tax codes not including Philadelphia. This does not apply to Philadelphia residents, and the position will remain blank for Philadelphia and all other locality taxes.
  • Unchecked:
  • The MasterTax (v47 and above) RTS will NOT include the Work Out of State Flag in the download.
  • The MasterTax (v29 and above) PTS will NOT include the Work Out of State Flag in the download. | Write PTS/RTS Files To Mastertax Directory | > Note: Note: This parameter does not apply to cloud customers. Enable to write generated files to the MasterTax directory on the PrismHR database server. | | Employer Options | Select an Employer ID; the Employer Name displays. Fill in the Employer Options field with alphanumeric values up to 20 characters. | | FUTA Credit Reduction Maintenance | Displays the year in which a FUTA credit reduction was taken and the state where it was taken. FUTA credit reductions occur when a state borrows money from the Federal Unemployment Trust Fund to pay unemployment insurance benefits for its residents. If a state has outstanding loan balances, this lowers the credit rate by 0.3% for each year in arrears until these loans are repaid. | | SBEA Tax Code Reporting Override | If you use Schedule R reporting (the client-by-client report breakdown), you can use this setting to override client-level tax reporting. In this table, specify any tax codes that you want to report on the employer level of the MasterTax RTS and PTS files. |
  1. Click Save.

Other Setup Procedures

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Other_Setup_SBEA.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Other_Setup_SBEA.htm

Other Setup Procedures

This section describes other special considerations to keep in mind during SBEA setup.

========================================================================

Rebuilding SBEA Client Federal Entity Tax Registers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Rebuild-Client-Fed-Entity-Tax-Reg.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Rebuild-Client-Fed-Entity-Tax-Reg.htm

Rebuilding SBEA Client Federal Entity Tax Registers

Use this form to rebuild SBEA Client Federal Entity Tax Registers for a specific tax year. To perform this action, associated employer entities must be certified and must have posted payrolls.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To perform a rebuild:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, click Tax > SBEA Processing > Setup > SBEA Rebuild Client Federal Entity Tax Register.

  2. Enter a tax Year.

  3. Optional: Select an Entity ID to limit rebuilds to one or more clients. If left blank, the rebuild is performed for all clients.

  4. Click Run. The system deletes the register, sorts vouchers, and takes each voucher and runs them through a payroll posting routine that rewrites the tax registers. PrismHR recommends performing the 941 tax register rebuild.

Note: Note: You receive an email notification when the Rebuild Client Federal Entity Tax Register process has completed or failed.

========================================================================

Rebuilding Client Federal Tax Registers

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Rebuild_Client_Federal_Tax_Registers.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Rebuild_Client_Federal_Tax_Registers.htm

Rebuilding Client Federal Tax Registers

If one or more employers in your organization are SBEA-certified and you use MasterTax, you need to have payroll vouchers stamped with the A, B, or C designation. The system has a utility for updating existing vouchers from 2017 and later. When you enable the SBEA feature set, the system stamps all new vouchers appropriately.

Note: Note: Rebuild tax registers during off hours. Do not rebuild tax registers while processing payroll. This process can take a while.

To perform a rebuild:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, click Tax > SBEA Processing > Setup > SBEA Rebuild Client Federal Entity Tax Register.

  2. Enter a tax Year.

  3. Optional: Select an Entity ID to limit rebuilds to one or more clients. If left blank, the rebuild is performed for all clients.

  4. Click Run. The system deletes the register, sorts vouchers, and takes each voucher and runs them through a payroll posting routine that rewrites the tax registers. PrismHR recommends performing the 941 tax register rebuild.

Note: Note: You receive an email notification when the Rebuild Client Federal Entity Tax Register process has completed or failed.

SBEA Processing

Form W-2 Processing for SBEA

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_Chapter_Form_W2_Processing.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_Chapter_Form_W2_Processing.htm

Form W-2 Processing for SBEA

There are some special considerations to keep in mind when processing Forms W-2 in an organization that includes one or more SBEA-certified employers.

The SBEA is unclear as to how certified PEOs must produce their Forms W-2. PrismHR offers certified PEOs the option to break out Forms W-2 by employer and then by client federal entity, and to report under the client federal entity. Currently, you must contact PrismHR and request that we enable this feature.

If your organization is considering this option, contact the PrismHR Customer Support Team.

[Image: Screenshot of the “Contact Customer Support” form, requesting a low-priority support ticket in the Taxes product area, with description: “We are certified as a PEO under the SBEA and would like to learn more about our options for Form W-2 processing.”]

System-Level Setup

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_Chapter_System_Setup.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_Chapter_System_Setup.htm

System-Level Setup

To use the SBEA features in the system, you must first configure a range of settings. Please note that many of these setup procedures are best performed on days where there is no active payroll, because they impact the behavior of payroll processing.

========================================================================

Creating Client Federal Entities

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_Create_Client_Federal_Entities.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_Create_Client_Federal_Entities.htm

Creating Client Federal Entities

After you flag the employers that are SBEA certified, you can use the Create Client Federal Entities feature to create the client federal entity records for all clients of your certified employers.

Note: Note: To accrue taxes properly and report SBEA information, you must create client federal entity IDs and assign them to the clients. You must also have the client federal entity information set up before running payrolls so that the system includes the client federal entity ID on the payroll vouchers.

Federal Entity IDs

After an employer is flagged as a Certified PEO on the Employer Details form, a new Federal Entity ID field displays on the Client Details form for each client serviced by that employer. You must assign a Federal Entity ID to each client.

If you do not assign this ID to each client federal entity (CFE), your payroll will fail with a “hard” error because this causes severe issues later in the SBEA reporting process. The Create Client Federal Entities utility performs this task for each client automatically.

Using the Create Client Federal Entities Utility

To create client federal entities:

  1. Click Work Centers, then go to Tax > SBEA Processing > Setup > SBEA Auto Create Client Federal Entities.

  2. Select an Employer ID (the employer must be a Certified PEO).

  3. Enter a Certified Relationship Start Date, which is the date the contract was signed.

This controls the SBEA wage code. Wage code A will be on any voucher after this start date. If the date is left blank, Wage code C is used.

  1. Click Run. A report displays with the processing steps, number of records created, and the date and time the process ran.

Checking Client Federal Entity Information

Client Details

You can confirm that a client federal entity ID was assigned to a client on the Client Details form:

  1. Open Client Details (found under Client|Change).

  2. In the Contract panel, locate the Federal Entity ID field.

  3. If this field contains a value, the system assigned a CFE ID successfully.

Client Federal Entities

You can view other details about client federal entities on the Client Federal Entities form:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. Under Employer|Change, open Client Federal Entities.

  3. Enter or select an Entity ID. All information about the CFE populates on the form.

  4. Review this information and click Save if you made any changes.

Special Considerations

In some special cases, manual adjustments to the client federal entity records are required:

  • If any of the clients of the employer(s) share an FEIN, you need to manually edit the client records. This is because the system creates separate federal entity records for each of those clients and assigns those different federal entity IDs to the clients. You must edit the clients and set them all to the same client federal entity ID in the Client Details Detail tab.

  • If any of the clients’ worksite locations operate under different FEINs than the client, you need to assign a unique client federal entity ID to those locations in the Worksite Locations Other Parameters panel. If the entity does not exist, you must first create it in Client Federal Entities.

========================================================================

Manually Adding Client Federal Entity IDs

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_in_Client_Details.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_in_Client_Details.htm

Manually Adding Client Federal Entity IDs

For each client that is serviced by a certified PEO, your organization must add the federal entity ID to the client record. Please note that you can only enter this information if the employer assigned to the client is marked as a certified PEO in the employer record.

Note: Note: If your organization has just enabled the SBEA feature set, there is a utility to assign client federal entity IDs to all clients. see Creating Client Federal Entities. In a system with SBEA features enabled, when a user creates a new client and assigns it to a certified PEO employer, the system automatically creates a client federal entity record and assigns it to the new client. See Important Note About Creating New Clients.

To add a federal entity ID to the client:

  1. Open Client|Change > Client Details.

  2. Enter the Federal Entity ID in the Contract panel of the Detail tab.

  3. Click Save.

========================================================================

SBEA Settings in Worksite Locations

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_in_Worksite_Locations.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_in_Worksite_Locations.htm

SBEA Settings in Worksite Locations

There are circumstances where you need to set SBEA information at the worksite level:

  • A single client might have worksite locations with different Federal Employer Identification Numbers (FEINs) than other worksites. In this case, on the Worksite Locations form, you would assign the client federal entity that has the FEIN associated with that worksite.

  • Clients can enter into a certified relationship but have one or more worksite locations that do not pass the “85% test”. In this case, you can use the SBEA Ineligibility Periods panel to enter the dates the locations were ineligible.

This information is important for your quarterly reporting for Schedule R (Form 941 and Form 940).

To set SBEA information at the worksite level:

  1. Open Client|Change > Worksite Locations.

  2. Enter the Location Code.

  3. Expand the Other Parameters panel.

  4. Enter the Federal Entity ID only if the location is a different federal entity than the client.

  5. In the SBEA Ineligibility Periods panel, enter the Starting Date and Ending Date of the ineligibility periods when the location did not pass the 85% test for certification under the SBEA.

  6. Click Save.

========================================================================

Updating Vouchers with SBEA Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_Voucher_Update_Utility.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/SBEA_Voucher_Update_Utility.htm

Updating Vouchers with SBEA Information

The SBEA Voucher Update Utility takes the federal taxes that were previously tracked and accrued at the employer level and associates them with client federal entities. It adjusts previously processed payroll vouchers by flagging them with the client federal entity ID that the employees worked for. It also marks each voucher with the appropriate SBEA wage code.

Note: Note: Run the SBEA Voucher Update Utility during off hours. Do not rebuild tax registers while processing payroll. This process can take a while. Federal taxes must be associated with the client federal entity to ensure correct client tax Schedule R reporting for SBEA purposes.

Your organization needs to use this utility if:

  • You did not complete SBEA setup before your certification start date. The most common example is to accommodate a retroactive certification.

  • You are making adjustments mid-year or backdating start dates.

SBEA Wage Codes

Note: Note: If you are not a CPEO, you do not need to worry about SBEA Wage Codes.

The voucher update utility adds two pieces of information to each voucher: client federal entity ID and SBEA wage code. You created and assigned CFE IDs in the previous step (see Creating Client Federal Entities). The system determines which wage code to post. There are three options:

  • A - The client is in a certified relationship with the employer.

  • B - The client opts to use the 95% rule for a particular worksite exclusion.

  • C - The client is not in a certified relationship with the employer.

Checking Voucher Information

You can confirm that the utility posted the CFE ID and SBEA wage code to each voucher on the Payroll Voucher form.

To check the SBEA information on a specific payroll voucher:

  1. Open the Payroll Voucher form, found under Payroll|View.

  2. Enter or select a Voucher Number. The form populates with information.

  3. Check the values in the Client Fed. Entity and SBEA Wage Code fields.

  4. Repeat for any other vouchers you want to check.

[Image: Employee Voucher form, open to a sample voucher. The Client Fed. Entity and SBEA Wage Code fields are highlighted. In this case the wage code is ‘C’.]

========================================================================

Tax Limit Basis for Client Billing

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Taxable_Limit_Basis_for_Client_Billing.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Taxable_Limit_Basis_for_Client_Billing.htm

Tax Limit Basis for Client Billing

If your organization uses the SBEA feature set, you must set the PEO tax limit basis to be for the Client, rather than the Employer. This setting impacts client billing.

You must also ensure that all clients with a certified PEO employer do not have their tax limit basis override set to Employer.

Setting Up the Tax Limit Basis

To set the system-level tax basis:

  1. Open System Parameters from the Back Office menu .

  2. Select the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] and then select Tax Year Limits.

  3. In the Tax Year Limits Maintenance form, set the PEO Tax Basis for the appropriate reporting year to Client.

  4. Click Accept.

  5. Click Save in System Parameters.

[Image: System Parameters Tax Year Limit panel, with one year highlighted. The PEO Tax Basis dropdown is open for that row, showing Client and Employee options.]

Removing the Client Override

To remove the client-level tax limit basis override:

  1. Open Client|Change|Client Details.

  2. Ensure that you have selected the appropriate client . If not, click the client name that displays above the form and then choose the appropriate client.

  3. Select the Other tab.

  4. Set Tax Limit Basis to –Select–.

  5. Click Save.

  6. Repeat steps 2 through 5 for each client you need to edit.

[Image: Client Details Other tab, with Tax Limit Basis highlighted. The dropdown is open and showing the Company and Employer options.]

========================================================================

Using the SBEA Voucher Update Utility

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/SBEA/Setup/Using%20the%20SBA%20Voucher%20Update%20Utility.htm Path: CMP/SBEA/Setup/Using the SBA Voucher Update Utility.htm

Using the SBEA Voucher Update Utility

Use the SBEA Voucher update utility to retroactively add or update the client federal entity ID on the payroll vouchers. You need to run this utility for several different reasons. For example,

  • When a PEO becomes a certified PEO mid-year and needs to retroactively stamp vouchers.

  • When the entity ID has been edited on the client details or the client’s worksite location record.

This utility provides the capability to restamp the payroll vouchers by client. If the system detects that the client federal entity ID was updated on any vouchers, it displays the following warning message:

To ensure accurate tax calculations for subsequent payrolls, you must rebuild your client federal entity tax register under Tax > SBEA Processing > Setup > SBEA Rebuild Client Federal Entity Tax Register.

To use the SBEA Voucher Update Utility:

  1. On the Work Center panel, click Tax > SBEA Processing > Setup > SBEA Voucher Update Utility.

  2. Fill in the form as shown in the following table.

Use this option — To define this

Employer ID — Enter the Employer ID or click the link to display a list from which you can select an employer. The system displays the employer name.

Start Date — Enter the start date for the report.

End Date — Enter the end date for the report.

  1. Click Build. The system builds the report.

Viewing and Defining Form 1099 Layouts

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/1099%20Processing/1099-Form-Layout.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/1099 Processing/1099-Form-Layout.htm

Viewing and Defining Form 1099 Layouts

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

The 1099 Form Layout form specifies different formats for the printed Form 1099, based on paper size and other factors.

Note: Note:

  • Do not change this form unless instructed to do so by PrismHR Support.

  • Deleting a record causes the specified form type to be unavailable for printing Forms 1099.

To configure form boxes for printing:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, click Tax > 1099 Processing > 1099 Printing > 1099 Form Layout.

  2. Fill in the form as required.

Field — Description

1099 Form Year — Tax year for processing.

1099 Form Code — Pre-printed form types. Options include: - T: Two-up Simplex – One-sided printing with two pages on one sheet. Copy A (1099-MISC with NEC data removed). - TA: Two-up Recipient – Two recipients are printed on one sheet. Copy B (1099-MISC with NEC data removed). - R: Address Formatted – Includes special provisions for addresses that aren’t in a standard format, and puts the address in a different position on a form. - NEC3: Three-up Simplex NEC – Form 1099-NEC, three sets of information on one sheet, one-sided printing. - NECT: Two-up Simplex NEC – Form 1099-NEC, two recipients on one sheet, one-sided printing. - NECP: Two-up Pressure Seal NEC – Form 1099-NEC, two recipients on one sheet, one-sided printing for pressure seal format. > Note: Note: In the backend system, additional options are available for printing three-up Forms 1099-NEC. For details, see the “1099s” chapter of the Year End Guide.

1099 Form Name — Description of this form layout.

1099 Lines Per Page — Number of lines to print per page.

1099 Item Code — Codes that are printed on 1099 forms.

1099 Position — Position of codes on the printed form. Enter data as Line#/Pos/Length; if more than one location is required, enter as much information as needed. For example,1/15/10/1/65/10/1 – use just a / (slash).

1099 Spec Pcl — Enter special PCL (Printer Command Language) codes in this field. This column is only used on the first position list (n/n/n), so if every form needs it, use multiple lines. These codes can send printer-relevant information, such as the font to use.

Form Adjustments

Number of Lines — Select the number of lines from the drop-down. Options available are 1-5 where 3 is the default option. > Note: Note: This option only applies to the form type within the menu. You can have a different options for different Form Types for example Form Type L can have 5 and Form Type S can have 3.

  1. Click Save to save the changes made to the form.

  2. Click Delete to delete the record.

Creating the BDS/SOLV 1099 Download File

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/1099%20Processing/Creating-BDS-IBF-Download.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/1099 Processing/Creating-BDS-IBF-Download.htm

Creating the BDS/SOLV 1099 Download File

Use the SOLV 1099 option to generate a fixed width text file that you can send to SOLV and other third party partners that use the file to print and mail the employee’s 1099 forms. The file is formatted for Billing Document Specialists, or BDS.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group security.

To create the SOLV 1099 download file:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, click Tax > 1099 Processing > 1099 Exports > SOLV 1099. The SOLV 99 page opens.

  2. Complete the form.

Option — Description

Tax Year — Enter the 4-digit tax year. Typically this is the year before the current year.

Employer ID — Select the Employer IDs of the employers for whom to generate the file.

Use Company as Payer — Select No to use the employer as the payer or Yes to use the company as the payer. No is selected by default.

  1. Click Accept.

  2. Click Yes when prompted for confirmation. The system displays a message that shows the status of processing and a notification when processing is complete. Click Close to dismiss the message or Report to open the Job Control Status report. See See Viewing the Job Control Status Report

Creating Employee 1099 Forms

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/1099%20Processing/Creating-EE-1099s.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/1099 Processing/Creating-EE-1099s.htm

Creating Employee 1099 Forms

Use the Create Employee 1099 form to generate the employee’s 1099 register as a background process. It was enhanced to provide a list of tax years for selection. By default, the selection defaults to the previous year. For example, in 2020 the tax year default value is 2019.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group security.

To create employee 1099 form data:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, click Tax > 1099 Processing > 1099 Processes > Create Employee 1099. The Create Employee 1099 page opens.

  2. Complete the form.

Option — Description

Employer ID — Select one or more employer IDs for whom to generate 1099 data. The system supplies the Employer Name.

Select 1099 Tax Year — Select the tax year for which to generate the form data.

Employee ID — To initialize a single employee’s 1099 form, select the Employee ID. The system supplies the Employee Name.

List Name — Enter the name of the file that contains employee IDs that were queried directly from unidata.

  1. Click Accept. The system informs you that previously generated or manually updated 1099 forms for the selected Employer ID and Tax Year will be deleted before running the process.

  2. Click Yes to continue. A progress message shows the status of processing.

  3. When the status is 1099 Forms Successfully initialized, click Close to dismiss the message or Report to view the Job Control Status Report. See See Viewing the Job Control Status Report

Downloading the IRS Form 1099 e-File

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/1099%20Processing/Download-IRS-1099-eFile.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/1099 Processing/Download-IRS-1099-eFile.htm

Downloading the IRS Form 1099 e-File

Use the IRS 1099 E File option to build an export file for 1099 employees. When the export is complete, the file is downloaded as a TXT file.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

To build an export file for 1099 employees:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, click Tax > 1099 Processing > 1099 Exports > IRS 1099 E File.

  2. Fill in the form.

Option — Description

Select 1099 Tax Year — Enter the 4-digit year for which to build the export file. The default selection is the current year minus 1. For example, in 2020 the default year is 2019.

Form Type — Select M for Miscellaneous or R for Retirement or Pension.

Transmitter Record Employer ID — Select the Employer ID of the employer who should appear in the T-Transmitter record.

Transmitter Control Code (TCC) — Enter the 5-character alphanumeric TCC assigned by the IRS.

Employer ID — Select the employer IDs of each employer for which to limit the download. The system provides the employer name. These employers will be shown in the A records.

Text File Indicator — Select this option to submit a test file. Leave blank if you do not want to submit a test file.

Optional State — To limit the download to a specific state, select it from the list.

Employers With Same Fein Are Single Employer? — Select this option to consider multiple employers with the same FEIN as a single employer.

  1. Click Accept. You are prompted for confirmation.

  2. Click Yes to begin the process. A progress box displays the status.

  3. When the progress box shows that the process is complete, click Close to return to the IRS 1099 E File page or Report to open the Job Control Status Report. See See Viewing the Job Control Status Report

========================================================================

Downloading the Maine 1099 e-File

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/1099%20Processing/Download-Maine-1099-eFile.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/1099 Processing/Download-Maine-1099-eFile.htm

Downloading the Maine 1099 e-File

Use the Maine 1099 E File option to build an export file for 1099 employees who reside and work in Maine. When the export is complete, the file is downloaded as a TXT file.

To build an export file for Maine 1099 employees:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, click Tax > 1099 Processing > 1099 Exports > Maine 1099 E File.

  2. Fill in the form.

Option — Description

1099 Tax Year — Select the 4-digit year for which to build the export file. The default selection is the current year minus 1. For example, in 2020 the default year is 2019.

Form Type — Select M for Miscellaneous or R for Retirement or Pension.

Transmitter Record Employer ID — Select the Employer ID of the employer who should appear in the T-Transmitter record.

Employer ID — Select the employer IDs of each employer for which to limit the download. The system provides the employer name. These employers will be shown in the A records.

Text File Indicator — Select this option to submit a test file. Leave blank if you do not want to submit a test file.

  1. Click Accept. You are prompted for confirmation.

  2. Click Yes to begin the process. A progress box displays the status.

  3. When the progress box shows that the process is complete, click Close to return to the Maine 1099 E File page or Report to open the Job Control Status Report. See Viewing the Job Control Status Report

Suppressing Client W-2 Benefits

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/W-2%20Processing/Client-W2-Benefit-Suppression.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/W-2 Processing/Client-W2-Benefit-Suppression.htm

Suppressing Client W-2 Benefits

Use the Client W2 Benefit Suppression form to specify which healthcare premiums are reported for selected clients on the W-2 form using code DD.

To suppress client W-2 benefits for selected clients:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, click Tax > W-2 Processing > W-2 Setup > Parameter Setup > Client W-2 Benefit Suppression.

  2. Complete the form.

Option — Description

Method for limiting — Select Exclude to suppress reporting healthcare premiums on Box 12 for all clients in the list. Select Include to only allow healthcare premiums to appear in Box 12 for the clients in the list. By default, the Create W-2 process includes healthcare premiums to appear on Box 12 for all clients.

Client ID — Select each client for whom you want benefits to be included or excluded.

  1. Click Save. You can clear the selections by clicking Delete.

Creating the EFW2 Download

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/W-2%20Processing/Create-EFW2-Download.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/W-2 Processing/Create-EFW2-Download.htm

Creating the EFW2 Download

Create different types of electronic EFW2s to upload to federal, state, or local government agencies with the EFW2 Download form.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

The Address tab on the Employee Details form populates the State field once you enter the ZIP Code field. However, you can change it manually. In case, the lower case is used for the State field, the EFW2 Download file used to pick up that lower case which resulted in filing failures. The same applies to the use of tilde in the employee’s name, etc.

From now on, to avoid filing failures, the EFW2 and EFW2C Download file will:

Will only accept any of the following; all other characters will be excluded:

    • Uppercase letters (A-Z) (If the state is in proper case, the export will translate it to upper case)
  • Numbers (0-9)
  • Space
  • Hyphen (-)
  • Period (.)

To create the EFW2 download:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu and select Work Centers|Tax > W-2 Processing.

  2. Under EFW2 & W-2 Exports > EFW2 EXPORT, select EFW2 Download.

  3. Select an export file type (SSA (Federal), State, Local). The screen displays different fields depending on the choice made.

  4. Complete these fields as required:

Field — Description

SSA (Federal)

Tax Year — Select a tax year. The Tax Year would be the year you are generating the tax form for. The Tax Year drop-down lists up to the current year and the following tax year.

Submitter — Enter the EFW2 Submitter ID. This can be set to DEFAULT if one submitter is used. For specific submitters, manually enter the ID or select it from the Submitter pop-up.

Edit EFW2 Submitter — Click the link to change the EFW2 submitter. The EFW2 Submitter Codes screen displays. See EFW2 Submitter Maintenance for more information on submitter codes.

Submission Type — Select a submission type. Options include: - Original EFW2C - EFW2C Resubmission using this Resubmission WFID (Wage File Identifier) - The WFID field is disabled unless this option is selected. Enter the ID displayed on the notice sent to you by the SSA.

File Name — The file name default is W2REP0RT.txt, to indicate this is a text file. If you rename the file, use the .txt extension.

Shared FEIN Handling — Handling options for employers with the same FEIN. Options are: - List Employers with the same FEIN under a single Employer - Individually list Employers with the same FEIN

State

Tax Year — Select a tax year. The Tax Year would be the year you are generating the tax form for. The Tax Year drop-down lists up to the current year and the following tax year.

Submitter — Enter the EFW2 Submitter ID. This can be set to DEFAULT if one submitter is used. For specific submitters, manually enter the ID or select it from the Submitter pop-up.

Edit EFW2 Submitter — Click the link to change the EFW2 submitter. The EFW2 Submitter Codes screen displays. See EFW2 Submitter Maintenance for more information on submitter codes.

State — Select the state for this file. Once a state is selected, a Resubmit field appears if a file was previously submitted. Click to resubmit your file to the selected state.

File Name — The file name default is W2REP0RT.txt, to indicate this is a text file. If you rename the file, use the .txt extension.

SSN Range (Indiana Only) — Starting SSN - Starting Social Security Number in a range. Ending SSN - Ending Social Security Number in a range.

Local

Tax Year — Select a tax year. The Tax Year would be the year you are generating the tax form for. The Tax Year drop-down lists up to the current year and the following tax year.

Submitter — Enter the EFW2 Submitter ID. This can be set to DEFAULT if one submitter is used. For specific submitters, manually enter the ID or select it from the Submitter pop-up.

Edit EFW2 Submitter — Click the link to change the EFW2 submitter. The EFW2 Submitter Codes screen displays. See EFW2 Submitter Maintenance for more information on submitter codes.

Local EFW2 Code — Local code for this EFW2. Select a code from the drop-down.

File Name — The file name default is W2REP0RT.txt, to indicate this is a text file. If you rename the file, use the .txt extension.

  1. Click Create Export. The system displays the Building EFW2 Download file for tax year: window.

  2. Click Close to return to the form, or Report to generate a report

  3. Click Download EFW2 to download the exported .txt file.

Creating the EFW2C Re-Submit Download

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/W-2%20Processing/Create-EFW2C-ReSubmit-Download.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/W-2 Processing/Create-EFW2C-ReSubmit-Download.htm

Creating the EFW2C Re-Submit Download

The Social Security Administration (SSA) requires you to provide the previously reported or the corrected Form W-2c information in a specially formatted electronic file. Use this form to submit or resubmit the EFW2C data for your employees to the SSA for the current year or for a previous year.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group Security.

The Address tab on the Employee Details form auto-populates the State field once you enter the ZIP Code field. However, you can change it manually. In case, the lower case is used for the State field, the EFW2 Download file used to pick up that lower case which resulted in filing failures. The same applies to the use of tilde in the employee’s name, etc.

From now on, to avoid filing failures, the EFW2 and EFW2C Download file will:

Will only accept any of the following; all other characters will be excluded:

    • Uppercase letters (A-Z) (If the state is in proper case, the export will translate it to upper case)
  • Numbers (0-9)
  • Space
  • Hyphen (-)
  • Period (.)

To create the EFW2C Re-Submit download:

  1. On the Work Centers panel, under Tax click W-2 Processing. Under EFW2 & W-2 Exports, under EFW2 EXPORTS click EFW2C Download and Maintenance.

  2. Complete these fields as required:

Field Description
Create EFW2C Export
Tax Year Select a tax year. The year selected determines the EFW2 program in use.
Submitter Enter the EFW2 Submitter ID. This can be set to DEFAULT if one submitter is used. For specific submitters, manually enter the ID or select it from the Submitter pop-up.
Edit EFW2 Submitter Click the link to change the EFW2 submitter. The EFW2 Submitter Codes screen displays. See EFW2 Submitter Maintenance for more information on submitter codes.
Submission Type Select a submission type. Options include: - Original EFW2C - This is default option once you arrive on this form. Use this option generate the file when you want to submit an employee’s Form W-2c data for the first time to the Social Security Administration. - Regenerate Processed EFW2C Export - Select this option if you want to regenerate an existing file (the original submission had errors or did not get submitted correctly for some reason) to re-submit an employee’s Form W-2c data to the Social Security Administration. Once you select this option, a drop-down field appears listing all the files having a Status of Regenerate on the Generated EFW2C Files grid below.
File Description This is not a mandatory field. However, you may choose to provide a file name here to identify a particular file better. This file name will appear under the Description field in the grid below for the corresponding system generated Filename. If you name/rename a file, use the .txt extension.
Generated EFW2C Files This section will only appear for Tax Year 2024 and beyond. This grid lists all the batches of EFW2C download files generated for the selected tax year and other parameters selected on this form. Only the first four files will be display in the grid. If there are more, you need to scroll down. This grid has the following columns:
Filename This is a system generated file name for a batch.
Description The file description entered on the Filename field above. It will be blank if nothing was entered.
Status Displays the current status of the file. If you want to change the status of a file, click the status of that file to open the Edit EFW2C File Status form for that file and edit the status. For example, once you have submitted an unsent file to the Social Security Administration, you may want to change the status of that file from Unsent to In Process. A file will have one of the following statuses: - Unsent - Once you click Create Export File to generate a file for a batch for the first time for an original EFW2C submission, it will be listed as Unsent in the grid with a new file name. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you cannot edit information for any employee on this file. - In Process - A file that has been submitted to the Social Security Administration but is yet to be accepted by them. You can enter the Receipt ID received for that file submission on the Edit EFW2C File Status form for that file. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you cannot edit information for any employee on this file. - Accepted - A file that was submitted successfully to the Social Security Administration and accepted by them. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you cannot edit information for any employee on this file. - Rejected - A file that was rejected upon submission by the Social Security Administration. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you can edit information for any employee on this file, and the values will be pre-populated for that employee. - Regenerate - A file that was regenerated prior to submission to the Social Security Administration. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you can edit information for any employee on this file, and the values will be pre-populated for that employee. - Cancelled - A generated file that is not currently under submission the Social Security Administration and cancelled owing to some reason. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you can edit information for any employee on this file. However, you will need to re-enter all the values since all the fields will be blank for that employee.
  • Unsent - Once you click Create Export File to generate a file for a batch for the first time for an original EFW2C submission, it will be listed as Unsent in the grid with a new file name. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you cannot edit information for any employee on this file.
  • In Process - A file that has been submitted to the Social Security Administration but is yet to be accepted by them. You can enter the Receipt ID received for that file submission on the Edit EFW2C File Status form for that file. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you cannot edit information for any employee on this file.
  • Accepted - A file that was submitted successfully to the Social Security Administration and accepted by them. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you cannot edit information for any employee on this file.
  • Rejected - A file that was rejected upon submission by the Social Security Administration. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you can edit information for any employee on this file, and the values will be pre-populated for that employee.
  • Regenerate - A file that was regenerated prior to submission to the Social Security Administration. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you can edit information for any employee on this file, and the values will be pre-populated for that employee.
  • Cancelled - A generated file that is not currently under submission the Social Security Administration and cancelled owing to some reason. Using the Update Employee W-2C Forms form, you can edit information for any employee on this file. However, you will need to re-enter all the values since all the fields will be blank for that employee. | WFID | The unique Wage File Identifier on the notice sent to you by the Social Security Administration for every initial submission. | | Ver | When a EFW2C batch is initially created, this will be version 1. If the file is then cancelled and regenerated, the original version 1 will be replaced with version 2 and so on. | | Create Date | The date when this batch was first created. | | Create Time | The time when this batch was first created. | | Last Modified on | The date when this file was last modified. |

  1. Click Create Export File. The system displays the Create EFW2C Export window.

  2. Click Close to return to the form, or Report to generate a report.

  3. Click Download EFW2C File to download the exported .txt file.

Creating Employee W-2 Forms

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/W-2%20Processing/Create_Employee_W-2.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/W-2 Processing/Create_Employee_W-2.htm

Creating Employee W-2 Forms

Use the Create Employee W-2 Forms utility to create the electronic W-2 form data that needs to be uploaded to federal, state, and local government agencies. If the system detects any errors that will prevent processing, it displays an error message when you select the option. Click OK to dismiss the message and then resolve the errors.

The system uses the Email Services to send notification messages during and after processing.

To create employee W-2 form data:

  1. Open the Work Centers menu and select Work Centers|Tax > W-2 Processing.

  2. Under W-2 Processes, select Create Employee W-2 Forms.

  3. If the system notifies you that it found pre-processing errors, open the report and resolve the errors if necessary.

  4. Complete the form.

Option Description
Build options: Select one of the following options: - Select Exclude manually updated W-2s to exclude manually updated W-2 data from processing. During processing, the system skips W-2s that have the Manually Edited option selected. The record remains as it was before the build. The entire record, including the manual updates, is retained. The employee will be flagged in the error report. - Select All W-2s, including updated W-2s to delete any existing records and create W2s. The manually updated records are not skipped during processing and they revert to their state before they were manually changed.
  • Select Exclude manually updated W-2s to exclude manually updated W-2 data from processing. During processing, the system skips W-2s that have the Manually Edited option selected. The record remains as it was before the build. The entire record, including the manual updates, is retained. The employee will be flagged in the error report.
  • Select All W-2s, including updated W-2s to delete any existing records and create W2s. The manually updated records are not skipped during processing and they revert to their state before they were manually changed. | Tax Year | Select the year for which to generate W-2 data. Typically this is the year before the current year. | | Employer ID | Select one or more employer IDs for whom to generate W-2 data. | | Load All Employers | Click this option to process W-2 data for all employers. | | Employee ID | Select one more specific employees for whom to generate W-2 data. | | List Name | Enter the name of the file that contains employee IDs that were queried directly from UniData. | | Suppress Local If Zero Tax | Select to suppress the reporting of taxable wages for local tax authorities when the tax amount withheld is zero. | | Suppress State If Zero Tax | Select to suppress the reporting of taxable wages for state tax authorities when the tax amount withheld is zero. | | Suppress Third-party Sick Pay | Select to suppress the reporting of all taxable and tax withheld amounts associated with third party sick pay from being added to W-2 forms. This is highly unusual. Most employers are required to report these amounts on W-2 forms. This option is intended to support employers who take advantage of an optional rule for Form W-2 that permits the employer and third party to enter into a legally binding agreement designating the third party to be your agent for preparing W-2 forms reporting sick pay. Be sure that this optional rule applies to you before choosing to suppress third party sick pay. | | Display Employees with No Earnings on Error List | Select to add an employee with no vouchers to the error log. This option is selected by default. | | Zero Out Ohio Resident-Based Taxable Wages | Ohio local taxes are work-based and resident-based. Select this option to zero out the resident-based Ohio taxes. This option is selected by default. | | Include Kentucky School Tx with Local Tax in RTS File | Select to report Kentucky school taxes with local taxes in the MasterTax RTS file. Selecting this option does not change the taxes on the W-2 form itself. | | Use PA Combined Monthly Filer PSD code in Box 20 | Select this option only if you are a service provider in the state of Pennsylvania who is registered as a combined monthly filer, and using an agency such as Keystone to collect and remit Earned Income Tax (EIT). Enter the 2 digit PA Monthly Filer Tax Collection District Code. This will appear in Box 20 of Form W-2 for the Earned Income Tax (EIT) for the state of Pennsylvania. [Image: W-2 locality name box, showing workplace PSD and central payrol codesl, separated by a hyphen] |

  1. Click Create Forms. The system displays a warning any W-2 forms previously generated for the employer will be deleted before running the process.

  2. Click Yes to continue. A progress message shows the status of processing.

  3. When the status is shown as completed, click Close to dismiss the message or Report to view the Job Control Status Report.

Updates to Form W-2 build

The State Income Tax Account Number on the employee Form W-2 will now be pulled from the Client Details form or the Employer Details form based on the tax deduction code, unless an override exists on the Employer Details form on the SIT Rpt Entity field for that state under the State Tax section.

The State Income Tax Account Number on the employee Form W-2 will be:

  • Client State Tax ID when:

  • Tax deduction code set to report at Client Level and ER Details, State is set to -select- or Client

  • Tax deduction code NOT set to report at Client Level, but ER Details, State is set to Client

  • Employer State Tax ID:

  • Tax deduction code NOT set to report at Client Level and ER Details, State is set to -select- or Employer

  • Tax deduction code set to report at Client Level, but ER Details, State is set to Employer

Note: Note: If the State Tax ID is not available on the corresponding reporting entity, the Form W-2 will not be built, and an error will display.

Selecting Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/CMP/Year-End/W-2%20Processing/Data%20Selection.htm Path: CMP/Year-End/W-2 Processing/Data Selection.htm

Selecting Data

Many of the forms used in W-2 processing provide linked data fields. Each linked field provides a table where you can enter data to use. You can either enter the data manually or you can click the link to display a list from which you can make a selection. Your access privileges determine the items that are shown in the list.

This topic uses Employer ID as an example, although there are many other linked fields from which you can select other types of data. Each data item that you select is shown in a separate row in a table. You can insert and delete rows in the following ways:

  • To insert a row at the end of the table, click + next to the Employer ID link.

  • To insert a row at the beginning of the table > next to the selected Employer ID.

  • To delete a row, select it and click x.

To manually select an employer:

  • Select a blank row and enter the Employer ID. The system provides the Employer Name.

To select an employer using a linked field:

  1. Select a blank row and then click the Employer ID link.

  2. In the list of results, select an employer using the following navigational techniques.

Since some lists can be quite long, the system provides various navigation options for you to find the information you want. The options are shown at the bottom of each page in the list. The available options may differ slightly depending on the data that you select.

Option — Description

Select Row — Enter the row number for the employer. It does not need to be visible on the current page. The system populates the field with the selected Employer ID and Employer Name. If an employer is already selected. the new selection replaces it.

Page (n) of (n) — Displays the current page number and the total number of pages. Click Previous or Next to move one page back or forward.

Select Page — To display a different page, enter the page number and click Go to Page.

Refine Search — Displays fields where you can enter specific search information and then click Refine to narrow the results shown in the list. Click Reset to revert to the default display.

To select all employers:

  • Click Load All Employers.

To remove an employer from the table:

  • Click the X in the applicable row.

If there are many rows and you need to clear them all, close the page and then open a new page.
